The Action Refresh also gives you a sneaky bonus when you initiate combat with an attack: because you attacked on a "separate" turn from the First Round of Combat, *you get your Extra Attack from your "previous" turn too*! To put this in clearer English, you'll get one extra shot than usual when you start combat with an attack as an Assassin. Whether this is intended or not, we don't know, but it's consistent and we're not about to *not* use an attack, right?  
If you thought you were doing big damage before, wait til you start dropping enemies with 100 HP in 2 shots. It may be tempting to go back to abusing stealth to get that first round carnage... ***Do not give in.*** Most things will struggle to survive your first round of combat, you will be able to mop up way more consistently now. You must continue to learn how to play the game, as you will be approaching Myrkul at this level and making it past him confidently and consistently separates the Solo'ers from the Camp Casters.  
  
**Lvl 9: Gloom Stalker 5 Assassin 3 War Cleric 1** is also big. We grab War Cleric 1 which gives us Guidance, Thaumaturgy and Resistance, as well as Command, Inflict Wounds, Guiding Bolt and Protection from Evil and Good. We also get to respec and pick a different Favoured Enemy if we have Ranger Knight, as War Cleric gives you your Heavy Armour Proficiency, and we have the option of using Bonus Actions to attack in combat.   
  
Please take care not to waste your Bonus Actions by mistake. You can not only waste it on something that didn’t need to be shot this turn, but it also consumes your Bonus Action. This is no exaggeration: that can get you killed.   
If you use all of your Actions, only have a Bonus Action and have a War Priest charge remaining, using that Bonus Action for anything other than an attack will cause the game to consume your Bonus Action but behave as if your normal Action requires a Bonus Action to use. This is important to know because if you drink a Speed Potion in this situation hoping to get an extra Action/attack, this bug softlocks you into being unable to do anything until your next turn, wasting the potion and the Bonus Action. We will explain this another way for clarity: do not drink a Speed Potion if you only have your War Priest attack left available to you. Yes, we have almost lost a run over this. Learn from our mistakes!   
  
**Lvl 10: Gloom Stalker 5 Assassin 3 War Cleric 1 Fighter 1** is your first Fighter dip. You wanna pick Defence as your fighting style for the +1 AC. You can take Duelling if you want but you shouldn’t really be hitting anything serious with your melee weapon, even with Cloudy G potions.   
  
**Lvl 11: Gloom Stalker 5 Assassin 3 War Cleric 1 Fighter 2** is cool as you get your Action Surge. Same principle as the War Cleric attacks: it’s an extra round you can use to seal the deal on bosses or get yourself out of a bad situation. Also refreshes on a Short Rest which is nice. In an ideal situation you now get: your first attack, your extra attack, your Dread Ambusher attack, Action Surge extra action, Action Surge extra attack, your War Priest attack and, if you popped a Speed Pot/Haste Spores, an extra attack from that action too, for a grand total of 7 attacks in the first round. Only certain bosses will live through that kind of punishment and even fewer will remain a threat beyond the third round, dice willing.  
  
**Lvl 12: Gloom Stalker 5 Assassin 3 War Cleric 1 Fighter 3** gives you choices. You can choose Champion to lower your Crit Chance threshold or Battle Master for Tripping Attack, Evasive Manoeuvres and Menacing Attack. Champion is a little underwhelming unless you stack Crit Chance, as there is something to be said for consistent Crits outside of the first round, but in your first round of combat Champion is functionally useless. For Battle Master you don’t have to choose those exact abilities but Evasive Manoeuvres is Advantage on Saving Throws that doesn't cost an Action, Menacing Attack can Fear the target which makes you harder to hit + they can't move and Tripping Attack can put targets with Legendary Actions on their backs, which means no Legendary Action (Yes, you read that right). It also has a Ranged version, which is sweet. By this point though you'll have been ripping through fights so it's all just extra strategies when you need to get creative.  
  
**[Patch 8]** The next section talks about Arcane Archer all on its own. For those of you that don't know, these sections have a character limit and we really wanted to get into more depth than our current character count would allow, which is why we've left the above section on your Fighter Subclass unchanged. It's all still valid, there's just another option now!  
  
That's pretty much it! There are variations of this build, such as not taking War Cleric and taking 4 Rogue (just for a feat) or 4 Fighter (for a feat and minimal extra HP), but we found that the feat tended to be an ASI and with Assassin & our equipment, it really did feel like overkill. However, there is a build we feel we should talk about... after we get into Patch 8 goodness of course...

## Why War Cleric and not Fighter 2?

A good point was raised in our Discord so we felt it would be prudent to answer it to everyone.  
  
Our guide recommends War Cleric 1 Fighter 1 over Fighter 2 for your lvl 9 & 10 selections. There is good reasoning behind this but in the spirit of giving everyone choices, we will explain a little about why we recommend this approach.  
  
Fighter 2 will give you Action Surge in time for Myrkul; this is two extra attacks in, if you're smart, the first round of combat which will automatically be Critical Hits. Check our Myrkul-specific guide for more info but to summarise: your first shot is against the Mindflayer to start combat, your second is your Extra Attack from that first shot to kill it, throwing Haste Spores is one Action, free Aylin with Scratch so you can do damage, Extra Haste Action is two Attacks and Dread Ambusher is your third. Using War Cleric, you get a fourth shot but using Action Surge, you get a fifth too.  
  
Why wouldn't we advocate for landing five attacks in the first round versus four? This also adds up to a total of 7 guaranteed Crits on Myrkul rather than 6 with Luck of the Far Realms and Killer's Sweetheart in Round 2, so why wouldn't we recommend this?  
  
We will answer our question with two more questions: what happens if we miss and what happens if we have to eat a Gaze of the Dead (GotD) and our Protection from Evil and Good (PfEaG) drops in Round 2 onwards?  
  
The answer is Action Surge does not help us in these situations at all. If you miss a shot in Round 1 you're on the same footing as if you'd picked War Cleric. If you eat a GotD in Round 2 you must decide whether it is worth casting PfEaG again and losing out on an attack or risking continuing without it. Bear in mind, if you have to eat another GotD and you become Feared, your To Hit Chance takes a massive hit.   
  
War Cleric spreads out your additional attacks over the first 3 rounds, allowing you to cover for a miss or eating a GotD and casting PfEaG again without sacrificing the amount of attacks you can land. Yes, you can still miss if you choose War Cleric (and that does suck) but doing the maths, War Cleric is three extra attacks versus Action Surge's two. We choose to go with the higher amount spread out over the first three rounds rather than two in the first, as we cannot stress enough how important PfEaG is to the success of your fight alongside keeping up the DPS on Myrkul before you need to revert to Necromite control. Finger of Death and Myrkul starting to heal is the beginning of the end; if you start getting to Round 5 of combat and Myrkul isn't almost dead, odds are your run is over.   
  
If you want to take Fighter 2 and put all your eggs in one basket, as it is completely possible to 1 or 2-turn Myrkul with that amount of attacks and crits, go for it. If you want to have contingencies in place and love a bit of risk-management, take War Cleric. War Cleric at lvl 9 also offers you a lot more utility than Fighter 1 for taking on Moonrise and Persuading guards you didn't just steal that thing or kill that dude with Guidance.   
  
For those of you asking what if you need to heal and therefore can't use your Bonus Action for a War Cleric shot, this is literally what the Dwarf Meat and Raspberries are for. With the Periapt of Wound Closure, each piece of Dwarf Meat is 16 HP and each Raspberry is 4 HP. One of each is a Greater Healing Potion and if you've saved your Dwarf Meat and been careful with your berries, you should have more than enough to cover your HP losses. Do not be scared to use them all if you need to as this is the last hurdle before the game opens up and you can stack mad amounts of powerful Scrolls and Potions. If you can manage to save some then that's awesome but to reiterate, do not be afraid to use them all if you need to.   
  
As always, we leave the decision of your build up to you.

## [Patch 8 Additions] Gloom Stalker: Builds & Multiclassing

[Patch 8]

***Remember: we haven't tested this enough to guarantee anything, so this is all still theorycrafting.***  
  
**TLDR:   
Slot 1: Seeking Arrow  
Slot 2: Shadow Arrow or Beguiling Arrow   
Slot 3: Grasping Arrow or Enfeebling Arrow**  
  
With the release of Patch 8, we've had a look at the most obvious component that would fit into this build: Arcane Archer.   
  
News is mixed, in our opinion, but we'll continue testing and updating as we go.   
  
Arcana & Nature Proficiency is whatever but you get Guidance, which *might* make the case for Fighter 4/Rogue 4 for a Feat over War Cleric a little stronger, but you still don't get Resistance, Thaumaturgy, the Spells and the War Priest Charges. Light and True Strike are trash though, don't use those.   
  

Arcane Shots: There's Always a Catch

Looking at the Arcane Shots and the Arcane Arrow selection, there's is some good options (on paper). The first snag is fairly obvious though, unfortunately: ***the Spell Save DC for their effects is based on INT.*** To clarify, the Save DC is set by your INT but the target will need to roll different Ability Checks based on what arrow you're using, so it's also not going to capitalise off some enemies having poor INT scores themselves as none of the arrows need an INT save.  
  
Of course, the Warped Headband of Intellect exists, but we're not sure we can advocate that being a mandatory pick when so many better helmets exist (Arcane Acuity, Baldurans, Soul Perception, etc). You'd be sacrificing a lot for some frankly mediocre effects in some cases, and it's still not guaranteed; 17 INT would give you a DC13, which is the same as elemental Special Arrows... which you're probably better of using unless you're exclusively using Seeking Arrow. However, theoretically, we *could* shift some stats around in the later acts to accommodate INT.   
  
We have INT 10 in the Amulet of Greater Health Stat Spread above which is already a decent start and we do have some points in Charisma and Wisdom to play around with to bump that INT up to 12-14, but we're honestly not sure if it's worth it. You *could* drop Wisdom and get your INT to 16, but this will lock you in to the Boots of Persistence or the Ring of Free Action. Not a massive deal but you're also then nerfing your Perception Checks, Insight checks and Ambush Discovery, as well as your Spellcasting Modifier. This will also render the Diadem of Arcane Synergy useless, as 1 point of damage isn't worth your whole Helmet slot. These are fairly minor points as far as we can see but still points nonetheless.   
  
The biggest flaw with this theory is that you won't get this til Act 3 and you'll probably need to rush the House of Hope in order to benefit from it as soon as possible, which means you'll have to either wait for the Craterflesh Gloves or do it after you grab them. Again, not major but not a smooth ride.   
  
The reality of the situation is if you want the INT for the effects you're only gonna have the stats in Act 3 to make them viable, but then again you'll be hitting lvl 11 and getting the subclass in Act 3 anyway so they kind of cancel out. This isn't to say the effects won't work at all if you don't spec for INT, but the Save DC will be so low you won't get them very often.  
  

It's Not All Negative

Having said all that, you are still getting some extra damage out of these Arcane Arrows that you wouldn't be getting with Battle Master. Having played around with these a little in one of our testing saves, Arcane Arrows that we found to be the "best" were Seeking Arrow, Shadow or Beguiling Arrow and Enfeebling Arrow or Grasping Arrow.   
We'll go through every arrow type below and you can decide what you wanna go for.  
  
**Banishing Arrow:** Banishes your target for 2 turns after failing a Charisma save. No extra damage and again, you're gonna need INT to reliably proc it. This is mid at best, really.   
  
**Beguiling Arrow:** 2d6 Psychic and Charms the target after failing a Wisdom save. The Charm might come in handy, especially in a 4-man with a Glamour Bard (although why you'd pick this over Swords Bard is a mystery to us) but the 2d6 is really what you want this for since that's 4d6 in the opening round. Tasty.   
  
**Bursting Arrow:** 2d6 Force in an AoE around the target. The 2d6 *doesn't apply to the target itself.* We'd give this one a miss, honestly, as you'd be better served using an Arrow of Many Targets or a Smokepowder Arrow, which is essentially the same thing as this but better.  
  
**Enfeebling Arrow:** 2d6 Necrotic and Enfeebles the target after failing a Constitution save. This is nice against those pesky melee targets, as Enfeebled means they deal half their weapon damage but again, it's the damage you're after.  
  
**Grasping Arrow:** 2d6 Poison and reduces Movement Speed while causing 2d6 Slashing when the target moves once per turn after failing a Strength save. It's worth noting that the condition that's applied on a failed STR save then turns into Wisdom to remove the condition, or it ends after 10 turns. Basically, this is single target, diet Spike Growth. Also, Poison is resisted often by enemies. We think this is worth a look, but if you chose Enfeebling over this we wouldn't blame you.  
  
**Piercing Arrow:** Fire an arrow that hits all targets in a straight line in front of you dealing an extra 1d6 Piercing Damage (or Force if you're using Ne'er Misser) on a failed Dexterity save; those that save against it still take half damage. This one is a bit of a let-down to be honest, for three reasons:  
The range is awful. Seriously, it's risky trying to get this to hit multiple targets.   
It *doesn't add Sharpshooter, Arcane Synergy or Hunter's Mark as it doesn't have an Attack Roll*.  
It requires INT for the extra damage, so you're more often than not going to see that extra damage being saved. Ability Drain (and Encrusted with Frost if you roll with the Slippy Bow Build) will help you in proc'ing this as it is a Dex save, but don't expect miracles. You're basically nerfing your damage *hard* and putting yourself at risk to use this. We recommend avoiding it for this build.  
  
**Seeking Arrow:** 1d6 but it never misses and applies Faerie Fire on a failed Dexterity save. This is probably what you'll spend the majority of your Arcane Charges on, as when you really need something to die, this arrow has got your back. This is, in our opinion, a mandatory pick for Arcane Archer, and we genuinely believe this alone is worth the subclass. The Faerie Fire proc is a bonus, but realistically we used this to finish targets more often than not so it was kind of redundant.  
  
**Shadow Arrow:** 2d6 Psychic and Blinds the target after a failed Wisdom save. Same schtick as Beguiling Arrow, it's just dependent on what flavour of condition you want to attempt to inflict. Blinding something is nice though, especially for Casters and Ranged users, so if it was us we'd probably pick this over Beguiling Arrow.   
  

Final Thoughts

We'd recommend picking this over Battle Master purely based on the Seeking Arrow if you want extra damage options. Having an extra 2d6 Psychic, Necrotic or Poison on your attacks as a backup is always nice to have although we wouldn't attempt to build around the effects proc'ing unless we specifically spec'ced for INT, which would involve a build overhaul or another build entirely, which we just don't see the point in. We'd say choose Battle Master if you want CC + Evasive Manoeuvres over extra damage, as it is still effective. As always it's up to you.   
  
Keep in mind that this summary is based on the fact you get this subclass with this build at lvl 11/12 depending on your choices, which is where these judgements come from. You'll see a lot more usage from the rest of the arrows in the earlier acts.   
  
There is another build that blows all of this completely out of the water, though, which leads us nicely to...

## Gloom Stalker: Bonus Build - The Not Gloom Stalker

Another way, you say?

So, you've hit lvl 12 and are feeling rather mighty with your War Priest Charges and your Action Surge. But what if we told you, there is another way? An objectively *stronger* way?  
  
Once you hit lvl 12, you can respec into Swords Bard 6/Gloom Stalker 3/Assassin 3 and you will be able to play the same as you have played for the whole game, but now you have Flourishes and Spells to play with. Flourishes like Slashing Flourish, which allows you to shoot twice for the cost of one action and your Defensive Flourish, which gives you +4(!) AC for the rest of the turn. Spells like Hold Person, Glyph of Warding and Hypnotic Pattern. Spells like Misty Step but you can cast it more times, Cantrips like Minor Illusion to group enemies together for a sweet Arrow of Many Targets, as well as very high Charisma which makes speech a breeze now.   
  
Couple this with the Helmet of Arcane Acuity and the Ring of the Mystic Scoundrel (more on this in the equipment section) and you now have a Gloom Stalker that can open with an Acid Arrow, a Slashing Flourish, Dread Ambusher and then cast upcasted Hold Person on everyone *with a 95% success chance*. Pass your turn, then run to the nearest Held target and shoot them point-blank with an Arrow of Many Targets which guarantees critical hits not only on the target *but the rest of the consecutive hits the arrow makes as well*. We're doing *silly* damage here; it only gets sillier when you grab the Bhaalist Armour, which makes targets within 3m vulnerable to Piercing damage... You've spent the game keeping your distance and it's time to get up close, as this equates to roughly 4x damage (Vulnerability doubles your damage and then the Crit rerolls *all the damage dice again*. Yeah. We know. *We know*, and it is as good in practice as it is on paper.   
  

Not Gloom Stalker Stat Spread

  
Str = 8  
Dex = 17(19 w/ Mirror)  
Con = 14  
Int = 8  
Wis = 10  
Cha = 16  
  
We're playing fairly risky with the 10 Wisdom, but the logic is offence is your best defence; target big threats in your initial round of combat. There are also items that can protect you against Hold Person & Paralysis (lookin' at you Dolor) but your Perception has taken a hit. Fortunately, most of the Perception checks in Act 3 aren't mandatory; we still have decent Constitution and we're still using Strength elixirs & stacking Dexterity, so not much has changed.  
  
Why Bard 6? This is when you get your Extra Attack, and lvl 5 means you regain Bardic Inspiration on a Short Rest so you get Flourishes back on a Short Rest. That's basically it.   
  
We call it the "Not Gloom Stalker" because you're now a Bard for the majority of your class levels. This is an option you can take but we ***highly recommend waiting until lvl 12 to switch due to how jarring the playstyle difference is and what you lose vs what you gain from changing over.***  
  

Why didn't you tell me this before now?

Well, because it's not a majority Gloom Stalker build, so it's not technically a Gloom Stalker build. We generally consider a Gloom Stalker build as a Bow build, so we've just figured out the most damaging way to utilise bows; the reality of this is that Gloom Stalker 5 isn't the most damaging way. We'll throw you some examples of when you might want to switch to Swords Bard and then some counterpoints to try and convince you it isn't as fun as it sounds until you hit lvl 12.  
  
Switching to Bard 6 instead of Gloom 5/Rogue 1 will give you your extra attack, sure, and you'll have Flourishes. You've also just lost the ability to wear Heavy Armour, use Shields if you didn't pick Half-Elf, you have lost your +4 to Initiative, your Bonus Action Hide, you no longer have your Dread Ambusher attack, you don't have Longstrider or Enhanced Leap and you now need Charisma if you're casting offensive spells. Yeah, not cool.   
While we are huge advocates of learning to play the game, you should be in the Grymforge around about now and it is *difficult* trying to adjust to the new playstyle while not killing your run. Trust us.   
  
Ok, so lets switch to Bard at lvl 9 and we'll take Swords Bard 6/Gloom Stalker 3, we hear you say. Now we have the best of both worlds, right?  
Yes, true, but you're also missing out on an entire 3 levels in Rogue and your 1 level in War Cleric. So now you have your Flourishes + Gloom advantages (without your lvl 2 Ranger spells) for the price of the entire Assassin subclass and your War Priest charges, as well as your War Cleric bonuses. You should be just about to assault Moonrise Towers at this stage and there is a reason we strongly recommend having Assassin and War Priest charges. That reason is Myrkul. You will be going into Myrkul without the big first round of combat, extra sneak attack damage and 3 less attacks in total. This will add at least 2-3 turns on to your fight, *minimum*. You do have Flourishes, but you can't use Special Arrows with a Flourish, and we kind of need Arrows of Undead Slaying as Myrkul is resistant to both physical and magical Piercing damage. We *need* that double damage to do normal damage. You ***could*** offset this by using a certain Hand Crossbow but you need to consider what you will lose taking this route, not just what you'll gain.   
  
Bard 6/Assassin 3 is an option to keep your damage, but you're losing your Initiative, your Archery Passive, Misty Step and your extra attack. You also lose your War Cleric & Fighter levels. True, you will do way more damage from Flourishes and have Spell utility but you're also gonna need to switch up equipment, Dex is now way more valuable and frankly, this is an "akshually" that we just didn't want to get into.  
  
The only smart time, in our opinion, to switch to Bard 6 is at lvl 12, when you will have all your big advantages from your main 2 classes up until now and equipment that makes this all come together. Yes, you don't have Action Surge anymore or your War Priest charges, but your Slashing Flourishes make up for this by allowing you to make 4 extra shots per Short Rest, which adds up to a grand total of 16 (!!!) per Long Rest, because you also get a free Short Rest with your Song of Rest. Action Surge + War Priest charges will give you 2-5 extra attacks per Short Rest and a grand total of 11 per Long Rest, as War Priest charges only refresh on Long Rest. We said "rest" a *lot* there, we know, but hopefully you can see the maths adds up.   
You will also lose your free +1AC from your Fighting Style and obviously Guidance (unless you use Arcane Archer) + Resistance (Thaumaturgy is covered by your proficiencies. You picked Persuasion right?) but your spells are basically way better now, you *should* have perma-Bless from the Sweet Stone Features and your 'risma will be so high you won't need to worry about Guidance buffs, really. You can experiment with Bard 6 Assassin 3 Fighter 3/Fighter 2 War Cleric 1 but remember our warnings about initiative above, and remember we did the game with the builds we mention so we can verify their effectiveness. Here, we're entering theorycrafting territory so we don't want to go into it more than we already have unless we get enough requests to do so.   
  
As you can see, this is a viable option but you'll only really enjoy it throughout Act 3, so there is absolutely nothing wrong with staying as a traditional Gloom Stalker Assassin for the entire game. You can also start as a Bard while following the instructions above if you're confident you can play without high Initiative and Dread Ambusher. It's a harder version of Gloomstalker until Act 3, if you want the challenge. We present you these paths for consideration only and encourage you to make your own decisions.

## Gloom Stalker: Act 1 Equipment Loadout

Gimme the Good Stuff

We'll go slot by slot, act by act. We'll list the order of items that you want to show your progression and tell you why it's good. If you just want to know what we wore at the end of each milestone/what we consider to be BiS, skip ahead to the "End of Act X" sections.  

Act 1 Helmets

* [Haste Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHaste_Helm)[bg3.wiki] - Momentum for 3 turns at the start of combat. Coupled with extra move speed from Longstrider (+ your racial passive if you picked Wood Elf) you are *rapid* in combat. You find this in the chest next to the fish wagon in the Blighted Village.
* [Shadow of Menzoberranzan](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FShadow_of_Menzoberranzan)[bg3.wiki] - Free invisibility on Short Rest. It's handy but you can keep the Haste Helm if you prefer the movement. Find it by killing the Duergar for Spaw; he'll open the vine room behind the Deep Rothe and it's found in there.
* [Dark Justiciar Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDark_Justiciar_Helm)[bg3.wiki] - +1 to Saving Throws while you're Obscured, which will happen often in the Grymforge. Again, it's decent but either of the other two are just as good as each other, it depends on your situation. You grab this off the Justiciar skeletons in the forge near the Waypoint (check the wiki for better directions).
* [Grymskull Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGrymskull_Helm)[bg3.wiki] - Crit Immunity, Resistance to Fire and another cast of Hunter's Mark. Keep hold of this but if you have the Adamantine Shield or one of the Armours, you don't need to equip it. We mostly use this to retain Crit immunity in the Myrkul fight. You get it for killing Grym in the Grymforge.

Act 1 Cloaks

* [The Deathstalker Mantle](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Deathstalker_Mantle)[bg3.wiki] - This is a controversial cloak for us, as it *is* good but it is relied upon far too heavily. Basically, once per turn, this cloak makes you invisible for 2 turns after a kill.   
  This is a strong ability to have, but in the later stages of the game it becomes very unreliable and cumbersome. The first hurdle is once you have Cull the Weak the invis won't proc if CtW finishes off a target. The second issue you come across is once you have 2 attacks + DA attack, you can usually kill something and have an arrow left over, which you won't want to use as it will break your invis.   
  This cloak is eventually going to drag out combats or put you at more risk than you need to be in while you try and proc the invis when you would be better served learning positioning, planning your fights, your routes to safety and trying to predict how the enemy will move relative to your position. ***You do not need the mantle to complete a solo and in our opinion it will actually be detrimental to your run past lvl 5.*** If you want to keep using it, by all means do so, but do not say we didn't warn you!

Act 1 Chest Pieces

* [Githyanki Half-Plate](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGithyanki_Half_Plate)[bg3.wiki] - 15 AC as base with Disadvantage on Stealth Checks. You can steal this from Lae'zel after the very first Imp fight.
* [Hide Armour +2](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHide_Armour_%252B2)[bg3.wiki] - One less AC than your Half-Plate, but gives you +1 to Dex Saves & Ability Checks, +1 Initiative and of course, removes the Disadvantage on Stealth Checks. It is up to you if you take this over the Half-Plate. This is "purchased" from Grat the Trader... by that we mean if you kill him he'll drop it.
* [Adamantine Splint Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAdamantine_Splint_Armour)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 2. This armour is good, giving you 18 AC, reducing incoming damage by 2 and making you immune to critical hits. It also inflicts the Reeling condition, which is a stacking debuff that gives anything that hits you a -1 to Attack Rolls per stack remaining. You do have Disdvantage on Stealth Checks again though.   
  You can take the Medium armour version, but we go for the Heavy because it is *functionally the same except the Heavy version has more dmg reduction.* Same AC with our Dex, still causes Reeling, still gives you crit immunity. It is up to you but Heavy seems the logical choice. You get this by forging it in the Adamantine Forge (read the wiki for how to do this).

Act 1 Gloves

* [Gloves of Power](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Power)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Sleight of Hand. They're better than nothing, but the Absolute's Bane doesn't proc unless you have the Absolute's Brand and by the time you get that you'll have better gloves. Loot them from Zak'rug after the fight in front of the Grove.
* [Gloves of Missile Snaring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Missile_Snaring) [bg3.wiki]- Use a Reaction to Reduce incoming Ranged Damage by 1d10+ your Dex modifier. Handy gloves to stop snot-nosed little gobbos from causing problems while you deal with their casters or are tied up in melee. Only works once per turn but it's worth it in those early combats. Buy them from Arron in the Grove.
* [Gloves of Archery](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Archery)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 2. +2 dmg to ranged attacks. Every little helps. Buy these from Grat the Trader.

Act 1 Boots

* [Githyanki Boots](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGithyanki_Boots)[bg3.wiki]- +1 Athletics. Better than no shoes. Also steal these from Lae'zel after the very first Imp fight.
* [Swiresy Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSwiresy_Shoes)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Acrobatics and increased Jump distance of 1.5m. Buy them from Grat the Trader.
* [Boots of Speed](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Speed)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 2. Gives you a bootleg version of Cunning Action: Dash. Get these from Thulla in the Myconid Colony. Be nice to her and give her an antidote, she'll give you these boots. You can give them back to Sergeant Thrinn for the quest and kill her to get them back, but we normally keep them.
* [Boots of Stormy Clamour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Stormy_Clamour)[bg3.wiki] - We will outline a build that utilises these but you won't be able to use it properly until Act 2. Causes Reverberation on a target when you hit it with another condition. Buy them from Omeluum after completing his quest.
* [Disintegrating Night Walkers](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDisintegrating_Night_Walkers)[bg3.wiki] - Free Misty Step, you can't slip on Ice or Grease and cannot be Ensnared, Enwebbed or Entangled. They're ok but we prefer Click Heels. You get these from killing Nere.

Act 1 Amulets

* [Silver Pendant](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSilver_Pendant)[bg3.wiki] - Guidance for free. We argue this is BiS until you get to the creche, but be aware it is a Concentration spell which can override your Hunters' Mark. Find this on a plateau outside the Grove, should be on your left as you head west towards the Blighted Village. Check the wiki link for more detailed directions.
* [Broodmothers' Revenge](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBroodmother%2527s_Revenge)[bg3.wiki] - Adds 1d6 Poison dmg after a heal. This is classed as a coating so you can't use it with other Poisons, which kind of kills its overall usefulness. Drow Poison and Malice are really strong, worth way more than 1d6 Poison, especially because Poison is one of the most resisted dmg types in the game. For Act 1 it's ok, but remember to re-equip your Guidance Amulet for conversations. You get this from killing Kagha; it's easier to do so after the Tieflings have left and Halsin has given her a smacked bottom, as she goes to the entrance of the Grove away from anyone who might snitch you up for murder.

## Gloom Stalker: Act 1 Equipment Loadout Cont.

Act 1 Amulets Cont.

* [Amulet of Misty Step](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Misty_Step)[bg3.wiki] - Free Misty Step cast per Short Rest. It's Misty Step, what's not to like? You get this from a chest in Gut's Private Quarters, just don't let Paloma catch you. Or, you know, kill her first. That works too.
* [The Ever-Seeing Eye](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Ever-Seeing_Eye) [bg3.wiki]- Free Protection from Good and Evil per Short Rest. It also stays on when you remove the amulet, so that's nice. Grab this from Ethel's back room. Honestly, this is more prep for Act 2 than something you wanna use now.

Act 1 Rings

* [The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki] - Bless for 2 turns after you heal. This is BiS until Act 3, hands-down. Bless is a flat 10% to your Hit Chance and 1d4 to your saving throws. Couple this with your Safeguard Shield/Absolute's Warboard and you now have minimum +2 to saving throws. Too good. Buy this from Volo in the Grove after you pass the three Druids forming the roadblock.
* [Crusher's Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCrusher%2527s_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - A handy little ring that increases you movement speed by 3m. Until you get the Ring of Protection, this and TWP are your go-tos. You get this after killing Crusher in the Gobbo Camp Exterior.
* [Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki] - Arguably BiS along with The Whispering Promise until the back end of Act 2/Act 3. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws. You get this from stealing the Idol of Silvanus for Mol in the Grove. ***You must make sure to get the quest from Mol BEFORE you rescue Halsin.***
* [Ring of Poison Resistance](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Poison_Resistance)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to being Poisoned and Poison Damage (these aren't the same thing). Situational but it comes in handy in those situations. Auntie Ethel and the Phase Spider Matriarch are both good places to use this. You get it from a Sarcophagus outside of the Worg Pens in the Shattered Sanctum.

Act 1 Ranged Weapons

* [Light Crossbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FLight_Crossbows)[bg3.wiki] - As basic as it gets. You'll find one of these on The Nautiloid.
* [Heavy Crossbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHeavy_Crossbows)[bg3.wiki] - Slightly less basic than the Light version. You can grab a free one from Aradins' area in the Grove (the table and chest under the tent next to Zevlors' room). You'll need to steal it or is a free pickup if the adventurers died/left the Grove.
* [Titanstring Bow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FTitanstring_Bow)[bg3.wiki] - The Precious. This is BiS and you get it after the Grove fight. You can be packing this at lvl 3 and it will last the whole run. We have internal debates about this weapon; one of us adores it and the other wishes there were more bows to play with. There are a few, but this one is the best for raw damage and it genuinely is not even close. You get this for delivering the chest in the Gnoll Cave, unopened, to Zarys in the Zhentarim Hideout behind Waukeens Rest. You don't even need to buy it, just burst Brem and he'll drop it. ***You must have delivered the chest unopened before it appears in his inventory.*** While there are other crossbows/bows in Act 1, we will tell you how to get this ASAP. There is no need to grab any other weapon as your free Heavy Crossbow will last you until you get this.

Act 1 Melee Weapons

* Anything - Use any melee weapon you prefer that is compatible with a shield until you get to Withers' Tomb. You shouldn't be melee-ing anything really but it's handy to have a backup.
* [The Watchers' Guide](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Watcher%2527s_Guide)[bg3.wiki] - a spear that gives you True Strike (Advantage on attack rolls against the targeted creature) when you miss. This is a tidy little backup you can use in a pinch but truthfully, it's only equipped because it's better than a generic unenchanted weapon. You find this in a trapped sarcophagus in Withers' Tomb; make sure to deactivate the traps via the button on the nearby pillar.
* [Ritual Dagger](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRitual_Dagger)[bg3.wiki] - Gives a Bonus Action ability that deals 1d4 Slashing damage to the user in exchange for Bless, or gives the wielder Bless after a successful attack. For Gloom Stalker you won't be using melee except in rare circumstances & should be packing a shield until Act 3, so you really only want this for the Bless ability/action.   
  While this sounds good and is an alternative to The Whispering Promise if you want to experiment with rings, there are some big flaws in this item and the mileage you get from it:  
  The Bless ability takes temp health first, so once you get Shield of Thralls you're cutting into your own shield to use it. We don't recommend this. You *could* try to use Blade Ward from somewhere and the Adamantine Splint Mail passive to reduce the damage down to 0 theoretically but this is a lot of effort when TWP exists.  
  The ability requires a Bonus Action. While this is also true for TWP, that Bonus Action is a heal and you'll need your Bonus Actions for other things too. TWP Bless is also two turns and not one, so you don't have to heal every turn/can proc the heal with Raspberries or Dwarf Meat for free.   
  At the beginning of Act 2 you'll have access to the Defender Flail and The Blood of Lathander which are both objectively better weapons. We would argue this is the hard cut-off point for the Ritual Dagger in solo play. In party play, using this dagger has more utility (especially if you aren't taking a Cleric) but unfortunately, there are better things to be using. You get this from Abdirak (Self-Flagellation dude) in the Goblin Camp Interior or it's on the table next to the Ritual Axe. The axe isn't good, don't use it.
* [Phalar Aluve](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FPhalar_Aluve)[bg3.wiki] - The Notorious. You can activate the Scream and get an extra 1d4 Thunder damage on targets in range but you need to be close enough than we'd like to get this bonus, and by the time you're at the stage where melee isnt as scary there's frankly better stuff to use. Sing doesn't affect the wielder, before you try. You pull this out of a stone near the Selunite Outpost in the Underdark. We don't use this but it's worth mentioning.
* [Club of Hill Giant Strength](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FClub_of_Hill_Giant_Strength)[bg3.wiki] - sets your Strength to 19. It's a stat stick and it's not as good as your Hill Giant Elixir (Hilly G adds +5 to your Titanstring, CoHGS adds +4). You can use this if you feel like using another Elixir (Bloodlust or Vigilance come to mind) but beyond that it's fairly pointless.

Act 1 Shields

* [Safeguard Shield](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSafeguard_Shield)[bg3.wiki] - +1 to Saving Throws & +2 AC. Really really handy, don't sell this. It's always worth keeping around as there are multiple sources of increasing your Saving Throws . Buy this from Dammon in the Emerald Grove.  
  [Absolute's Warboard](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAbsolute%2527s_Warboard)[bg3.wiki] - Safeguard Shield with a free Heroism cast. You need the Absolute's Brand from Priestess Gut to get the full benefits of this shield. It's worth noting that if you cast Heroism then use another effect, the Heroism stays until you lose the Temp Health. Although... bit cheesy, that.
* [Adamantine Shield](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAdamantine_Shield)[bg3.wiki] - +2 AC Crit Immunity, inflicts Reeling on miss (unlike the armour that inflicts it on hit) and you get a Shield Bash ability if something does hit you. This is an amazing shield, but you mainly want it for the crit immunity, as you shouldn't be close enough for melee 90% of the time. If you don't have something giving you crit immunity, wear this, otherwise you should leave the Safeguard Shield on. You get it by forging it in the Adamantine Forge (check the wiki for where you find the mould).

## Gloom Stalker: End of Act 1 Equipment Loadout

At the end of Act 1, before you enter the Mountain Pass, our loadout was:  
  
[Dark Justiciar Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDark_Justiciar_Helm)[bg3.wiki]   
[Gloves of Archery](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Archery)[bg3.wiki]   
[Adamantine Splint Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAdamantine_Splint_Armour)[bg3.wiki]   
[Boots of Speed](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Speed)[bg3.wiki]  
[Silver Pendant](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSilver_Pendant)[bg3.wiki]  
[The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Titanstring Bow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FTitanstring_Bow)[bg3.wiki]  
[The Watchers' Guide](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Watcher%2527s_Guide)[bg3.wiki]  
[Safeguard Shield](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSafeguard_Shield)[bg3.wiki]

## Gloom Stalker: Act 1.5 Equipment Loadout

Continuing our loadout, here are the items you will find in the Mountain Pass/Githyanki Creche, followed by what we were wearing as we headed into Act 2.  

Act 1.5 Helmets

* [Diadem of Arcane Synergy](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDiadem_of_Arcane_Synergy)[bg3.wiki] - BiS for a long time, only really needs switching if you decide to grab Arcane Acuity and stack Hold Person scrolls. This adds your Spellcasting Modifier to your next attack when you inflict a condition. This may not seem like much, as if you have 16 Wisdom that's an extra +3 dmg. Remember when we were talking about crits on vulnerable targets? That's 12 dmg. That's 1.2 Sharpshooters. In this game, those little numbers add up. You get this by killing Ardent Jhe'rezath in the W'wargaz fight.

Act 1.5 Cloaks

* Unless you have the Deathstalker Mantle, there aren't any cloaks here.

Act 1.5 Chest Pieces

* There aren't any chestpieces here that are better than the Adamantine Splint Mail.

Act 1.5 Gloves

* [Gloves of Belligerent Skies](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Belligerent_Skies)[bg3.wiki] - Inflicts Reverberation against a target when you deal Thunder, Lightning or Radiant damage. These can help you in a certain build like the Boots of Stormy Clamour, but you'll need something from Act 2 to make it work. You find these in a chest in Inquisitor W'wargaz' room.
* [Winter's Clutches](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FWinter%2527s_Clutches)[bg3.wiki] - "Why do I want these?" we hear you ask. Well, in Act 2 there's a certain item that will allow you to make a fun build with the Titanstring that's actually highly effective. Pick these up if you want to find out what it is, but it is not mandatory. You buy them from Lady Esther on the Rosymorn Trail.

Act 1.5 Boots

* There are no boots better than the Boots of Speed here.

Act 1.5 Amulets

* [Periapt of Wound Closure](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FPeriapt_of_Wound_Closure)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 3, and even then it's a toss-up. This amulet stabilises you at the start of your next turn, which is useless as the game ends before that can happen, but it also *gives you the maximum amount of HP possible from any source of healing.* Greater Healing Potions are now a flat 20HP. Superiors are 40HP. Your cheeky Healing Potions to proc Bless (you're wearing TWP right?) before every fight are now a flat 10HP, which will keep you topped up easily. You NEED this guarantee in a fight to know where you stand, where you can take risks and where you need to bail. We cannot stress enough how important removing the randomness from healing potions is. You can buy this from Lady Esther.

Act 1.5 Rings

* [Strange Conduit Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FStrange_Conduit_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - Adds 1d4 Psychic damage to your weapon attacks while you're Concentrating. Hunter's Mark is a Concentration spell, as is Protection from Evil and Good, which is about to be 50% of the MVP in Act 2 for you. You can swap out TWP or RoP for this but be aware of what you are sacrificing for it. You get this from a chest in Inquisitor W'Wargaz' room.

Act 1.5 Ranged Weapons

* Titanstring is king. Don't put it down. Ever. Well, not ever, but there's nothing beating it right now.

Act 1.5 Melee Weapons

* [Defender Flail](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDefender_Flail) [bg3.wiki]- Reduces dmg by 1 & gives you +1 AC. This is nice but it's replaced fairly quickly, which is a bit of a shame. This has more utility if you picked Half-Orc though. You can buy this from the Githyanki merchant in the Creche.
* [The Blood of Lathander](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Blood_of_Lathander)[bg3.wiki] - The Ultimate. This is the other 50% of the MVP of Act 2 along with PfEaG. Free Sunbeam cast per Long Rest, casts light in a 9ft radius around you, any Undead entering that 9ft radius need to pass a DC 14 Con check or are Blinded and (you ready?) *once per long rest when you are reduced to 0HP this thing insta-revives you, like the Half-Orc passive.* That's so valuable we don't even need to elaborate. You get this from the secret passageway in Inquisitor W'wargaz' room (check the wiki for exact instructions). ***Be careful with the traps, we have lost a run to them in the past by accident.*** We do not recommend trying to disarm the traps, as the run we lost was in Turn-Based while we tried to disarm it. Now we Enhanced Leap through and use 2 scrolls of Magic Missile to destroy the last barrier.
* [Knife of the Undermountain King](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FKnife_of_the_Undermountain_King)[bg3.wiki] - More like Knife of the Underwhelming King. Now that the Advantage against Obscured Targets has been fixed, this is definitely worth having equipped. Crit reduction is actually useful coupled with the Advantage because the maths says that with 3 attacks in the First Round (we don't have Assassin yet) you have a ~47% chance to Crit. Two attacks is a ~34% chance, which is going to be your Round 2 and onwards chance of landing Crits. Loses a bit of usefulness once you get Assassin, but still, 1/3 chance to Crit after the first round is decent when it's not even what you're using this weapon for. Rerolling 2 dmg or less is nice, no doubt about it, but the Advantage against Obscured Targets is the real MVP here. Start turning off all the light sources you can and Sharpshooter doesn't need to be turned off half as much anymore. In Act 2 though, we'd still recommend The Blood of Lathander as it's just too good to get the Undead off of you if they get close. You can also buy this from the Githyanki merchant in the Creche.

Act 1.5 Shields

* There aren't any shields here that can replace the Safeguard or Adamantine.

## Gloom Stalker: End of Act 1.5 Equipment Loadout

After the Creche, our loadout looked like this:
