## Gloom Stalker: Act 2 Equipment Loadout

Cracking straight into Act 2, you have some serious instant upgrades to look forward to.   

Act 2 Helmets

* [Flawed Helldusk Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFlawed_Helldusk_Helmet)[bg3.wiki] - +1 to Con Saving Throws and +2 to Saving Throws against spells. Nice. You get this from Dammon by giving him a piece of Infernal Iron.
* [Helmet of Arcane Acuity](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelmet_of_Arcane_Acuity)[bg3.wiki] - This thing is godlike, especially if you've been stacking scrolls. Gain Arcane Acuity stacks when you make a successful weapon attack. What this means is when you hit things, your spellsave DC goes up by 1 per stack of the condition. This means every stack increases the likelihood of your spells succeeding by 5%. No, that's not a typo. Until you hit Act 3 your combat strat is now "maul something in the first round of combat and avoid getting hit, then cast CC spell/scroll, mop up the battlefield." You can also drink a Haste Potion (or have Haste pre-cast but have a contingency if you lose Concentration) and pull off CC in the first round; Hypnotic Pattern is a really solid option and pairs beautifully with the Ring of the Mystic Scoundrel which we'll grab in Act 3, and those two items pair exceptionally well with the Not Gloom Stalker build. This isn't mandatory per se but it's handy to have as until now, we weren't spec'ced for offensive magic and now we are. You get this from the secret meeting chamber in the Mason's Guild (check the wiki for better directions).

Act 2 Cloaks

* [Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws. If you've been using the Mantle, now is the time to free yourself from the shackles of mediocrity. You buy this from Quartermaster Talli in Last Light.

Act 2 Chest Pieces

* [Dwarven Splint Mail](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDwarven_Splintmail)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 3. This Heavy Armour has 19 base AC which is one better than your Adamantine, gives you +1 to Strength Saving Throws and Ability Checks, gives you +2 Constitution and reduces all Piercing Damage by 1. That Con boost & the Str saves are unbelievably helpful for Myrkul. You get this by passing an Insight Check while talking to Disciple Z'rell and then telling Lann Tarv that you can see his special stash. If you fail this Insight Check, however, do not despair.
* [Yuan-Ti ScaleMail](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FYuan-Ti_Scale_Mail)[bg3.wiki] - BiS if you can't get the Dwarven Splintmail. Has the same AC with our Dex being 18 by now. Adds your full Dex modifier to your AC instead of maxing it out at +2, gives you +1 to Initiative rolls and has a dope name. This isn't as good as the Dwarven Splintmail bonus-wise, but is its equal in AC which is the saving grace. You can buy this from Quartermaster Talli in Last Light.

Act 2 Gloves

* [Flawed Helldusk Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFlawed_Helldusk_Gloves)[bg3.wiki] - 1d4 Fire damage for weapon attacks, and Unarmed Attacks deal 1d4 Necrotic and can Bleed your target. We don't really care about the Unarmed nonsense, we want that sweet, sweet damage rider. You grab these by giving Dammon a piece of Infernal Iron.
* [Gloves of the Automaton](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_the_Automaton)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. Advantage on your Weapon Attacks & Resistance to Lightning for 10 turns per Long Rest. You'll also be considered a Construct but that's kind of irrelevant. Basically, these things allow you to continue landing shots after your first round of Assassin, and Advantage increases your crit chance indirectly. You want these for certain fights. You buy them from Barcus in Last Light. You *did* let him down from the windmill nicely, right?

Act 2 Boots

* [Evasive Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FEvasive_Shoes)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 3, IF you keep Last Light alive, otherwise BiS. +1 AC & +1 Acrobatics. You want to stack AC and these help you do that. That's pretty much it. Buy them from Mattis in Last Light.

Act 2 Amulets

* Periapt is still BiS. You can switch it up if you want but we don't recommend it.

Act 2 Rings

* [Ring of Free Action](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Free_Action)[bg3.wiki] - Not mandatory per se but worth picking up, especially when you're trying to take down a certain red-armoured ♥♥♥♥♥♥♥ in Act 3. You ignore Difficult Terrain and are immune to being Paralysed or Restrained. No Paralysis or Hold Person for you anymore! You'll probably want to switch this up with your Ring of Protection, as Bless from The Whispering Promise is more valuable. You buy this from Araj Oblodra in Moonrise Towers.
* [Risky Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRisky_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - Advantage on Attack Rolls but Disadvantage on Saving Throws. This ring lives up to its name as it is risky to use. In a 4-man it's a no-brainer, but as a solo? We recommend picking this up but also waiting to use it regularly and only equipping it fully in Act 3, where you can grab equipment that offsets the risk a little. You should also remember that Bless will give you 1d4 to Saving Throws and Advantage is mathematically +5, so consider this when you decide whether to drop The Whispering Promise or the Ring of Protection for it. You also buy this from Araj Oblodra in Moonrise Towers.
* [Killer's Sweetheart](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FKiller%2527s_Sweetheart)[bg3.wiki] - Free Crit after killing something. Useful for keeping the pain train rolling after round one, along with your Luck of the Far Realms. Probably BiS for the Myrkul fight (drop Ring of Protection, The Whispering Promise is needed for Hit Chance and Saving Throws). You get this by killing your doppelganger in the Shar Temple. For 4-man parties you can have characters engage each others doppelgangers, but only after theirs is dead.
* [Snowburst Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSnowburst_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - Grab this if you want to engage in a spicy little build we list down below. It's not mandatory but it's good fun. You get this from under a floorboard in a room downstairs at the back of Last Light. You'll need a Perception Check to discover it. Thank you to Archduke Pebbles for pointing out that we forgot to mention this here!

## Gloom Stalker: Act 2 Equipment Loadout Cont.

Act 2 Ranged Weapons

* [Ne'er Misser](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FNe%2527er_Misser)[bg3.wiki] - Hand Crossbow that does Force Damage and has a free Magic Missile cast. The Magic Missile is handy to clip something that needs to die but remember it takes up a whole action. The real thing here is the Force Damage; it helps to defeat the Shadow-Cursed Shambling Mound easier, and, if you insist on it, to defeat Thisobald Thorm through combat rather than dialogue while bypassing his drinking/spewing mechanic. It also bypasses Myrkuls' Piercing Resistance but if you've already checked out our Solo Myrkul Guide or video, you'll know this isn't necessary. Finally, we *do* switch to it for the Steel Foundry. We don't use this ourselves outside of these scenarios though; travel down to the Gloom Stalker FAQ to read about why. You buy this from Roah Moonglow in Moonrise Towers.

Act 2 Melee Weapons

* [Drakethroat Glaive](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDrakethroat_Glaive)[bg3.wiki] - This isn't for using, it's for enchanting your Titanstring Bow with Thunder Damage (the least resisted damage type). It has an ability that casts Magic Weapon and adds 1d4 elemental damage of your choice to a weapon, so start buffing that Titanstring. Don't forget! Drop your Titanstring, equip the Drakethroat, use the ability on the Titanstring, pick up & re-equip the TItanstring and re-equip your Blood of Lathander & Adamantine Shield. Job done. The buff resets on Long Rest so you have to cast it again each time. It's also worth noting that this *overrides and is overridden by regular Magic Weapon.* This means if you use the Drakethroat ability then cast Magic Weapon, you will not only now be Concentrating on Magic Weapon but you'll lose your extra 1d4 damage.   
  Also, remember those Gloves of Belligerent Skies? Now you can use them. You want to try and stack Reverberation though, so Boots of Stormy Clamour and Ability Drain will make you land 4 Stacks of Reverb per arrow, which starts to really make an impact in combats. You buy this from Roah Moonglow in Moonrise Towers.

Act 2 Shields

* You're probably rocking the Dwarven Splintmail and any other Helmet besides the Grymskull Helm, which means you *really* want to keep using the Adamantine Shield. The crit immunity is so vital to keeping your combat encounters predictable; one crit can put you on the back foot instantly or even kill you outright.   
  If you ARE using the Grymskull helm then believe it or not, you still want to be using that Safeguard Shield from Act 1, as combined with your Ring & Cloak of Protection + Bless, that's +4-7 on your Saving Throws *before you start adding individual stat Saving Throws and Proficiencies*. Any other shield is simply not worth losing that crit immunity/saving throw stack for, or has something that we don't need.

## Gloom Stalker End of Act 2 Equipment Loadout

Before we hit Myrkul we were equipped with:  
  
[Grymskull Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGrymskull_Helm)[bg3.wiki]   
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Dwarven Splint Mail](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDwarven_Splintmail)[bg3.wiki]  
[Gloves of the Automaton](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_the_Automaton)[bg3.wiki]  
[Evasive Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FEvasive_Shoes)[bg3.wiki]  
[Periapt of Wound Closure](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FPeriapt_of_Wound_Closure)[bg3.wiki]   
[The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki]  
[Killer's Sweetheart](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FKiller%2527s_Sweetheart)[bg3.wiki]  
[Titanstring Bow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FTitanstring_Bow)[bg3.wiki]  
[The Blood of Lathander](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Blood_of_Lathander)[bg3.wiki]  
[Safeguard Shield](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSafeguard_Shield)[bg3.wiki]  
  
As we mentioned earlier, you want to have some sort of Crit immmunity. Helmets are probably the least impactful thing to a Gloom Stalker at this stage compared to the Armour & Shield you want, so taking the L on Arcane Acuity or Diadem of Arcane Synergy just makes the most sense. Arcane Acuity won't get silly good until Act 3 *and* you decide to switch to Not Gloom Stalker but the Diadem is nice and putting it down hurts. It's what it is.  
  
This is also the setup we had after Myrkul and moving into Act 3; once you get into Baldurs Gate proper, you're going godlike.

## Intermission: The Slippy Bow Build

What?

Ok, so remember how we told you to grab Winter's Clutches? Well, this is the reason. The build and loadout we have provided you will be doing catastrophic damage to 90% of targets in the game, and you're also fairly tanky. We played this build a *lot* and in our boredom, we tried to figure out a way to make it more interesting and fun, while keeping the combat utility and efficiency.  
  
We found a way to do exactly that. We present to you, The Slippy Bow Build. We will list all of the alternative things you need to make this build work, so forgive us for repeating ourselves a little.  
You will need:   

* [Winter's Clutches](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FWinter%2527s_Clutches)[bg3.wiki] - Inflicts 2 stacks of Encrusted with Frost. EwF gives targets Disdavantage on Dex Saving Throws and 7 stacks will force a DC 12 Con save. If they fail, they take 1d4 Cold Damage and become Frozen. Frozen means the target is Incapacitated and vulnerable to Bludgeoning, Thunder and Force damage. If they take any of those damage types, the condition ends. Fire also removes it instantly. Buy these from Lady Esther on Rosymorn Monastary Trail.
* [Snowburst Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSnowburst_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - Creates a patch of ice surface beneath a target when you deal cold damage to it. You can get it from a hiding spot under the floorboards in a back room at Last Light (check the wiki for more detailed directions).
* [Boots of Stormy Clamour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Stormy_Clamour)[bg3.wiki] - Inflict Reverberation on a target when you inflict a condition. You buy these from Omeluum after completing his quest.
* [Coldbrim Hat](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FColdbrim_Hat)[bg3.wiki] - Inflicts 2 stacks of Encrusted with Frost alongside other spell or cantrip conditions. For our purposes this only works with Winter's Clutches, as the Encrusted with Frost stacks with the Winter's Clutches version and bypasses the spell requirement. This works at the time of writing pre-Patch 8. You get this from a chest hidden behind the bookcase in Balthazar's Room in Moonrise Towers.

  

The Strategy

We hope you can see where this is going. You enchant your Titanstring with Cold damage by using the [Drakethroat Glaive](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDrakethroat_Glaive)[bg3.wiki] and now you're cooking with... frost? Anyway, you're going to throw water on things before starting combat and then start shooting. They will take 2d4 Cold damage, gain 4 stacks of Encrusted with Frost, 2 stacks of Reverberation and now be standing on an Ice Surface which can prone them if they don't jump out of it, *on top of all the damage you normally do*. What's that you say? What happens when you use an Arrow of Many Targets with this build? We won't spoil it for you...   
  
This build is about combining damage with CC while also taking advantage of Wet targets being weak to Frost. The CC makes up for having less AC than the vanilla build and the extra Frost damage makes up for your lost damage riders (notably the Flawed Helldusk and Diadem of Arcane Synergy, if you're using it). It doesn't *quite* match up to the same numbers, but it's enough. Interestingly, the Disadvantage on Dex Throws + Ability Drain *should* make it harder for targets to make the Prone Saving Throw, but extensive Googling and staring at the combat log haven't proved this for us. Remember, the Coldbrim only applies EwF to the first target you hit that turn, so a Many Targets will inflict 4 stacks on the thing you shoot it at and 2 on the subsequent targets.   
  
Applying your chilly death functions best with Arrows of Many Targets, but it's still very effective against single targets, especially once you start getting Disintegrate scrolls. Not Gloom Stalker will also get Thunder Wave for those unlucky enough to get Frozen but you can grab Thunder Wave, Shatter and Magic Missile scrolls in the meantime.  
  
The downside to this build is in Act 2 a lot of undead are resistant to Cold damage. This doesn't stop you from applying your conditions but the build won't quite match the damage numbers you were putting out; it will always be slightly behind as you don't have the extra dmg riders from Diadem of Arcane Synergy & Flawed Helldusk gloves.   
Additionally, we don't recommend this for Myrkul as he is one of the things resistant to Cold damage. You also need to remember you are losing 2AC (Evasive Shoes & Ring of Protection), 1d4 unless things are Wet, Arcane Acuity or Diadem of Arcane Synergy and you must have the Adamantine Shield equipped at all times. Honestly, this is a lot of fun to try out but it feels better suited to party play.  
  
For those of you that want to do 4-man runs, this is the perfect way to compliment your Storm Sorcerer (Frost Sorcerer will want your Frost gear) while still putting serious damage downrange. Add a Fighter to tank & a Cleric to throw water while buffing everyone and you got a yourself a *par-tay*. The cool thing about that is a Storm Sorcerer will probably want your Boots of Stormy Clamour which allows your Gloom Stalker to take the Evasive Shoes and the same amount of Reverb is still going downrange.   
  
With the intermission out of the way, lets move on to Act 3 gear.

## Gloom Stalker: Act 3 Equipment Loadout

Act 3 Helmets

* [Helm of Balduran](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelm_of_Balduran)[bg3.wiki] - BiS, although you might wanna switch up with the Arcane Acuity sometimes for that sweet CC. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws, Crit Immunity, Stun Immunity and heal 2 points per turn. This is just a dope helmet in general and improves your survivability. The 2HP per turn may not seem like much but read on to find a very, very tidy little synergy with a pair of absolutely slept-on gauntlets. Additionally, the Stun Resistance is handy in the Brain fight for characters that need to get up close.
* [Mask of Soul Perception](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FMask_of_Soul_Perception)[bg3.wiki] - +2 to Perception Checks, Initiative and Attack Rolls + a free Detect Thoughts cast. This is a nice helmet that pairs well with Rhapsody (check that out down below) to give you +25% (when Rhapsody is charged) to your Hit Chance, although the Initiative is overkill and the Perception and Detect Thoughts are whatever. The only issue with it is it isn't The Helm of Arcane Acuity or The Helm of Balduran, which are both more valuable in our opinion. Still, give it a go if you feel like it. We'd argue *someone* in your party should be wearing it if you're in a 4-man though.

Act 3 Cloaks

* Cloak of Protection is still BiS. You can try other cloaks but the +1AC & +1 to Saving Throws is too good. If you wear the Armour of Persistence, Balduran's Helm, this and your Ring of Protection, you're sitting on a very respectable 23 AC. While also putting out catastrophic damage. Nice.  
  If you insist though:
* [Cloak of Displacement](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Displacement) [bg3.wiki]- Disadvantage on Attack Rolls against you. Basically Blur that refreshes every turn. You can use this instead of the Cloak of Protection when you know you won't be taking guaranteed damage/damage over time, as the effect is removed when you take damage. You can swap between this and the Cloak of Protection as and when you please, but there's not really anything else you'd want for this slot. You can buy it from Danthelon in Rivington.

Act 3 Chest Pieces

You have a choice to make in Act 3 about what direction you want to take your run. Your choice is similar damage to usual but way more defence, or going full send on offence and taking your damage to another level. The two chest pieces that allow you to do this are listed below. Trust us, these two are all you need.  

* [Armour of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FArmour_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki] - BiS for defence, hands down. 20 AC baseline, reduces all incoming damage by 2 and gives you Resistance and Blade Ward, which give you 1d4 to Saving Throws and Resistance to Bludgeoning, Piercing and Slashing damage, respectively. We'll give you that once more again: *resistance to all physical damage types and 1d4 to Saving Throws*. Yes, really. You can become tanky between this, Baldurans Helm, Boots of Persistence and Amulet of Greater Health, while still putting serious damage downrange. It just depends on how much risk you want to take. You buy these from Dammon at the Forge of the Nine.
* [Bhaalist Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBhaalist_Armour)[bg3.wiki] - BiS for offence and it isn't even close. Anything within 2m of you is now vulnerable to Piercing damage. What type of damage to Arrows do? Yeah...   
  Remember earlier when we were talking about the Not Gloom Stalker, using Hold Person and shooting stuff point-blank? This armour makes this possible because it does 4x damage due to the Vulnerability and when something is held, Melee attacks are a guaranteed crit... and this is applied using the distance to the target, not a differentiation between ranged and melee shots. Basically, at point blank, your arrows are classed as melee attacks and proc the crit. 4x damage. 4. 4! Disgusting levels of damage have now been reached. You buy this from the Echo of Abazigal in the Murder Tribunal. You have to kill the little flying elephant thing and become an Unholy Assassin of Bhaal before you can talk to Abazigal.

Act 3 Gloves

* [Craterflesh Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCraterflesh_Gloves)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. Adds 1d6 Force damage on critical hits, which is actually 2d6 Force damage because... well... you just crit. Between these and your Melee weapons we recommend, you're going to be doing *mad* damage in your first round of combat. It's scary, honestly. You buy these from the Echo of Abazigal in the Murder Tribunal.
* [The Reviving Hands](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Reviving_Hands)[bg3.wiki] - Cast Revivify, +1 to Strength Saving Throws and you cast Blade Ward or Death Ward on healthy or downed opponents, respectively, when you heal them. What you really want these for is to pair them with Balduran's Helm, which will proc Blade Ward for you every turn. This combo allows you to be a little tankier when you're wearing your Bhaalist Armour, however you forsake the Craterflesh Gloves which are really really good. Saying that, giving things Vulnerability to your arrows is more than makes up for it. It's up to you. You buy these from Vicar Humbletoes in the Stormshore Tabernacle. What a name that is, by the way.

Act 3 Boots

* [Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. Freedom of Movement, Longstrider and +1 to Dex saves. Freedom of Movement is the same thing the Ring of Free Action gives you, so now you don't need to worry about being Held, Restrained or Paralysed ever again. Coupled with Balduran's Helm and you can't be Stunned either. Sweet. You buy these from Dammon in the Forge of the Nine.

Act 3 Amulets

* [Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. Gives you Advantage on Constitution Saving Throws and sets your Constitution score to 23. While the Periapt maxes out your heals and not having that will take some getting used to, being able to dump Constitution AND Strength is very very handy. You get this by robbing it from the House of Hope.

Act 3 Rings

* [Band of the Mystic Scoundrel](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBand_of_the_Mystic_Scoundrel)[bg3.wiki] - BiS when you're using the Helmet of Arcane Acuity. This allows you to cast Illusion Spells as a bonus action after hitting something with a weapon attack. So your combat strat will be opening round as Assassin dealing damage and weapon attacks, then use your Bonus Action to cast Hold Person/Hold Monster and mop up the next turn. Arcane Acuity means these spells will be anywhere between 80-95% success chance due to your Spell DC being monumentally high. You get this by stealing the Djinni's ring in the Circus, spinning his wheel, winning the jackpot and talking ♥♥♥♥ to him afterwards. He sends you to the Jungle, where this ring can be found in a backpack next to a skeleton.
* [Ring of Regeneration](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Regeneration)[bg3.wiki] - Heals you for 1d4 per turn in combat. This is kind of a "nothing else to use" situation to stack with Balduran's Helm heals if you don't want to use the Risky Ring or Band of the Mystic Scoundrel. You either buy it from Sorcerous Sundries or get it from killing Rolan if you killed Lorroakan before him. We recommend just buying it as soon as you walk in if you want it as it can disappear from the game if Rolan has it.

## Gloom Stalker: Act 3 Equipment Loadout Cont.

Act 3 Ranged Weapons

* [Gontr Mael](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGontr_Mael)[bg3.wiki] - Possibly cast Guiding Bolt after hitting a target, gives free un-Lethargic-able Haste once per Long Rest and gives Bolt of Celestial Light ability which shoots and arrow that Frightens targets and does an extra 1d4 Radiant Damage for 10 turns afterwards. The ability itself has the Radiant Damage attached to it, which is nice. Oh, and it glows in a 9m radius.  
  Yes, Guiding Bolt procs with AoE arrows and effects. Honestly, we feel this would be the better bow for a pure Hunter Ranger build, especially in a 4-man party, but you can give it a try.   
  Remember: the Radiant Damage and the +2 dmg you get from this bow does not outweigh Titan Weapon and the Guiding Bolt is not the full spell but the effect of the same name that gives Advantage on the target. The Haste IS nice though.
* [The Dead Shot](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Dead_Shot)[bg3.wiki] - Double your Proficiency Bonus when making an Attack Roll unless you have Disadvantage and reduce the amount you need to Crit by 1. This is for those of you that miss Bliss Spores and want that extra To Hit. You might as well stack Crit Reduction now too but remember it is functionally useless with an Assassin build until Turn 2, so in the first round all this bow has going for it is +5 To Hit chance when compared to the Titanstring. We see this as more of a Crit Reduction item for melee users than an actual bow to be fired, really, but it's still worth mentioning.

Act 3 Melee Weapons

* [Dolor Amarus](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDolor_Amarus)[bg3.wiki] - You can grab two of these, and we recommend you do. Adds +7 Piercing damage to critical hits. "Why not [Crimson Mischief](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCrimson_Mischief)[bg3.wiki]?" we hear you cry, which does the same thing and gives you 1d4 Piercing to targets with 50% or less health. Well... because none of that works in Honour Mode. Seriously. Check the wiki page, that's why we highlighted it. You can get one of these from Dolor & buy the other from the Echo of Abazigal in the Murder Tribunal.
* [Rhapsody](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRhapsody)[bg3.wiki] - Gain +1 to Attack Rolls, Damage and Spell Save DC for each enemy killed, to a maximum of +3. You can use Scarlet Feast once you've hit this threshold for 3d10 healing, guaranteed Crit damage on your next attack or give Disadvantage on your next Spell cast. This is a nice dagger, adding +15% to your hit chance, +3 damage and +3 to Spell Save DC once it's fully charged, although the Spell DC doesn't matter too much with Arcane Acuity. If you're concerned about your To Hit chance, this dagger isn't a bad shout. You are missing out on Dolor Damage of course, but you can always dual wield. You get this from killing Cazador in his dungeon.

Act 3 Shields

As we just covered your melee options and recommended you take 2 Dolor Amarus, you've probably realised that this is where we leave shields behind and go for pure damage. If you want to remain safer with a shield, however, your Safeguard is still good. Not as good as this though...   

* [Viconia's Walking Fortress](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FViconia%2527s_Walking_Fortress)[bg3.wiki] - This, paired with the Armour of Persistence, turns you into a literal walking fortress. Disdvantage on spell attacks made against you, Advantage on spell Saving Throws and a neat little ability that reflects damage back to the attacker. Oh, you also have a Shield Bash that does Force damage and can knock the attacker Prone. It's worth noting that your Reflective Shell ability is good for those Mind Flayers in the Brain Fight that ping Magic Missle all the time. You get this from murdering Viconia in the Shar Headquarters. She deserves it, don't worry.

## Gloom Stalker: End of Act 3 Equipment Loadout

We're here. The Endgame. Since we've given you quite a few options, we'll outline the three main equipment loadouts for full-send on offence, defence and somewhere in the middle, and then show you what we used for the Brain fight.   
  

Offensive Equipment Loadout

[Helmet of Arcane Acuity](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelmet_of_Arcane_Acuity)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Bhaalist Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBhaalist_Armour)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]   
[Craterflesh Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCraterflesh_Gloves)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]   
[Band of the Mystic Scoundrel](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBand_of_the_Mystic_Scoundrel)[bg3.wiki]  
[Risky Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRisky_Ring)[bg3.wiki]  
[Titanstring Bow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FTitanstring_Bow)[bg3.wiki]  
[Dolor Amarus](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDolor_Amarus)[bg3.wiki] x2  
  
This really does rip through enemies. You usually want to use this with the Not Gloom Stalker build to take full advantage of upcasted Hold Person rather than relying on scrolls, but you'll want scrolls of Hold Monster (Raphael about to be child's play, *trust us*) and other such nastiness anyway. Your strat will involve Arrows of Many Targets to stack your Arcane Acuity, then casting Hold Person as your Bonus Action and moving into melee range for 4x damage. It's that simple. Videos to come!  
  

Defensive Equipment Loadout

[Helm of Balduran](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelm_of_Balduran)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Armour of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FArmour_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]   
[Craterflesh Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCraterflesh_Gloves)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]   
[Ring of Regeneration](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Regeneration)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Titanstring Bow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FTitanstring_Bow)[bg3.wiki]  
[Dolor Amarus](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDolor_Amarus)[bg3.wiki]  
[Viconia's Walking Fortress](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FViconia%2527s_Walking_Fortress)[bg3.wiki]  
  
This loadout is for those of us that are confident we can play the game properly, as you will be in combat longer than the above loadout, on average. You're sacrificing 7 dmg on your crits, Piercing Vulnerability, Advantage on Attack Rolls and Arcane Acuity but you're gaining more AC, Blade Ward, Resistance, 3-6 health regen per turn, Advantage on spell saving throws and Disadvantage on spell attacks made against you, with a sweet Reflective Shell ability. Remember it's not worth having the Reviving Hands when you have the Armour of Persistence, so we do get the Craterflesh Gloves for that extra dmg on your first round. Your strategy here will be the same as it has been the whole game, but you can afford to take a few more risks.  
  

Balanced Equipment Loadout

[Helm of Balduran](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelm_of_Balduran)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Bhaalist Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBhaalist_Armour)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]   
[The Reviving Hands](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Reviving_Hands)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]   
[Risky Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRisky_Ring)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Titanstring Bow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FTitanstring_Bow)[bg3.wiki]  
[Dolor Amarus](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDolor_Amarus)[bg3.wiki]  
[Viconia's Walking Fortress](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FViconia%2527s_Walking_Fortress)[bg3.wiki]  
  
Here you can see we're leveraging defence with the Shield and the Blade Ward, but also retaining the Bhaalist Piercing Vulnerabilty and the Advantage from the Risky Ring. This is a nice middle-ground as you get the majority of the Offence and the majority of the Defence without leaning too hard in either direction. If you want to swap out the Risky Ring by all means do so as we feel like this would be a more defensive-oriented build if we removed that, which may be a better fit for some players. You'll also want to make extensive use of Crawler Mucus and Karabasan's Poisons, as you won't have those guaranteed Hold Person/Hypnosis procs from Arcane Acuity anymore. Simply start with an Arrow of Many Targets, see who gets Paralysed then use those guys to land your multi-crits. Use the Cloak of Displacement to nullify the Disadvantage from Risky Ring but your AC in turn is about to be worryingly low; we preferred the AC but as always, it's your choice.  
  

Brain Fight Equipment Loadout

[Helm of Balduran](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelm_of_Balduran)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Armour of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FArmour_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]   
[Craterflesh Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCraterflesh_Gloves)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]   
[Ring of Regeneration](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Regeneration)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Titanstring Bow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FTitanstring_Bow)[bg3.wiki]  
[Dolor Amarus](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDolor_Amarus)[bg3.wiki] x2  
  
We leant into more damage without a shield while retaining the defence of the Armour of Persistence for the Brain fight. It's worth mentioning that we swapped between the full-send damage build and this one a lot during Act 3, depending on what boss we were fighting.   
  
Play around with your loadouts once you're at this stage in the game as there are plenty of combats you can try builds out with before heading to a boss. You've made it this far, the rest is easy as long as you remain patient and respect your enemies.

## Monk: Origin

According to the statistics released some time back, Monk is the 3rd least-played class, ahead of Ranger and Cleric. We don't know why, because Open Hand Monk is an absolute *machine*. Here we will enlighten those of you that wish to try some monastic action in your playthroughs. As with the Gloom Stalker, we'll start by going through Origins, Races and Stats, then get into the Build & Equipment Loadouts. Check out [this video to see Myrkul eating our fists](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wQSlXj-skOI) for proof that this build works just as well as we say it does!  
  
Please note some of these entries will be repeated text from up above as we're only human and this amount of writing takes time, so we want to save time where possible. We will also direct you to other sections if the information there will cover something important, as we do have character limits. Anyways, onwards!  
  

The Dark Urge

The Dark Urge gets the Deathstalker Mantle. We don't believe the Mantle is endgame or Best in Slot (BiS), it just makes fights before lvl 5 easier/less risky. We will dive deeper into the Mantle in the equipment section. To be fair to Monk, the amount of damage you eventually start putting out will probably work in your favour as you'll be killing a lot of things outright, so you might get more consistent use out of the Mantle than with other classes.   
Medicine Proficiency is whatever, Intimidation can come in handy.   
Slayer Form is ok but in our opinion should be better, considering what you sacrifice to get it. Saving Last Light isn't as important for Monks, but obviously having more options in Act 3 for equipment is a benefit we'll want to keep.  
Another reason to pick Durge is to have a 1v1 vs Orin in Act 3, making that fight substantially easier. Top tip, Orin's Slayer Form isn't a Wildshape, so when it gets to 0 health she dies; another reason to save Last Light as, if you have the Slayer Form, she doesn't.   
Finally, and this is the most important reason to pick Durge: walking around kicking squirrels and carving up Bards is just good fun.  
  

Astarion

The main reason you take Astarion is his "Happy" condition after a bite, which gives you +1 to Attack Rolls, Saving Throws and some Ability Checks. As a Monk, you'll be in melee range the whole game so you will be able to consistently get the buffs, but remember Bite is a Bonus Action and a lot of your powerful Monk abilities are also Bonus Actions; try to grab the buff out of combat if you can.  
It's worth mentioning that Astarion gets a +1d10 (sweet) Necrotic bonus added to his Weapon and Unarmed Attacks if he Ascends, but you will only get to enjoy this in Act 3. He is also an Elf, so he'll get Perception Proficiency, Darkvision & the Charmed Advantage & Magic Sleep Immunity.  
  

Gale

Gale is Human, but you don't play him for that. He gets a free lvl 3 Spellslot in Act 2 after wrecking the Thorm siblings, gets the Ring of Evasion (use your Reaction to pass a Dex Saving Throw if you fail it) and can get Anointed in Splendour for free if he chooses to kiss and make up with Mystra. Not bad, but genuinely not *that* great either. Open Hand Monks won't be slinging spells, so we're not *really* sure what you'll be using this spellslot for.   
  

Karlach

Playing as Karlach means you can't get the Infernal Robe, so skip her for this one.  
  

Lae'zel

Lae'zel is a Githyanki and comes with all of the bonuses they get. She's also starting out as a Fighter. Realistically, she's the same as Shart: unless you want story flavour, just pick a Tav Githyanki.   
  

Shadowheart

Shadowheart is a High Half-Elf (which, fun fact, is why her Firebolt cantrip sucks: it scales off her INT and not her WIS) so you can have some story flavour if you want to pick Half-Elf, but that is genuinely about it.  
  

Wyll

Wyll gives you access to some nice Warlock gear but other than that, also a Human. Snore. You'd probably be better off picking a Tav and grabbing him as a companion (not in your party, just at camp... and not casting buffs on you) to get the gear, as his Infernal Robe is really useful to us in this build (more on that later).

## Monk: Durge/Tav Race

For all Durge and Tav lovers, we'll list all of the races in the order we feel they suit the playstyle best. For Tav specifically, we recommend choosing Guild Artisan as your background, but it's up to you.  
  

Elf

Your optimum selection is Wood Elf, purely based on the Perception Proficiency and the extra movement distance (1.5m) which will help you close the gap as a melee class, which only gets better once you get your Unarmoured Movement & double Improved Unarmoured Movement passives; these increase your Movement Speed by 3m, 4.5m and 6m respectively at lvls 2, 6 & 10 but for Elf you get 4.5m, 6m and 7.5m. Tidy.   
You also have Stealth Proficiency and Darkvision, as well as Advantage on Charmed & you *can't be magically put to sleep.* Perception Proficiency alongside your Wisdom (run ahead to the Stats section for more on this) means you'll have a good chance of finding all those buttons and detecting those Ambushes. You *could* go High Elf and take Minor Illusion for an easy Grym fight and grouping gribblies up, but we don't believe this is worth it over the Wood Elf bonuses. You'll want high Wisdom as a Monk anyway but Perception is an important check to have good rolls on throughout the majority of the game, so getting +2-+4 on it on top of Wisdom Modifiers means you'll rarely fail one.  
  

Githyanki

Next best would be Githyanki due to their Astral Knowledge, Mage Hand and free Enhanced Leap & Misty Step per Long Rest. The Mage Hand in particular can be used as a source of Wet, Haste Spores or just another target on the field that can throw stuff for you, which is pretty valuable when you only have one character. They don't get that extra movement distance or Proficiencies, though. Not having Darkvision will also suck in the Underdark, Grymforge and Act 2, although this can be offset by the fact you'll be in melee range. Additionally, Gith equipment is dope but you won't be using most of it as it's all Medium Armour or Weapons and the Mask of the Shapeshifter has you covered.  
  

Half-Orc

Half-Orc is a melee race, no doubt about it, but it's Savage Attacks Passive only procs with melee weapon attacks, and we're going to be using Queensberry Rules. It does, however, come with a sweet ability called Relentless Endurance that improves your survivability drastically. When you die as a Half-Orc, once per Long Rest, you are *insta-rezzed with 1 HP.* Yeah, you read that right.  
**Note:** This is more for saving you against deaths outside of combat, as in combat it is likely you simply survive one more hit than you normally would and will die anyway, depending on your turn order. It won't help against chasms or falls either. Still, not getting completely bodied and losing your run to a trap is nice.  
  

Drow

Basically a Wood Elf without the Stealth Proficiency and Movement Speed. They are also objectively cooler. Weapon Proficiencies don't matter as we be brawlin', and a free Faerie Fire (lvl 3) & Darkness (lvl 5) cast can come in handy but it's never going to be game-changing. You also don't have to Shapeshift in Act 1 at all, which is convenient but again, not game-changing.   
  

Half-Elf

Half-Elf is the same as Elf except it doesn't get the Perception Proficiency and instead gets Shield Proficiency. As above, Weapon Proficiencies don't matter as (we're banging this drum a lot here aren't we?) you'll be using Unarmed attacks. We prefer Elf and Drow over Half-Elf due to this and the fact that you won't get Monk passive bonuses while using a Shield, so this trade-off for lack of Perception Proficiency is moot.  
  

Halfling

Halflings come with the Lucky & Brave features, which let you re-roll a 1 at will and give you Advantage on Fear checks, respectively. You can also choose between having Advantage on Stealth Checks or Advantage on Saving Throws against Poison and Resistance to Poison Damage. We'd go with the second option here. The drawback is you move 1.5m less than your long-legged counterparts and, again, don't get relevant Proficiencies but Unarmoured Movement Passives will help negate this.  
  

Dwarf

Dwarven Resilience, which is Advantage against being Poisoned and Resistance to Poison Damage. You only really wanna go Duergar here for the Invis and Advantage against Illusions, Charm and Paralysis. Honestly, you don't need the Invis and the Advantage on Paralysis Saves is probably a waste; if you ever need to use it, you've made a mistake by allowing whatever it is to attempt to Paralyse you in the first place. The Enlarge cast can be nice on Monk as it gives you Advantage on Strength Checks and Saving Throws, but the extra 1d4 damage only applies to weapon attacks. You also have less Movement Speed than the long-legged races but again, your passives can offset this.   
  

Gnome

Advantage on Wisdom, Charisma and Intelligence Saving Throws. The Sub-Race bonuses are all meh here for Monk too, as doubling your History Proficiency Bonus, Speak with Animals or Advantage on Stealth Checks are either pointless or situational. They have nice saving buffs but like Duergar, if you're utilising them it means you've played too risky or made a mistake, and you're also nerfed in movement but for the third time, passives can cover this for you. There's still better options above, truthfully.  
  

Tiefling

Tieflings are cool but all they're really offering consistently is Resistance to Fire. The 3 subraces all have some spells that you *might* use but they either have Charisma or Constitution as their modifiers. Mephistopheles Tieflings would probably be our pick for Mage Hand, closely followed by Asmodeus for the Hellish Rebuke with Zariel coming in last with Thaumaturgy. As for their other spells: Produce Flame sucks, Darkness is fine, Flame Blade & Burning Hands most likely won't be used and the smites need weapons to use.  
  

Human

Shield Proficiency and an extra Proficiency, with extra Carry Weight. Meh. Next.   
  

Dragonborn

Oh, this is what's next? Ouch...  
Objectively the coolest-looking race and also objectively the worst to play as; you're nerfing yourself for zero reason as these guys literally have nothing going for them, not even Darkvision. Don't pick Dragonborn unless you plan on impressing your enemies to death with your sick horns and tail. Let us know how it goes in the comments.   
  
Our runs were done with Wood Elf & Githyanki, but it's up to you. Remember, we've based these selections on what we found to be consistently useful throughout our runs and comparing what you would lose when compared to our optimal picks. You can have a different opinion to us but we believe Wood Elf and Githyanki to be the "best" races for this build, with Half-Orc being worth a look purely for their passive.

## Monk: Stats

Here are the stat spreads you want for your Monk. We have broken them down with optimum switcheroos in mind, but it is completely up to you how much of our advice you take.   
  

Character Creation Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 16  
Con = 14  
Int = 8   
Wis = 16  
Cha = 12   
  
This is your spread at character creation. We don't really recommend changing any of this around for Monks but you do you. You may only have 2 skills that go to 16; later on we will be respec-ing to get 18 Wis due to the Hag's Hair and grabbing an ASI for Dex. That means your Con or Cha will have to take a hit but for now, that 17 is a complete waste of a point that you can utilise elsewhere, for the time being.  
  
Strength 8 is because we're going to dump Strength, as you will be using Hill Giant Elixirs and, when you unlock them, Cloud Giant Elixirs.   
Dex 16 is as high as possible. Stacking Dex is a no-brainer; that’s your Initiative and (some) AC.  
Con 14 gives you respectable health and a decent +2 to Con saves.  
Int 8 will make Arcana checks suck but it contributes nothing to the build and you’ll grab the Warped Headband of Intellect anyway if you *really* want some brainpower.  
Wis 16 is due to the fact that Monks get tasty bonuses that are based on their Wisdom Modifier. It's also nice for +3 to Perception checks and if you take it as a Proficiency, that's +5 from the Nautiloid. Nice.  
Cha 12 is basically what we have left. Not great, not terrible. Don't get attached to it though, as it will be getting dumped soon.   
  

Tavern Brawler Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 16  
Con = 15 + 1 (Tavern Brawler)  
Int = 8   
Wis = 16  
Cha = 10  
  
We'll be taking Tavern Brawler as our first Feat (more on that later) which allows you to add a point into your Con or Str. As we're using Strength Elixirs exclusively, the Str point is... pointless (heh). We're also losing our first +1 from 'risma to get Con to 15 so that Con point isn't wasted either. As mentioned above, only even numbers in a stat will give you a point towards it.   
  

Auntie Ethel Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 16  
Con = 15 +1 (Tavern Brawler)  
Int = 8   
Wis = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
Cha = 8  
  
You'll want to boost that Wisdom to 18; we will eventually grab an ASI to get Dex to 18 too but we won't be enjoying that until lvl 12. As mentioned above, Monks get bonuses that are based off of their Wisdom Modifier and as such, we will want it to be as high as we can get it to reap those benefits. Charisma has to go in order to get us up to 17 Wis to then even it up with Hag's Hair. It is what it is. Using Guidance can help you with Speech Checks but honestly, if things go west and you end up in combats, just Invis potion out or maul everything with your insanely high melee damage.   
  

ASI Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 18  
Con = 15 +1 (Tavern Brawler)  
Int = 10  
Wis = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
Cha = 8  
  
Once you hit lvl 12, you'll want to take an ASI and get your Dex to 18. This is extra AC & Initiative for you in the final boss fights. Every little helps.   
  

Amulet of Greater Health Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 16/18  
Con = 8 +1 (Tavern Brawler)  
Int = 10  
Wis = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
Cha = 14  
  
The Amulet of Greater Health allows you to dump Con & Str, which gives you more room for 'risma or Wisdom. Tavern Brawler's stat bonuses are now moot, but it's a small price to pay to get your 'risma up to 14. Bear in mind that you won't see this spread until Act 3 and by then, in our experience, you're not really going to be needing it for many checks and you'll have managed so far without it, so it's up to you. Int 10 is literally because we have extra points and nowhere else to put them. Dex being 16 or 18 depends on whether you hit your ASI before the House of Hope.   
  
*These next two stat spreads are pretty extra; you can stop here and skip to the build section if you want. For you fellow Turbo-Nerds, read on.*   
  

Araj + Astarions Strength Potion

Str = 10  
Dex = 16  
Con = 15 +1 (Tavern Brawler)  
Int = 8   
Wis = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
Cha = 8  
  
If you make Astarion bite Araj in Act 2, you will get a potion that permanently increases your Strength by +2. "Aren't we using Strength Elixirs?" we hear you cry. Yes, but there is a reason for gaining these extra 2 points and they are not mandatory.  
  

[Mirror of Loss](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FMirror_of_Loss)[bg3.wiki]

Str = 8   
Dex = 16/18  
Con = 15 +1 (Tavern Brawler)  
Int = 8   
Wis = 19 +1 (Hags Hair)  
Cha = 8  
  
We can afford to play with Str now since we are offsetting it with the potion. This will be your endgame stat spread; you won't enjoy these stats until Act 3 but be mindful that you may not get the stat points in Wis (read the wiki page on how the Mirror works.) You can add the Amulet of Greater Health to dump your Con and get your 'risma up to 14, but as we said you will have some leftover points to drop into Int. Which is fine.  
  
It is worth mentioning that this is min/max'ing and you can function on 18 Wis for the entirety of the run with no issues.

## Monk: Builds and Multiclassing

We'll be talking you through our Monk build that we used to complete a run. For those of you that like multiclassing synergies, we have bad news: we did this as a Pure Monk because honestly, it's all you need. If you want to check out some multiclass build recommendations, fly down to the FAQ - Monk section where we talk about what you can take.   
  
For now though, here's what we'll be going for.   

Monk Build Breakdown

We're aiming for Open Hand Monk 12. Unlike Gloom Stalker, this is going to be a fairly straightforward process:  
   
**Lvl 1: Monk** Surprise! Pick Monk as your starting class. Take Athletics and Acrobatics as your Proficiencies as there's not much else to take and if you need to launch things at other things or from the top of cliffs, these will help you do that and defend against it.  
You'll get a resource known as Ki Points which are what you'll spend to use your Flurry of Blows actions. There are ways to regain these through equipment and of course, you'll get more as you level up.  
You also get some Martial Arts passives, which we'll list for you now:  
Martial Arts: Dextrous Attacks allows your attacks with Monk weapons and Unarmed attacks scale with your Dexterity instead of your Strength if your Dexterity is higher. This will help until you get your Hill Giant Elixirs and then it's completely useless, as your Str will always be higher than your Dex.  
  
Martial Arts: Deft Strikes means attacks with Monk Weapons and Unarmed attacks deal 1d4 Bludgeoning damage unless their normal damage is higher. This will increase as your levels do, and it doesn't sound too gnarly until you get Tavern Brawler (more on this in a moment, we're *so close* to explaining it, we promise!).   
  
Martial Arts: Bonus Unarmed Strike allows you to make another attack with a Monk weapon or your fists as a bonus action. This lets you keep up your fisticuffs when your Ki Points run out.   
  
Unarmoured Defence is one of those aforementioned tasty Wisdom bonuses we get. While not wearing armour, you add your Wisdom Modifier to your Armour Class. This is really good, as when we do the maths on this, your AC is going to be 16 in the Nautiloid. To put that into perspective, the best Armour you can grab until reaching the Emerald Grove is Lae'zel's Githyanki Half-Plate which is 17 AC.  
  
Flurry of Blows is a Bonus Action that allows you to punch twice in quick succession. So you're smacking things with your action then giving a 1-2 combo straight afterwards. Our only complaint is we wish they'd added kicks...   
  
**Lvl 2: Monk** gives you another Ki Point and some cool abilities. Unarmoured Movement is mentioned earlier but it's giving you an extra 3m of Movement Speed as long as you're not wearing Armour or have a Shield. We can't stress enough how helpful this is, especially if you're a small race. Patient Defence gives enemies Disadvantage on Attack Rolls against you and gives you Advantage on Dex Saves. It's nice for when you can't get out of Dodge and know you're gonna have to eat a few hits.   
Step of the Wind: Dash & Step of the Wind: Disengage are how you're going to get around the battlefield while you practice your boxing. These actions are the same as Rogue's Cunning Action: Dash and Cunning Action: Disengage, except you can Jump for free after using them. Yeah. You wanna jump in combat? Use SotW: Dash and double your Movement as a freebie.   
  
**Lvl 3: Open Hand Monk** is where you start to see why we're genuinely confused as to why Monk is the 3rd least played class. You get another Ki Point and your base damage from Deft Strikes goes from 1d4 to 1d6. You'll also get Deflect Missiles, which allows you to use your Reaction to reduce Ranged Attack damage against you by 1d10+Dex and, if this reduces the damage to 0, you can use a Ki Point to fling it back at whoever shot it. Return to sender, indeed.   
Open Hand Monk also gives you better Flurry of Blows Attacks. You'll get Stagger which stops enemies from using reactions, Push which sends them back 5m and Topple, which makes them Prone. Topple is the real meat and taters here, as anything with a Legendary Action that you put onto their backs *can't use that Legendary Action* until they get back up. Yeah. As long as this lands, most bosses are about to become trivial.   
  
**Lvl 4: Open Hand Monk** is where you'll hopefully become a convert and also start asking why Monk is the 3rd least played class. You get another Ki Point and Slow Fall, which lets you use a reaction to gain Resistance to Fall Damage. Nice, but also don't rely on this and start dropping off of buildings.  
The real star of the show here is Tavern Brawler, which you'll be taking as your first Feat. This means your character is now online and ready to rip. Tavern Brawler adds your Str modifier twice to both your Attack Roll and Damage Roll. Now you see why we're using Strength Elixirs, because that's +10 to your Hit Chance *and* your damage. Start punching stuff and marvel at how easy the game just became.   
  
**Lvl 5: Open Hand Monk** is where you'll consider signing yourself up to go hang with those Shaolin dudes irl. You get another Ki Point and Extra Attack like other martial classes, but you'll also get Stunning Strike, which does exactly what it says in the description. You can now open up on things with this, Stunning them, and then follow up with a Topple to put them on their backs and guarantee they ain't doing anything useful. It also means you've got 2 chances to prevent bosses from using their Legendary Actions. It just keeps getting better and better.

## Monk: Builds and Multiclassing Cont.

**Lvl 6: Open Hand Monk** is where this class really becomes a powerhouse. If you thought lvl 5 was spicy get some milk, because this just got dialled up to 11. As usual, another Ki Point and your Unarmoured Movement is upped by another 1.5m.  
If you read our Tips section we talk about Diluted Oil of Sharpness. You now have Ki Empowered Strikes, which is basically that on your knuckles, for free, all of the time. Yes, really. Strap in, this isn't even the best bit!  
You now have access to Manifestations, which are toggleable abilities that add a 1d4+Wisdom Modifier's worth of damage to your attacks. These manifestations come in the Psychic (Mind), Necrotic (Body) and Radiant (Soul) flavours and there's no penalty for switching between them, you just can't have more than one active at a time. To do the maths, at this stage you'll have 18 Wisdom and therefore you're doing 4+ 1d4's worth of damage in the Manifestation type you pick. For free.   
But wait, there's more!  
Finally, you have access to Wholeness of Body. This is a Once per Long Rest ability that instantly heals you for 3x your Monk lvl (18 Health from the get go. That's almost a Greater Healing Potion! For free! Madness!) and instantly restores half of your Ki Points rounded down (you're lvl 7 so that means 3 Ki Points). You now have a 3-turn buff that *gives you an extra Bonus Action* (!!) and restores a Ki Point every turn.   
All of this is quite frankly overpowered at lvl 10, nevermind lvl 6. You're now competing for the highest damage output in the game and all you did was level up.   
  
**Lvl 7: Open Hand Monk** gives you yet another Ki Point and Evasion, which means when you save a Dex Spell Saving Throw instead of taking half damage, you take no damage. Pretty handy but don't rely on it.   
Additionally you get Stillness of Mind which sounds good on paper but is a bit of a pain. Whenever you're Feared or Charmed, you spend an Action to rid yourself of the condition. You also don't have a choice with this, as you cannot remove it/turn it off in your Reactions menu. This is like a double-edged sword if the sword also had a blade for a handle: rather than not worrying about Fear or Charm you actually don't want to get hit by it even more, as it will cost you your action. Yes, Bonus Actions are where you have Flurries etc and Speed Potions exist but it's just an inconvenience you don't need nor asked for.  
  
**Lvl 8: Open Hand Monk** is underwhelming compared to everything before it, frankly. Another Ki Point and choose a Feat. We'd recommend Alert for the Initiative bonus.  
  
**Lvl 9: Open Hand Monk** gives you another Ki Point, your base damage goes up from 1d6 to 1d8 and you gain Advanced Unarmoured Movement, which allows you to Jump an extra 6m. The real star here is your Ki Resonation strikes and Ki Resonation Blast abilities. You use the strike to Resonate a target for 10 turns (and it stays on corpses) then you can spend a Ki Point, and *only* a Ki Point, no Actions required, to detonate them for 3d6 Force. This is fairly slept-on in our opinion, as the Resonation Strikes are the same as your normal Action Attack and trigger your Extra Attacks just the same, so there's no downside to using it. We like to hit a few enemies (4 is a nice number), ping a Black Hole then detonate them all together. That's 12d6 on 4 enemies. That's gnarly.   
  
**Lvl 10: Open Hand Monk** has 11 Ki Points total now, you gain another 1.m to your Movement Speed and you're immune to Poison (damage and the condition) and Disease. Cool, we guess.   
  
**Lvl 11: Open Hand Monk** is 12 Ki Points total and you gain Tranquillity, which is free Sanctuary when you wake up after a Long Rest. Not game-changing but it's nice to have that initial protection in your first combat.   
  
**Lvl 12: Open Hand Monk** is the same as lvl 8: underwhelming. Your final Ki Point and a Feat. We recommend an ASI to take your Dex to 18, as this is more AC and another point to your Initiative.  
  
That's pretty much it! The astute among you will notice that Monk peaks at lvl 6 with the bonuses becoming more and more situational as you increase in levels. As always, we believe authenticity is key so this is what we did and we breezed the game, frankly, but if you want more information on how to use multiclassing to get even bigger damage numbers fly down to the FAQ - Monk section.

## Monk: Act 1 Equipment Loadout

Gimme the Good Stuff

We'll go slot by slot, act by act. We'll list the order of items that you want to show your progression and tell you why it's good. If you just want to know what we wore at the end of each milestone/what we consider to be BiS, skip ahead to the "End of Act X" sections.  

Act 1 Helmets

* [Haste Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHaste_Helm)[bg3.wiki] - Momentum for 3 turns at the start of combat. Coupled with your Unarmoured Movement you are *rapid* in combat. We basically used this the whole time until we got the Fistbreaker helm in Act 2. You find this in the chest next to the fish wagon in the Blighted Village.

Act 1 Cloaks

* [The Deathstalker Mantle](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Deathstalker_Mantle)[bg3.wiki] - This is a controversial cloak for us, as it *is* good but it is relied upon far too heavily. Basically, once per turn, this cloak makes you invisible for 2 turns after a kill.   
  This is a strong ability to have, but in the later stages of the game it becomes very unreliable and cumbersome. The first hurdle is once you have Cull the Weak the invis won't proc if CtW finishes off a target. The second issue you come across is once you have Flurries, you're going to be wasting them by not using them to keep your invis. You're far too powerful to be needing the invisibility by lvl 4, honestly.  
  This cloak is eventually going to drag out combats or put you at more risk than you need to be in while you try and proc the invis when you would be better served learning positioning, planning your fights, your routes to safety and trying to predict how the enemy will move relative to your position. ***You do not need the mantle to complete a solo and in our opinion it will actually be detrimental to your run past lvl 5.*** If you want to keep using it, by all means do so, but do not say we didn't warn you!

Act 1 Chest Pieces

* [Infernal Robe](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FInfernal_Robe)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Creche. +1 AC and lets you cast Fire Shield as a Bonus Action once per Long Rest. Fire Shield is whatever, it's the AC we want. You get these for Killing Karlach with Wyll in your party then Long Resting. For those of you that are reluctant to murder her, let us tempt you further: killing her and giving her head to the "Paladins of Tyr" will make the scary one give you his sword and, as long as you don't stop him from reaching you, he'll fight you Unarmed. She's also mid as a character, there we said it. Now there's *three* reasons.

Act 1 Gloves

* [Gloves of Power](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Power)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Sleight of Hand. They're better than nothing, but the Absolute's Bane doesn't proc unless you have the Absolute's Brand and by the time you get that you'll have better gloves. Loot them from Zak'rug after the fight in front of the Grove.
* [Bracers of Defence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBracers_of_Defence)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Myrkul, really. +2AC. When you add your Dex, your Wisdom from being Unarmoured and these bad boys, you're on 18 AC. Couple this with the Infernal Robe, you're on 19 AC. If you bumrush Karlach you can be 19 AC before you're lvl 4, and you're wearing robes. That's awesome. You get these from a chest inside the Cellar in the Blighted Village, where the skeleton coffins are. You don't have to trigger the skeletons to get these.

Act 1 Boots

* [Swiresy Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSwiresy_Shoes)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Acrobatics and increased Jump distance of 1.5m. Buy them from Grat the Trader.
* [Boots of Aid and Comfort](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Aid_and_Comfort)[bg3.wiki] - Our BiS until Act 2. Gives 3 Temporary HP to things you heal, including you. Coupled with The Whispering Promise, you'll be getting this HP a lot. Wearing these means that each potion is guaranteed to be at least 4HP minimum, and a Rest counts as healing so you'll start your next morning after a Long Rest with the Temp HP that you can top up when you're proccing your Bless. You also buy these from Grat the Trader.
* [Disintegrating Night Walkers](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDisintegrating_Night_Walkers)[bg3.wiki] - Free Misty Step, you can't slip on Ice or Grease and cannot be Ensnared, Enwebbed or Entangled. They're ok. You get these from killing Nere.

## Monk: Act 1 Equipment Loadout Cont.

Act 1 Amulets

* [Silver Pendant](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSilver_Pendant)[bg3.wiki] - Guidance for free. We argue this is BiS until you get to the creche, but be aware it is a Concentration spell which can override your Hunters' Mark. Find this on a plateau outside the Grove, should be on your left as you head west towards the Blighted Village. Check the wiki link for more detailed directions.
* [Amulet of Misty Step](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Misty_Step)[bg3.wiki] - Free Misty Step cast per Short Rest. It's Misty Step, what's not to like? You get this from a chest in Gut's Private Quarters, just don't let Paloma catch you. Or, you know, kill her first. That works too.
* [The Ever-Seeing Eye](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Ever-Seeing_Eye) [bg3.wiki]- Free Protection from Good and Evil per Short Rest. It also stays on when you remove the amulet, so that's nice. Grab this from Ethel's back room. Honestly, this is more prep for Act 2 than something you wanna use now.
* [Amulet of the Unworthy](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_the_Unworthy)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to Slashing damage and Vulnerability to Bludgeoning damage. This is slept on as it *sounds* trash but when you can guarantee something isn't going to do Bludgeoning damage (inside Myrkuls arena, for example) it's really nice to have. We grab this for Myrkul later on. You grab it from... I can't believe my eyes... A Minotaur... In the Underdark near the Selunite Outpost.

Act 1 Rings

* [The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki] - Bless for 2 turns after you heal. This is BiS until Act 3, hands-down. Bless is a flat 10% to your Hit Chance and 1d4 to your saving throws. We won't bang on about how good this is more than we already have. Buy this from Volo in the Grove after you pass the three Druids forming the roadblock.
* [Crusher's Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCrusher%2527s_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - A handy little ring that increases you movement speed by 3m. Until you get the Ring of Protection, this and TWP are your go-tos. You get this after killing Crusher in the Gobbo Camp Exterior.
* [Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki] - Arguably BiS along with The Whispering Promise until the back end of Act 2/Act 3. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws. You get this from stealing the Idol of Silvanus for Mol in the Grove. ***You must make sure to get the quest from Mol BEFORE you rescue Halsin.***
* [Ring of Poison Resistance](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Poison_Resistance)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to being Poisoned and Poison Damage (these aren't the same thing). Situational but it comes in handy in those situations. Auntie Ethel and the Phase Spider Matriarch are both good places to use this. You get it from a Sarcophagus outside of the Worg Pens in the Shattered Sanctum.
* [The Sparkswall](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Sparkswall)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to Lightning damage and you can't be Electrocuted. A staple of Lightning Charge builds because of those stupid boots that electrify water, we want this for fighting Bernard and Ansur later on. You get this from a chest in the Basement of the Arcane Tower in the Underdark. Check our tips section to find out how to get here without using Guiding Light.

Act 1 Ranged Weapons

* [Hunting Shortbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHunting_Shortbow)[bg3.wiki] - Advantage on Monstrosities. This is a stat stick and so you can shoot lever and stuff from a distance. Hunter's Mark only works with weapon attacks so don't waste your Bonus Action trying to use it. Or, you know, you can: you do you.
* [Bow of Awareness](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBow_of_Awareness)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Initiative. As above, stat stick and utility tool. Buy it from Roah Moonglow in the Shattered Sanctum.

Act 1 Melee Weapons

* Queensberry Rules, like we said. If you wanna switch it up and have a weapon Monk, be our guest, but you can check out a different guide for that!

Act 1 Shields

* You won't be using a shield because we want Unarmoured bonuses. You can choose to roll with different loadouts but we're not about to list everything here when Gloom Stalker and Paladin will have us covered.

## Monk: End of Act 1 Equipment Loadout

At the end of Act 1, before you enter the Mountain Pass, our loadout was:  
  
[Haste Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHaste_Helm)[bg3.wiki]  
[Bracers of Defence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBracers_of_Defence)[bg3.wiki]  
[Infernal Robe](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FInfernal_Robe)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Aid and Comfort](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Aid_and_Comfort)[bg3.wiki]  
[Silver Pendant](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSilver_Pendant)[bg3.wiki]  
[The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Bow of Awareness](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBow_of_Awareness)[bg3.wiki]

## Monk: Act 1.5 Equipment Loadout

Continuing our loadout, here are the items you will find in the Mountain Pass/Githyanki Creche, followed by what we were wearing as we headed into Act 2.  

Act 1.5 Helmets

* Nothing better than the Haste Helm. Seriously!

Act 1.5 Cloaks

* Unless you have the Deathstalker Mantle, there aren't any cloaks here.

Act 1.5 Chest Pieces

* [The Graceful Cloth](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Graceful_Cloth)[bg3.wiki] - This thing is so awesome for us, we'd argue BiS in the game excluding a few fights. +2 Dex, +1 to Dex Saving Throws, Advantage on Dex Saving Throws, take half Falling damage and Jump 1.5m further. +2 Dex means we don't lose any AC from switching out the Infernal Robe and the Jump distance is always going to be nice. You buy this from Lady Esther on the Rosymorn Trail.

Act 1.5 Gloves

* [Gloves of Cinder and SIzzle](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Cinder_and_Sizzle)[bg3.wiki] - 1d4 Fire Damage onto your Unarmed Attacks and a free Scorching Ray cast. You'll pick these up to use later but for now, keep wearing your Bracers of Defence. You also buy this from Lady Esther.

Act 1.5 Boots

* Amazingly, nothing here better than the Boots of Aid and Comfort.

Act 1.5 Amulets

* [Periapt of Wound Closure](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FPeriapt_of_Wound_Closure)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 3, and even then it's a toss-up. This amulet stabilises you at the start of your next turn, which is useless as the game ends before that can happen, but it also *gives you the maximum amount of HP possible from any source of healing.* Greater Healing Potions are now a flat 20HP. Superiors are 40HP. Your cheeky Healing Potions to proc Bless (you're wearing TWP right?) before every fight are now a flat 10HP, which will keep you topped up easily. You NEED this guarantee in a fight to know where you stand, where you can take risks and where you need to bail. We cannot stress enough how important removing the randomness from healing potions is. Lady Esther is the MVP of equipment here for Monk, as you buy this from her too.

Act 1.5 Rings

* Nope. Got nothin' for you.

Act 1.5 Ranged Weapons

* Other than your stat sticks, there's nothing else here.

Act 1.5 Melee Weapons

* We're not about to change our minds now. Keep punching!

Act 1.5 Shields

* Nothing's changed here either. On to Act 2!

## Monk: End of Act 1.5 Equipment Loadout

Here's what we were rocking after the Gith Creche and moving into Act 2.  
  
[Haste Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHaste_Helm)[bg3.wiki]

[Periapt of Wound Closure](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FPeriapt_of_Wound_Closure)[bg3.wiki]
