## Monk: Act 2 Equipment Loadout

Act 2 has some nice upgrades but truthfully, nothing more major than The Graceful Cloth.   

Act 2 Helmets

* [Fistbreaker Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFistbreaker_Helm) [bg3.wiki] - +1 Spellsave DC and +1 Initiative. You thought we were going to say Kushigo Hood? This does the same thing as the Hood but you don't have to hit something first and gives you +1 Initiative. Your Spellsave DC is whatever as you'll not be casting, but the +1 Initiative is nice. You can keep your Haste Helm if you've grown attached to the Momentum if you want. You buy this from Lann Tarv in Moonrise Towers.

Act 2 Cloaks

* [Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws. If you've been using the Mantle, now is the time to free yourself from the shackles of mediocrity. You buy this from Quartermaster Talli in Last Light.

Act 2 Chest Pieces

* You *just* got The Graceful Cloth and you want more? Unfortunately, there isn't really anything better than that. There's a few things that might be situational in some fights but you'll be using that for the most part if not the whole time until Act 3.

Act 2 Gloves

* You'd think we'd want Flawed Helldusk Gloves but they do Necrotic Damage; most things are Undead here and are therefore Resistant to Necrotic Damage. This is why we picked up the Gloves of Cinder and Sizzle, although we highly recommend using Scrolls of Mirror Image or Blur to offset your lack of AC by removing your Bracers of Defence. It's up to you whether you wanna stack more damage (an extra 1d4 is not to be laughed at when you're landing it 4 times in a turn on average) but if you feel safer with the Bracers then keep them on, as there's probably only about a turn or two's difference in the lengths of combats.

Act 2 Boots

* [Evasive Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FEvasive_Shoes)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 3, probably. +1 AC & +1 Acrobatics. You want to stack AC and these help you do that. That's pretty much it. Buy them from Mattis in Last Light.

Act 2 Amulets

* Periapt is still BiS. You can switch it up if you want but we don't recommend it.

Act 2 Rings

* [Ring of Free Action](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Free_Action)[bg3.wiki] - Not mandatory per se but worth picking up, especially when you're trying to take down a certain red-armoured ♥♥♥♥♥♥♥ in Act 3. You ignore Difficult Terrain and are immune to being Paralysed or Restrained. No Paralysis or Hold Person for you anymore! You'll probably want to switch this up with your Ring of Protection, as Bless from The Whispering Promise is more valuable. You buy this from Araj Oblodra in Moonrise Towers.

Act 2 Ranged Weapons

* [Darkfire Shortbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDarkfire_Shortbow)[bg3.wiki] - Resistant to Cold and Fire Damage & you can cast Haste once per Long Rest. We want this for the Resistances as we feel like Haste is *very* risky unless it comes from Haste Spores, Mind Sanctuary or Gontr Mael. If you go Lethargic without a robust contingency you're flipping a coin on your run and we just do not think it's worth it. You're genuinely better off using Speed Potions tactically at the back end of a combat to finish things off than entering combat with 10-turn Haste and risking losing your Concentration. As always, if you choose not to heed our warning and want to use the Haste, go for it. Don't come crying in the comments if it goes west though, we *did* just warn you! You buy this from Dammon at The Last Light Inn.

Act 2 Melee Weapons

* Still punching things to death, move along.

Act 2 Shields

* We don't need little transportable walls to defend ourselves, we're *quick like a cat* and that's enough. Isn't it?

## Monk: End of Act 2 Equipment Loadout

We'll give you what we walked around in Act 2 & left Act 2 with, but we'll also show you what we walked into the Myrkul fight with. You can see the video [here](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wQSlXj-skOI&t=540s) on how to fight him.  
  
We wrote this guide with authenticity in mind (banging this drum again, bear with us) and at the time of the video, we used the Infernal Robe over the Graceful Cloth... we're hilariously bad at following our own advice but the salient point is the AC bonus we had was the same as if we were using The Graceful Cloth; it's so minor it doesn't really matter.   
  

Act 2 & End of Act 2 Gear

[Fistbreaker Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFistbreaker_Helm) [bg3.wiki]  
[Bracers of Defence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBracers_of_Defence)[bg3.wiki]

Myrkul Gear

[Fistbreaker Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFistbreaker_Helm) [bg3.wiki]  
[Gloves of Cinder and SIzzle](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Cinder_and_Sizzle)[bg3.wiki]  
[The Graceful Cloth](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Graceful_Cloth)[bg3.wiki]  
[Evasive Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FEvasive_Shoes)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of the Unworthy](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_the_Unworthy)[bg3.wiki]  
[The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]

This is covered in our video but we'll reiterate here: Amulet of the Unworthy is Resistance to Slashing damage and there are no sources of Bludgeoning damage in Myrkul's Arena so the Vulnerability is nullified  
The Whispering Promise can be used at the start of combat but *do not try to heal on Myrkul's platform as you are Bone Chilled and cannot heal*. There's genuinely not anything else you can really use in your second Ring slot, if you find anything better then let us know!

## Monk: Act 3 Equipment Loadout

Act 3 is where we'll get some delicious options and we'll list the best ones here for you.  

Act 3 Helmets

* [Helldusk Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelldusk_Helmet)[bg3.wiki] - Anti Crit, Darkvision and immunity to Blind, +2 to Saving Throws against Spells and Immolating Gaze ability, which allows you to Frighten a target and deal 2d8 Fire damage against Burning targets. We want this for the +2 to Spell Saving Throws and the Anti-Crit, as we have not had a source of this until now. There may well be other helmets you can use but once we had Anti Crit, we never took this off. You get it in the House of Hope from the Vault opposite the Boudoir after passing a DC 10 Wisdom & DC 20 Intelligence Check. ***NOTE: If you do not want to fight Raphael and wish to steal these before coming back to kill him, DO NOT GET CAUGHT STEALING OR BLOW YOUR COVER IN ANY WAY***.

Act 3 Cloaks

* [Cloak of Displacement](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Displacement) [bg3.wiki]- Disadvantage on Attack Rolls against you. Basically Blur that refreshes every turn. You can use this instead of the Cloak of Protection when you know you won't be taking guaranteed damage/damage over time, as the effect is removed when you take damage. You can swap between this and the Cloak of Protection as and when you please, but there's not really anything else you'd want for this slot. You can buy it from Danthelon in Rivington.

Act 3 Chest Pieces

* [Vest of Soul Rejuvenation](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FVest_of_Soul_Rejuvenation)[bg3.wiki] - +2AC (tidy), when you save against a Spell you gain 1d4 HP and you can use a Reaction when an opponent misses you to make an Unarmed Attack. The Unarmed retaliation is nice, but the 2AC and healing is what we want this for. You buy this from either Lorroakan's Projection or Rolan, depending on if Rolan is alive or not.

Act 3 Gloves

* [Gloves of Soul Catching](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Soul_Catching)[bg3.wiki] - +2 Constitution, 1d10 Force Damage added to your Unarmed Attacks (oof that's nice) and after you hit something with your fists, you can either heal for a flat 10HP or gain Advantage on your Attack Rolls *and* Saving Throws for the rest of the turn. This is a little buggy so check the wiki to see what's up, but +2 Con and 1d10 Force is still gnarly. To get these gloves you *must* save Hope in the House of Hope. We recommend keeping her away from things that can kill her or (we swore not to gatekeep so here you go) use a Globe of Invulnerability to make sure she survives.

Act 3 Boots

* [Boots of Uninhibited Kushigo](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Uninhibited_Kushigo)[bg3.wiki] - Add Damage equal to your Wisdom Modifier to your Unarmed attacks. This is another reason why we want high Wisdom as it's just adding to the pile of destruction you're collecting. With 5 Wisdom this is +5 damage which doesn't feel too spicy, but remember you're averaging on 4 attacks per turn baseline. That's 20 damage before you've even calculated anything else. We *told* you Monk is awesome. You get these from Prelate Lir'i'c, one of the Githyanki Monks defending Orpheus, just before you enter Act 3. While the acquisition of these boots is a bit of a weird one, as it's between acts, we put them in the Act 3 section as you won't enjoy any of Act 2 with them, which makes sense to us.

Act 3 Amulets

* [Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki] - Sets your Constitution score to 23 and gives Advantage on Constitution Saving Throws. This allows you to respec and get yourself some more Charisma, but obviously having high Con is beneficial for the health you'll gain alone. We don't recommend any other amulets other than this one but be aware you'll need to respec as not to waste points in Con and respec again if you take it off... don't forget this and end up having to complete the House of Grief with 38HP like we did... You also get this from the House of Hope. Earlier warning about blowing your cover applies here too.

Act 3 Rings

* There are a few situational rings you can use, like the Ring of Regeneration but Ring of Free Action + Ring of Protection are your most reliable and consistent rings. Remember to grab the statue from the Mephit in the Circus for those Sweet Stone Features!

Act 3 Ranged Weapons

* [Gontr Mael](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGontr_Mael)[bg3.wiki] - Fly back up to the Gloom Stalker Act 3 Equipment Cont. section for the full lowdown on this but all you need to know is un-Lethergic-able Haste for 5 turns per Long Rest. Invaluable in the Brain Fight, frankly. You get this from killing the Steel Watcher Titan in the Foundry. Flashblinder grenades, anyone?
* [Hellrider Longbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHellrider_Longbow)[bg3.wiki] - +3 Initiative and Advantage on Perception Checks. Nice to have; we switched between this and Gontr depending on whether we needed to guarantee going first or not. You buy this from Ferg Drogher in Rivington. For you 4-man parties following this guide, you can't have Shadowheart in the party if you want to buy this.

Act 3 Melee Weapons

* If you think we've got melee weapons for you at this point in the game, you haven't been paying attention!

Act 3 Shields

* Lots of cool Shields but none we want to use, unfortunately.

## Monk: End of Act 3 Equipment Loadout

This is our loadout for finally ending the reign of the Absolute:  
  
[Helldusk Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelldusk_Helmet)[bg3.wiki]   
[Gloves of Soul Catching](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Soul_Catching)[bg3.wiki]  
[Vest of Soul Rejuvenation](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FVest_of_Soul_Rejuvenation)[bg3.wiki]   
[Boots of Uninhibited Kushigo](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Uninhibited_Kushigo)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]  
[The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Gontr Mael](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGontr_Mael)[bg3.wiki]  
  
That's it! With this equipment, your already overpowered Monk build will have so many neat little features that we'd be surprised if anyone struggled with the majority of bosses left for the game to throw at you. Unlike Gloomstalker, there's pretty much an objective best loadout and this is it, in our opinion. This doesn't mean you shouldn't experiment but also, we've lost runs in Act 3 and it's painful, so at your own risk.   
  
 On to Paladin!

## Paladin: Origin

Paladin. Big Smites. Enough said.   
  
Please note some of these entries will be repeated text from up above as we're only human and this amount of writing takes time, so we want to save time where possible. We will also direct you to other sections if the information there will cover something important, as we do have character limits. Anyways, onwards!  
  

The Dark Urge

The Dark Urge gets the Deathstalker Mantle. We don't believe the Mantle is endgame or Best in Slot (BiS), it just makes fights before lvl 5 easier/less risky. We will dive deeper into the Mantle in the equipment section. To be fair to Paladin, the amount of damage you eventually start putting out will probably work in your favour as you'll be killing a lot of things outright, so you might get more consistent use out of the Mantle than with other classes.   
Medicine Proficiency is whatever, Intimidation can come in handy.   
Slayer Form is ok but in our opinion should be better, considering what you sacrifice to get it. Saving Last Light is almost mandatory as Dammon sells some of the best gear in the game, but there are options in case the Isobel fight doesn't go your way.  
Another reason to pick Durge is to have a 1v1 vs Orin in Act 3, making that fight substantially easier. Top tip, Orin's Slayer Form isn't a Wildshape, so when it gets to 0 health she dies; another reason to save Last Light as, if you have the Slayer Form, she doesn't.   
Finally, and this is the most important reason to pick Durge: walking around kicking squirrels and carving up Bards is just good fun.  
  

Astarion

The main reason you take Astarion is his "Happy" condition after a bite, which gives you +1 to Attack Rolls, Saving Throws and some Ability Checks. As a Paladin, you'll be in melee range the whole game so you will be able to consistently get the buffs. As Bite is a Bonus Action, you won't have to worry overmuch about using it in combat or outside, just remember your Great Weapon Master attack (more on that later).  
It's worth mentioning that Astarion gets a +1d10 (sweet) Necrotic bonus added to his Weapon and Unarmed Attacks if he Ascends, but you will only get to enjoy this in Act 3. He is also an Elf, so he'll get Perception Proficiency, Darkvision & the Charmed Advantage & Magic Sleep Immunity.  
  

Gale

Gale is Human, but you don't play him for that. He gets a free lvl 3 Spellslot in Act 2 after wrecking the Thorm siblings, gets the Ring of Evasion (use your Reaction to pass a Dex Saving Throw if you fail it) and can get Anointed in Splendour for free if he chooses to kiss and make up with Mystra. Not bad, but genuinely not *that* great either. He's a decent choice here as you'll get another lvl 3 Smite for free in Act 2, which is always gonna help, but the tradeoff is you have to actually play as Gale.  
  

Karlach

Karlach would, at first glance, be a good pick for a Paladin, but her Soul Coins only really suit her when she stays as a Barbarian. We do NOT want to walk around at under 25% HP for the sake of 1d4 Fire Damage. She is a Zariel Tiefling though, so she'll get Smites of her own and Thaumaturgy to play with. If you've read everything before now, however, you'll know that Branding and Searing Smites use both your Action & Bonus Action which is not efficient, especially when you'll want that action for other things.   
***Note:*** It has come to our attention that Karlach's 1d4 Fire Damage is bugged and doesn't require you to be at <25% HP to get, so you do you. Still, playing as Karlach? No thanks.]  
  

Lae'zel

Lae'zel is a Githyanki and comes with all of the bonuses they get. She's also starting out as a Fighter. Realistically, she's the same as Shart: unless you want story flavour, just pick a Tav Githyanki.   
  

Shadowheart

Shadowheart is a High Half-Elf (which, fun fact, is why her Firebolt cantrip sucks: it scales off her INT and not her WIS) so you can have some story flavour if you want to pick Half-Elf, but that is genuinely about it.  
  

Wyll

Wyll gives you access to some nice Warlock gear but other than that, also a Human. Snore. You'd probably be better off picking a Tav and grabbing him as a companion (not in your party, just at camp... and not casting buffs on you) to get the gear but realistically you're not gonna use the robe or the Infernal Rapier later on, as you're gonna wanna be Two-Handing for those big damage numbers. Still, its stuff to sell.

## Paladin: Durge/Tav Race

For all Durge and Tav lovers, we'll list all of the races in the order we feel they suit the playstyle best. For Tav specifically, we recommend choosing Guild Artisan as your background, but it's up to you.  
  

Elf

With the addition of Patch 8, you can take Booming Blade when you choose High Elf, which is going to make your initial first 4 levels way less difficult. Booming Blade is a melee cantrip that adds 1d8 Thunder Damage when the target moves; this means you can batter something with it in melee and then push them so when they move back into range, you proc that damage. This gets better with more levels, as at lvl 5 you do an *additional* 1d8 on the cast and the target takes 2d8 when it moves, and this goes up to 2d8 inital and 3d8 movement damage when you hit lvl 11, *on top of your normal weapon damage and then you can proc your Smites*. Very tasty. This is essentially extra damage like the Half-Orc except you don't need a Crit to gain the benefits, therefore you will gain more consistent damage. Don't choose Wood Elf here, Booming Blade is too good.  
  

Half-Elf

Half-Elf is the same as Elf except it doesn't get the Perception Proficiency and instead gets Shield Proficiency. As above, Weapon Proficiencies don't matter as you'll always have a class that covers them for you in your build. High Half-Elf gets Booming Blade too, so all the stuff above about it applies here as well. Don't go Wood Elf here either.  
  

Half-Orc

Half-Orc is a melee race as it comes with the Savage Attacks Passive (not to be confused with Savage Attack*er*) which means you roll an extra Damage Dice on Crits. In English, say a normal roll is 1d6. Crit means 2d6. Half-Orc Crit means 3d6. Very tidy. Note we said "normal" damage dice. In Honour Mode, this *does not include smites or sneak attacks*. We feel like High Elf is now the top contender purely because of Booming Blade and its effectiveness, especially in those early levels. If you spec for Crits, you'll probably get more value out of the Half-Orc passive on the whole but you'll only really be able to spec into Crits heavily in Act 3 and honestly at this stage, you've passed the threshold for "this thing is about to get wrecked" you're just trying to get the "how wrecked is it" numbers higher.  
It also comes with a sweet ability called Relentless Endurance that improves your survivability drastically. When you die as a Half-Orc, once per Long Rest, you are *insta-rezzed with 1 HP.* Yeah, you read that right. We recommend playing like you don't have a safety net though, as this drastically increases your odds of never having to use it.   
**Note:** This is more for saving you against deaths outside of combat, as in combat it is likely you simply survive one more hit than you normally would and will die anyway, depending on your turn order. It won't help against chasms or falls either. Still, not getting completely bodied and losing your run to a trap is nice.  
  

Githyanki

Githyanki get Astral Knowledge, Mage Hand and free Enhanced Leap & Misty Step per Long Rest. The Mage Hand in particular can be used as a source of Wet, Haste Spores or just another target on the field that can throw stuff for you, which is pretty valuable when you only have one character. They don't get that extra movement distance or Proficiencies, though.   
Additionally, while there is a lot of equipment that have special effects that only proc when you're a Gith, you *can use the [Mask of the Shapeshifter](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FMask_of_the_Shapeshifter)[bg3.wiki] or a Disguise Spell/Ability to proc them anyway.* Make sure to equip the item after you transform or it won't register your bonus effects properly. The Soulbreaker Greatsword is a nice little weapon to have for a Paladin but genuinely, as MotSS keeps your shapeshifted after you take it off, choosing Half-Orc or Elf is just Gith with extra steps.  
  

Drow

Basically a Wood Elf without the Stealth Proficiency and Movement Speed. They are also objectively cooler. Weapon Proficiencies don't matter as we'll be covering all of that with our classes, and a free Faerie Fire (lvl 3) & Darkness (lvl 5) cast can come in handy but it's never going to be game-changing. You also don't have to Shapeshift in Act 1 at all, which is convenient but again, not game-changing.   
  

Halfling

Halflings come with the Lucky & Brave features, which let you re-roll a 1 at will and give you Advantage on Fear checks, respectively. You can also choose between having Advantage on Stealth Checks or Advantage on Saving Throws against Poison and Resistance to Poison Damage. These are fairly handy features, but the drawback is you move 1.5m less than your long-legged counterparts and again, don't get relevant Proficiencies.  
  

Dwarf

Dwarven Resilience, which is Advantage against being Poisoned and Resistance to Poison Damage. Gold Dwarf is a good shout for extra HP, especially in the early acts, and Duergar for the Invis and Advantage against Illusions, Charm and Paralysis is also good. Honestly, you don't need the Invis and the Advantage on Paralysis Saves is probably a waste; if you ever need to use it, you've made a mistake by allowing whatever it is to attempt to Paralyse you in the first place. The Enlarge cast is also good but situational and it only lasts for 10 turns. You also have less Movement Speed than the long-legged races.  
  

Tiefling

Tieflings are cool but all they're really offering consistently is Resistance to Fire. The 3 subraces all have some spells that you *might* use but they either have Charisma or Constitution as their modifiers. Mephistopheles Tieflings would probably be our pick for Mage Hand, closely followed by Asmodeus for the Hellish Rebuke with Zariel coming in last with Thaumaturgy. As for their other spells: Produce Flame sucks, Darkness is fine, Flame Blade & Burning Hands most likely won't be used and Searing Smite is... well... you should know our opinions on this from our info on Karlach. We don't think it's worth it, to be honest.   
  

Gnome

Advantage on Wisdom, Charisma and Intelligence Saving Throws. The Sub-Race bonuses are all meh here for Paladin too, as doubling your History Proficiency Bonus, Speak with Animals or Advantage on Stealth Checks are either pointless or situational. They have nice saving buffs but like Duergar, if you're utilising them it means you've played too risky or made a mistake, and you're also nerfed in movement. There's still better options above, truthfully.  
  

Human

Shield Proficiency and an extra Proficiency, with extra Carry Weight. Meh. Next.   
  

Dragonborn

Oh, this is what's next? Ouch...  
Objectively the coolest-looking race and also objectively the worst to play as; you're nerfing yourself for zero reason as these guys literally have nothing going for them, not even Darkvision. Don't pick Dragonborn unless you plan on impressing your enemies to death with your sick horns and tail. Let us know how it goes in the comments.   
  
We feel like High Elf is a serious contender for top spot with melee classes now, but our runs were done with Half-Orc as they were in Patch 7. As always, it's up to you. Remember, we've based these selections on what we found to be consistently useful throughout our runs and comparing what you would lose when compared to our optimal picks. You can have a different opinion to us but we believe Half-Orc to be the optimum and therefore "Best" pick for pretty much any melee-heavy character.

## Paladin: Stats

Here are the stat spreads you want for your Paladin. We have broken them down with optimum switcheroos in mind, but it is completely up to you how much of our advice you take.   
  

Character Creation Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 14  
Con = 16  
Int = 8   
Wis = 12  
Cha = 16  
  
This is your spread at character creation. You can play with Wis and Cha for a bit, for example going 14 apiece for a more even spread but remember, Paladins' Spellcasting Modifier is 'risma and they get bonuses (mainly Aura of Protection at lvl 6) based off of it. We'll tell you here and then probably remind you a few times later on, but your spellcasting modifier *does not affect the strength of your Smites*, only the level of spellslot you used. You may only have 2 skills that go to 16; later on we will be respec-ing to get 18 Cha. That means your Wis will have to take a hit but for now, that 17 is a complete waste of a point that you can utilise elsewhere.  
  
Strength 8 is because we're going to dump Strength, as you will be using Hill Giant Elixirs and, when you unlock them, Cloud Giant Elixirs.   
Dex 14 is as high as you'll want it to be as we'll be using Medium Armour and then Heavy later on down the line; you can only gain a maximum AC modifier of +2 when wearing Medium Armour.  
Con 16 gives you respectable health and a tasty +3 to Con saves.  
Int 8 will make Arcana checks suck but it contributes nothing to the build and you’ll grab the Warped Headband of Intellect anyway if you *really* want some brainpower.  
Wis 12 is just what we have left over that's useful. This is why we recommend Wood Elf as joint second for your selections, as that Perception Proficiency turns this +1 into a +3.  
Cha 16 is because we will be pumping this later to get better buffs from Aura of Protection, and it's always nice to have high Cha for early game speech checks.  
  

Auntie Ethel Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 14  
Con = 16  
Int = 8   
Wis = 10  
Cha = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
  
You'll want to boost that 'risma to 18; Paladins get Aura of Protection based off of their Charisma Modifier and as such, we will want it to be as high as we can get it to reap those benefits. Wisdom has to go in order to get us up to 17 Cha to then even it up with Hag's Hair. It is what it is. You *can* swap out some Con if 10 Wisdom makes you uncomfortable and as well it should: you're going to be fairly vulnerable to being Held until you get items to mitigate it and your Zai'thisk checks are going to solely rely on Bliss Spores. It's up to you.   
  

Amulet of Greater Health Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 16  
Con = 8  
Int = 10  
Wis = 14  
Cha = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
  
The Amulet of Greater Health allows you to dump Con & Str, which gives you more room for Wisdom. Bear in mind that you won't see this spread until Act 3 and by then, in our experience, you're not really going to be needing it for many checks and you'll have managed so far without it, so it's up to you. Int 10 is literally because we have extra points and nowhere else to put them. Dex being 16 is mainly for the Initiative and improved Dex Saves as you won't see any benefits from that extra point with regards to your AC.  
  
*These next two stat spreads are pretty extra; you can stop here and skip to the build section if you want. For you fellow Turbo-Nerds, read on.*   
  

Araj + Astarions Strength Potion

Str = 10  
Dex = 14  
Con = 16  
Int = 8   
Wis = 10  
Cha = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
  
If you make Astarion bite Araj in Act 2, you will get a potion that permanently increases your Strength by +2. "Aren't we using Strength Elixirs?" we hear you cry. Yes, but there is a reason for gaining these extra 2 points and they are not mandatory.  
  

[Mirror of Loss](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FMirror_of_Loss)[bg3.wiki]

Str = 10  
Dex = 14  
Con = 16  
Int = 8   
Wis = 10  
Cha = 19 +1 (Hags Hair)  
  
We can afford to play with Str now since we are offsetting it with the potion. This will be your endgame stat spread; you won't enjoy these stats until Act 3 but be mindful that you may not get the stat points in Cha (read the wiki page on how the Mirror works.) You can add the Amulet of Greater Health to dump your Con and get your 'Wisdom up to 14, but as we said you will have some leftover points to drop into Int. Which is fine. Again, you'll be hammering the 'risma to get those sweet Aura of Protection bonuses.  
  
It is worth mentioning that this is min/max'ing and you can function on 18 Charisma for the entirety of the run with no issues.

## Paladin: Builds and Multiclassing

Paladin builds are many but the premise behind all of them is Big Smites. You want to hit things and hit them hard while having as many spellslots as possible. Inevitably, as Paladins are half-casters, this means a multiclass. There are more options than what we are presenting here but as always, this is the build we used on a successful run so here it is, level by level, choice by choice.  
  

Paladin Build Breakdown

  
We went for the Paladin 6 Abjuration Wizard 4 Fighter 2 approach as we found this gave us a tasty balance between defence and spellslots. For those that don't know, Abjuration Wizards gain something called Arcane Ward which blocks damage equal to its intensity then decreases intensity by 1. You gain intensity by casting Abjuration spells; the ones you want will be listed below.  
Without further ado:  
  
**Lvl 1: Oath of Vengeance Paladin** Paladin is your first level (shock). Take Oath of Vengeance as when you get Vow of Enmity at Lvl 3 (literally after leaving the Grove, which is madness) you cast it on yourself to gain Advantage on *all enemies* for 10 turns. Yes. Really. We think it's a bug but it's whatever at this stage, it ain't getting fixed. At least it isn't "Cast Darkness + Devil's Sight" or "cheese Raph for the Armour" levels of trash... If you don't wanna use this for whatever reason, Oathbreaker is your next best bet due to Aura of Hate at Lvl 6 but beyond that, we'll be focusing on Vengeance as this is what we actually used.  
  
We recommend Athletics and Insight as your Proficiencies as you will have high 'risma; you can take Persuasion or Intimidation if you want instead of Athletics. As always it's up to you.  
  
**Lvl 2: Oath of Vengeance Paladin** The entire reason you take Paladin has arrived! Divine Smite. Divine. Smite. DIVINE SMITE! Let it roll off the tongue. After you connect with an attack, your weapon deals an extra 2d8 Radiant Damage, 3d8 against Fiends and Undead and *you can use it as many times as you like as long as you have a spellslot available.* It's also worth mentioning that each level of spell you use adds another 1d8 but this caps out at 4th Level Spells; any higher and you don't get more than 5d8 or 6d8 against Fiends and Undead. Delicious. As of now, you have 2 Lvl 1 Spellslots to use and whatever you use them on at this stage in the game usually won't live through it. We recommend you acquaint the Bugbear and Za'krug in the first Grove Fight with them.  
  
Last thing on Divine Smite: go into your Reactions menu and set it to Ask on Hit and Ask on Crit; you must do this every time you get a higher level spellslot as it counts as an entirely different reaction. This way, you can decide on every attack that hits what will and will not survive that turn. You don't want it on Always On because if something has next to no health, you waste the spellslot; you don't need to add extra damage dice when something will die to your normal weapon damage right? Do not be afraid to burn all your slots in a difficult fight as Camp Supplies are plentiful and you should be looting everything. it's not uncommon in a Paladin Solo to be Resting after every other fight. Momma's big strong boy needs his beauty sleep, a-yes he does!  
  
Take Great Weapon Fighting as your Fighting Style to reroll 1s & 2s on your damage dice (including your Smites!) and for your spells grab Shield of Faith, Command and Protection from Evil and Good. Grab whatever else you feel like but between your upcoming Vow of Enmity and wanting to save spellslots for Smites, you won't use anything else; Shield of Faith is a nice +2 AC bonus in the beginning of the game and Command is nice to keep particularly spicy things calm while it waits its turn to be Smote to bits. PfEaG will be mandatory in Act 2. Additionally Wrathful, Searing and Thunderous Smites are worse versions of Divine, don't use these; they cost your Bonus Action + a spellslot and do less damage. They're just not worth it.   
  
**Lvl 3: Oath of Vengeance Paladin** Here we go... Vow of Enmity is here, along with Abjure Enemy, which is whatever. You also get Bane and Hunter's Mark, both of which are pointless when you think about casting them or think about having another Smite. Unfortunately this is going to be a pattern until you get yourself Wizard levels and the Helm of Arcane Acuity (see the gear section) and even then you'll mostly want to use scrolls as a Smite is just the better option for your spellslots. Speaking of, you now have an extra lvl 1 to use. Tidy.  
  
**Lvl 4: Oath of Vengeance Paladin** is where we get our first really spicy upgrade. Take Great Weapon Master as your Feat, which is Sharpshooter for Melee. You also gain the ability to make another Melee Attack using your Bonus Action when you kill something or land a Crit; you have Etsy Extra Attack 1 level before everyone else gets an Extra Attack... and you have Smites. Quick little mathematical intermission: assuming you're drinking Hill Giant Elixirs (which you definitely should be) you're now doing your chosen weapon's damage + 5 + 10 + 2d8 Radiant, which when it crits is all doubled, and then you get to do it again with your Bonus Action. At level 4. *4*. As for your -5 chance to hit for that +10, Vow of Enmity has you covered with Advantage, as mathematically Advantage is equivalent to +3-5 on your rolls and you're gonna be using a weapon that gives you a 1d4 to your chance to hit, so that's 5-20% extra Hit Chance right there. You can also turn off All In and still get the Bonus Action attack on crits. It's just broken, in all honesty. No spellslot this level though. Boo.   
  
**Lvl 5: Oath of Vengeance Paladin** is where things just get silly. Extra Attack is here, so now you get all of the above guaranteed twice per turn + your Bonus Action if you crit. You also get Hold Person and Misty Step for free, which is nice, but you're still better off using Scrolls and saving your spellslots. You should take Magic Weapon as your spell this level too, as you now get to choose between Shield of Faith or Magic Weapon as your Concentration Spell for the remainder of Act 1 (you *really* want PfEaG in Act 2). Do you want +2AC or +1 to Attack Rolls and Damage? Choose depending on your fight, is our advice. Keeping track of your Smites, you now have four lvl 1 slots and two lvl 2 slots. Use them liberally. Remember, your lvl 2s add an extra 1d8 to your Radiant Damage, so use those on things you want to delete.   
If you picked High Elf as your Race, Booming Blade also gets a buff now that applies 1d8 Thunder to your damage roll and 2d8 Thunder if the enemy moves. Start pushing things, knife-ear!

## Paladin: Builds and Multiclassing cont.

**Lvl 6: Oath of Vengeance Paladin** is our final level of Paladin and where all this 'risma finally becomes useful. You get Aura of Protection which adds your 'risma to your Saving Throws. All of them. You now have +4 to all of your Saving Throws without Bless. Yeah, seriously. It only disappears if you get knocked Unconscious but if that happens you're probably dead anyway. You can pick another spell too but once again, it really doesn't matter what you pick because you probably (definitely) won't use it. No extra spellslots here either. Don't worry, that's gonna change next level...  
  
**Lvl 7: Oath of Vengeance Paladin 6 Wizard 1** is where we start to build on the foundations of Smites and Aura of Protection.  
You also get Arcane Recovery which is another lvl 1 spellslot with extra steps, as you can replenish spellslots outside of combat.   
  
You can pick 3 Cantrips here and we recommend Booming Blade if you didn't pick High Elf. For those that didn't, you now basically want to use Booming Blade instead of attacking normally, as it also triggers Divine Smite, inflicts 1d8 extra Thunder Damage (as we're past lvl 5) and inflicts 2d8 Thunder damage when the target moves that turn. Yes, really. Well done if you chose Athletics over a dialogue Proficiency, as now you can shove stuff to trigger extra damage with your Bonus Action if you have it to spare and you have a better chance at landing those shoves. Your other two cantrips can be anything you like as Booming Blade is the real star of the show here, although there's something to be said for Minor Illusion being able to group things for CC or lure stragglers away to be Smote.  
  
As for spells, Shield is mandatory to improve your survivability and as it is also an Abjuration spell, it will contribute to your Arcane Ward next level. Shield will drastically increase your defence but remember, it will eat into your Smites. The saving grace is if you're dead you can't Smite at all, so don't be scared to use Shield liberally, as it gets stronger next level.   
Also remember you can memorise up to lvl 3 spell scrolls now (as you're technically a lvl 5 caster) so it really doesn't matter what spells you take vs what scrolls you use, so from here on out we'll recommend good spells for utility and CC but you don't have to take them or prioritise them other than Shield as you still want spell slots for Smites.  
  
**Lvl 8: Oath of Vengeance Paladin 6 Abjuration Wizard 2** choose the School of Abjuration: every time you really don't wanna take a hit, ping Shield as your Reaction and you get +5AC for the rest of the turn, automatically block all Magic Missile missiles (yep, try it!) and increase your Arcane Ward intensity by 1 which means 1 less damage next time you don't use Shield. It also procs when you cast Protection from Evil and Good, which you should be doing as you'll be in Act 2 by now. This might not seem like a lot but with our equipment guide, this will be adding to your inherent equipment bonuses that also nullify damage. Remember, you can also cast Shield multiple times in a fight to stack that bonus.  
  
**Lvl 9: Oath of Vengeance Paladin 6 Abjuration Wizard 3** is where we slowly but steadily begin to climb the steps of greatness.   
You *can* take Shadow Blade but remember this is a one-handed weapon and we're specc'd for two-handers, plus if you're gonna do that respec and go full Bladesinger. Seriously. You don't need smites if you take Shadow Blade and we'll explain why in our Bladesinger section.  
  
**Lvl 10: Oath of Vengeance Paladin 6 Abjuration Wizard 4** is where our spellslots are finalised. You get another Cantrip and another spell here but same old same old, we don't really care about any of that. We want Booming Blade + Smites + Shield with Arcane Ward. That's all we care about.  
As for your Feat, Savage Attacker is what you want here; coupled with your Great Weapon Fighting your damage dice are now being re-rolled if you get a 1 or a 2 and *then* Savage Attacker rolls *again* and picks the highest result. It does this not only for your weapon damage but your Smites too, so the odds of you getting low damage rolls have now become very very low. Your Savage Attacker rerolls aren't what you're stuck with either: if you roll a 6 and a 3 on your first rolls and then a 3 and an 8 on your second ones, SA picks the highest so you end up with 6+8. You basically have Advantage on your damage dice, with the ability to reroll 1s and 2s, which in flat numbers adds on average 30% more damage due to getting better rolls. We're just breaking the game at this stage. You can also get an ASI and stick your 'risma up to 20 which gives you +5 on your Aura of Protection but Savage Attacker is, in our opinion, way more useful.  
  
**Lvl 11: Oath of Vengeance Paladin 6 Abjuration Wizard 4 Fighter 1** has a choice integrated into it. We made our decision with Fighter as we want Action Surge but you *could* keep going with Wizard if you wanted to get those dope lvl 3 slots but realistically, Action Surge is going to be putting out more DPS for those big opening turns where stuff just needs to be focused down (we're looking at you Ansur, Sarevok and Orin). If you do choose to go for Wizard 6, Counterspell is mandatory (and is also an Abjuration spell). Protection from Energy is nice for Ansur if you don't have The Sparkswall or don't want to wear it for whatever reason but you can also get that in scroll form. There's also Elixirs of Universal Resistance at this point too.  
Anyway, if you do choose to follow us and go Fighter, pick Defence as your second style for that +1 AC.  
  
**Lvl 12: Oath of Vengeance Paladin 6 Abjuration Wizard 4 Fighter 2** ends this build with a bit of a whimper rather than a bang, to be honest. Action Surge is here. Take another turn for free every short rest. You've earned it.  
  
That's pretty much it! As we said, all of this is your choice and you can choose to take other things but this is what we did. It's worth noting we didn't have Booming Blade when we completed our run with this build, so if anything it should be easier for anyone attempting a Paladin Solo these days. We will also reiterate if you want to take Shadow Blade, respec and go full Bladesinger or go Bladesinger 10 Paladin 2, which is technically a Bladesinger build, which we'll cover further into the guide.   
  
On to equipment!

## Paladin: Act 1 Equipment Loadout

Gimme the Good Stuff

We'll go slot by slot, act by act. We'll list the order of items that you want to show your progression and tell you why it's good. If you just want to know what we wore at the end of each milestone/what we consider to be BiS, skip ahead to the "End of Act X" sections.  

Act 1 Helmets

* [Haste Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHaste_Helm)[bg3.wiki] - Momentum for 3 turns at the start of combat. Coupled with extra move speed from Longstrider (+ your racial passive if you picked Wood Elf) you are *rapid* in combat. You find this in the chest next to the fish wagon in the Blighted Village. Probably BiS until Act 2, especially on Half-Orc.
* [Grymskull Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGrymskull_Helm)[bg3.wiki] - Crit Immunity, Resistance to Fire and a cast of Hunter's Mark. As you probably won't be using either version of the Adamantine Armour or the Adamantine Shield, you probably want to stick this on until the Creche and definitely keep it for fights where you need that Crit Immunity. You get it for killing Grym in the Grymforge.

Act 1 Cloaks

* Unless you go for the Deathstalker Mantle, there aren't any cloaks here.

Act 1 Chest Pieces

* [Githyanki Half-Plate](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGithyanki_Half_Plate)[bg3.wiki] - 15 AC as base with Disadvantage on Stealth Checks. You can steal this from Lae'zel after the very first Imp fight.
* [Luminous Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FLuminous_Armour)[bg3.wiki] - Possibly BiS until Act 3, definitely BiS until Act 2. When you deal Radiant Damage, you emit a Radiant Shockwave. Radiant Shockwave inflicts Radiating Orb in a 3m Radius around you. Radiating Orb reduces affected entities Attack Rolls by 1 per turn remaining and the standard duration is 2 Turns. Divine Smite deals Radiant Damage; you're basically indirectly increasing your AC per turn of this on an enemy which can lead to making scary fights laughable when you start stacking Radiating Orb gear. Yes, your AC for wearing this is 17 baseline, but with the Ring of Protection that's 18 and with Shield of Faith/Tyr's Protection you're at 20. Every stack of Radiating Orb can be considered a -5% chance for the enemy to hit you and it can stack up to 10 times which is mathematically giving you 28-30AC. This is so good we don't use it on most of our runs now because it makes you impossible to hit when used correctly. You get it from the Selunite Outpost in the locked and trapped chest behind the wall; if you fail the Perception check you can jump through a crack in the wall in the next room. Yes, you can get it as early as that.
* [Adamantine Splint Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAdamantine_Splint_Armour)[bg3.wiki] - This armour is good, giving you 18 AC, reducing incoming damage by 2 and making you immune to critical hits. It also inflicts the Reeling condition, which is a stacking debuff that gives anything that hits you a -1 to Attack Rolls per stack remaining.  
  You can take the Medium armour version, but we go for the Heavy because it is *functionally the same except the Heavy version has more dmg reduction.* Same AC with our Dex, still causes Reeling, still gives you crit immunity. It is up to you but Heavy seems the logical choice. You get this by forging it in the Adamantine Forge (read the wiki for how to do this).

Act 1 Gloves

* [Gloves of Power](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Power)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Sleight of Hand. They're better than nothing, but the Absolute's Bane doesn't proc unless you have the Absolute's Brand and by the time you get that you'll have better gloves. Loot them from Zak'rug after the fight in front of the Grove.
* [Fleetfingers](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFleetfingers)[bg3.wiki] - Use your Dash and you can Jump without using a Bonus Action. Coupled with the Boots of Speed, you can now Dash as a Bonus Action and Jump for free on top of it. Nice. You get these by looting a chest in the Forest leading to Ethel's Swamp (check the wiki page for more details). Don't worry if you fail the Perception check, as you can dig it up regardless; check the Specific Tips and Tricks section right at the tippy-top of the guide to find out how.
* [Gloves of the Growling Underdog](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_the_Growling_Underdog)[bg3.wiki] - +1 to Strength Saving Throws and Advantage on Attack Rolls while surrounded by 2 or more foes. This is more of a contingency than something that actively boosts your performance, as you should really avoid being surrounded in the first place. Still, Advantage when there's two things on top of you is good justification for turning on All In and deleting one from existence quickly. You get these in the Shattered Sanctum in the treasure chests behind Dror Ragzlin.
* [Wondrous Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FWondrous_Gloves)[bg3.wiki] - +1AC and extra Bardic Inspiration. First bit is nice, second bit is utterly useless. Still, +1AC adds up, especially if you're using the Luminous Armour. You get these from one of the Mimics near the Harper Cache.

Act 1 Boots

* [Githyanki Boots](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGithyanki_Boots)[bg3.wiki]- +1 Athletics. Better than no shoes. Also steal these from Lae'zel after the very first Imp fight.
* [Swiresy Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSwiresy_Shoes)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Acrobatics and increased Jump distance of 1.5m. Buy them from Grat the Trader.
* [Boots of Speed](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Speed)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 2. Gives you a bootleg version of Cunning Action: Dash. Get these from Thulla in the Myconid Colony. Be nice to her and give her an antidote, she'll give you these boots. You can give them back to Sergeant Thrinn for the quest and kill her to get them back, but we normally keep them.
* [Boots of Stormy Clamour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Stormy_Clamour)[bg3.wiki] - Causes Reverberation on a target when you hit it with another condition. Radiating Orb is a condition... you do the maths. You can argue these are also BiS depending on how hard you want to go in on targets, as a Reverb/Radiating Orb build is a known build that absolutely trivialises the game until you get to the really OP stuff. Up to you what you use. Buy them from Omeluum after completing his quest.
* [Disintegrating Night Walkers](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDisintegrating_Night_Walkers)[bg3.wiki] - Free Misty Step, you can't slip on Ice or Grease and cannot be Ensnared, Enwebbed or Entangled. They're ok but we prefer Click Heels. You get these from killing Nere.

Act 1 Amulets

* [Silver Pendant](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSilver_Pendant)[bg3.wiki] - Guidance for free. We argue this is BiS until you get to the creche, but be aware it is a Concentration spell which can override your Shield of Faith/Tyr's Protection. Find this on a plateau outside the Grove, should be on your left as you head west towards the Blighted Village. Check the wiki link for more detailed directions.
* [Broodmothers' Revenge](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBroodmother%2527s_Revenge)[bg3.wiki] - Adds 1d6 Poison dmg after a heal. This is classed as a coating so you can't use it with other Poisons, which kind of kills its overall usefulness. Oil of Accuracy, Diluted Oil of Sharpness, Drow Poison and Malice are really strong, worth way more than 1d6 Poison, especially because Poison is one of the most resisted dmg types in the game. For Act 1 it's ok, but remember to re-equip your Guidance Amulet for conversations. You get this from killing Kagha; it's easier to do so after the Tieflings have left and Halsin has given her a smacked bottom, as she goes to the entrance of the Grove away from anyone who might snitch you up for murder.
* [Amulet of Misty Step](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Misty_Step)[bg3.wiki] - Free Misty Step cast per Short Rest. You get this from a chest in Gut's Private Quarters, just don't let Paloma catch you. Or, you know, kill her first. That works too.

## Paladin: Act 1 Equipment Loadout Cont.

Act 1 Amulets Cont.

* [The Ever-Seeing Eye](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Ever-Seeing_Eye) [bg3.wiki]- Free Protection from Good and Evil per Short Rest. It also stays on when you remove the amulet, so that's nice. Grab this from Ethel's back room. Honestly, this is more prep for Act 2 than something you wanna use now.
* [Amulet of the Unworthy](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_the_Unworthy)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to Slashing damage and Vulnerability to Bludgeoning damage. This is slept on as it *sounds* trash but when you can guarantee something isn't going to do Bludgeoning damage (inside Myrkuls arena, for example) it's really nice to have. We grab this for Myrkul later on. You grab it from... I can't believe my eyes... A Minotaur... In the Underdark near the Selunite Outpost.   
    

  Act 1 Rings
* [The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki] - Bless for 2 turns after you heal. This is BiS until Act 3, hands-down. Bless is a flat 10% to your Hit Chance and 1d4 to your saving throws. Couple this with your Safeguard Shield/Absolute's Warboard and you now have minimum +2 to saving throws. It's starting to come together and you haven't even left the grove yet! Buy this from Volo in the Grove after you pass the three Druids forming the roadblock.
* [Crusher's Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCrusher%2527s_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - A handy little ring that increases you movement speed by 3m. Until you get the Ring of Protection, this and TWP are your go-tos. You get this after killing Crusher in the Gobbo Camp Exterior.
* [Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki] - Arguably BiS along with The Whispering Promise until the back end of Act 2/Act 3. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws. You get this from stealing the Idol of Silvanus for Mol in the Grove. ***You must make sure to get the quest from Mol BEFORE you rescue Halsin.***
* [Ring of Poison Resistance](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Poison_Resistance)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to being Poisoned and Poison Damage (these aren't the same thing). Situational but it comes in handy in those situations. Auntie Ethel and the Phase Spider Matriarch are both good places to use this. You get it from a Sarcophagus outside of the Worg Pens in the Shattered Sanctum.

Act 1 Ranged Weapons

* [Hunting Shortbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHunting_Shortbow)[bg3.wiki] - Advantage on Monstrosities. This is a stat stick and so you can shoot levers and stuff from a distance. Monstrosity Advantage comes in handy against some stuff in the Underdark (Hook Horrors and Minotaurs mainly). Hunter's Mark is extra Damage so it's basically a toss-up between this and Shield of Faith in the early game; we value more AC over the Damage, especially with Great Weapon Fighting in play, but it can be used to open Combat (and Surprise Enemies) *and* it is re-castable for free as long as you hold your Concentration so it's not without its' perks. It will definitely lose usefulness once you get the Boots of Speed though as we pretty much end up using Click Heels every turn for the sheer amount of kiting potential they give.
* [Bow of Awareness](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBow_of_Awareness)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Initiative. As above, stat stick and utility tool. Buy it from Roah Moonglow in the Shattered Sanctum.

Act 1 Melee Weapons

* [Everburn Blade](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FEverburn_Blade)[bg3.wiki] - extra 1d4 Fire Damage but remember due to the way that it's coded, it's classed as already Dipped so you can't increase this by trying to double dip. You get this from casting Command: Drop on Zhalk in the Nautiloid. You won't have access to this so you'll need Shadowheart to get it. You could start as a Cleric then switch to Paladin if you wanted a real true solo but honestly for the sake of 1d4 fire and what you have to do to get it, it's not *that* amazing. Still, we know there'd be tears in the comments if we didn't mention it.
* [Sword of Justice](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSword_of_Justice)[bg3.wiki] - Greatsword + 1 with a bespoke version of Shield of Faith that doesn't cost a spellslot, which is nice. You can get this from Anders the Paladin in the Toll house up on the Risen Road by either mauling him or mauling Karlach then showing him her head. We recommend Option B as Karlach is overrated (yeah we said it) and it means he's unarmed when you fight him, which vastly reduces the threat he poses.
* [Svartlebee's Woundseeker](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSvartlebee%2527s_Woundseeker)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Greatsword that gives you 1d4 to Attack Rolls against things that are already damage. This is extra Bless against wounded targets and goes a long way to overcoming your -5 To Hit from Great Weapon Master. This is easily BiS until you get to the Creche and honestly we'd argue into Act 2, as it isn't 1d8 it's 2d4, which means as a minimum you get an extra 10% To Hit chance against wounded targets *before you bring Vow of Enmity into the equation*. Madness. You get this from Gauntlet Yeva outside Waukeen's Rest; you can kill all of the Flaming Fist outside before rescuing Florrick and she's still cool with you but be aware it's a pretty naughty fight without any CC.
* [Doom Hammer](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDoom_Hammer)[bg3.wiki] - Maul that inflicts Bone Chilled on hit. This is for Myrkul in Act 2 but you can use it until you get either Svartlebee's or the Sword of Justice. You buy it from Grat the Trader.

Act 1 Shields

* We won't be using Shields on this build.

At the end of Act 1, before you enter the Mountain Pass, our loadout was:  
  
[Haste Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHaste_Helm)[bg3.wiki]  
[Gloves of the Growling Underdog](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_the_Growling_Underdog)[bg3.wiki]

## Paladin: Act 1.5 Equipment Loadout

Continuing the loadout, here are the items you will find in the Mountain Pass/Githyanki Creche, followed by what we were wearing into Act 2.  
  

Act 1.5 Helmets

* [Holy Lance Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHoly_Lance_Helm)[bg3.wiki] - +1 to Constitution Saving Throws and anything that misses an Attack Roll against you must make a DC 14 Dexterity Saving throw or take 1d4 Radiant damage. Yes, this procs Radiating Orb. Yes, this basically gives you free Radiating Orb and Reverb when stuff misses you, which it will do often when you start stacking Radiant Orb. See how the build just comes together and makes the game easy? See now why people like us don't use it? You get this from a painted chest at the top of the monastery.
* [Diadem of Arcane Synergy](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDiadem_of_Arcane_Synergy)[bg3.wiki] - This adds your Spellcasting Modifier to your next attack when you inflict a condition. 'Risma should be 18 by now, which means +4 to your next attack when you're inflicting a condition which, let's be honest, you're using the Luminous Armour aren't you, so these will proc often. It's up to you whether you want the Holy Lance Helm for defence or this for Offence. You get this by killing Ardent Jhe'rezath in the W'wargaz fight.

Act 1.5 Cloaks

* Unless you have the Deathstalker Mantle, there aren't any cloaks here.

Act 1.5 Chest Pieces

* There aren't any chestpieces here that are better than the Adamantine Splint Mail or Luminous Armour.

Act 1.5 Gloves

* [Gloves of Belligerent Skies](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Belligerent_Skies)[bg3.wiki] - Inflicts Reverberation against a target when you deal Thunder, Lightning or Radiant damage. This can proc with your Booming Blade or your Smites. Reverberation is a condition, which will trigger your Boots of Stormy Clamour too. You find these in a chest in Inquisitor W'wargaz' room.

Act 1.5 Boots

* There are no boots better than the Boots of Speed here.

Act 1.5 Amulets

* [Periapt of Wound Closure](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FPeriapt_of_Wound_Closure)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 3, and even then it's a toss-up. This amulet stabilises you at the start of your next turn, which is useless as the game ends before that can happen, but it also *gives you the maximum amount of HP possible from any source of healing.* Greater Healing Potions are now a flat 20HP. Superiors are 40HP. Your cheeky Healing Potions to proc Bless (you're wearing TWP right?) before every fight are now a flat 10HP, which will keep you topped up easily. You NEED this guarantee in a fight to know where you stand, where you can take risks and where you need to bail. We cannot stress enough how important removing the randomness from healing potions is. You can buy this from Lady Esther.

Act 1.5 Rings

* [Strange Conduit Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FStrange_Conduit_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - Adds 1d4 Psychic damage to your weapon attacks while you're Concentrating. Shield of Faith, Magic Weapon and Protection from Evil and Good, which is about to be 50% of the MVP in Act 2 for you, will all add this damage. You can swap out TWP or RoP for this but be aware of what you are sacrificing for it. You get this from a chest in Inquisitor W'Wargaz' room.

Act 1.5 Ranged Weapons

* Nothing here that's a better stat stick than the Bow of Awareness.

Act 1.5 Melee Weapons

* [Soulbreaker Greatsword](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSoulbreaker_Greatsword)[bg3.wiki] - +2 to Initiative, +1d4 Psychic if wielded by a Githyanki and the Soulbreaker ability, which allows you to deal additional Psychic damage equal to your Proficiency Bonus and possibly Stuns the target. Hands down the best weapon we've encountered so far in the game for this build. We will miss Woundseeker but at this point that extra 1d4 shouldn't be making the difference since you can afford to miss, given that we *know* you're using Radiating Orb + Reverb gear. What's that? "You told us to pick High Elf or Half-Orc?" Yes we did, so use your Mask of the Shapeshifter to shift into a Gith and get the 1d4 Psychic anyway. Don't have MotSs? It's still worth using for the Soulbreaker ability and the Initiative alone. You get this from killing Kith'rak Therezzyn.

Act 1.5 Shields

* We don't use shields here.

After the Creche, our loadout looked like this:
