## Paladin: Act 2 Equipment Loadout

Moving straight into Act 2, here's the good gear you definitely want.  

Act 2 Helmets

* [Flawed Helldusk Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFlawed_Helldusk_Helmet)[bg3.wiki] - +1 to Con Saving Throws and +2 to Saving Throws against spells. Nice. You get this from Dammon by giving him a piece of Infernal Iron.
* [Helmet of Arcane Acuity](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelmet_of_Arcane_Acuity)[bg3.wiki] - This thing is godlike, especially if you've been stacking scrolls. Gain Arcane Acuity stacks when you make a successful weapon attack. What this means is when you hit things, your spellsave DC goes up by 1 per stack of the condition. This means every stack increases the likelihood of your spells succeeding by 5%. No, that's not a typo. Until you hit Act 3 your combat strat is now "maul something in melee in the first round of combat and avoid getting hit, then cast CC spell/scroll, mop up the battlefield." You can also drink a Haste Potion (or have Haste pre-cast but have a contingency if you lose Concentration) and pull off CC in the first round; Hypnotic Pattern is a really solid option and pairs beautifully with the Ring of the Mystic Scoundrel which we'll grab in Act 3. This isn't mandatory per se but it's handy to have as until now, we weren't spec'ced for offensive magic and now we are. It's worth mentioning too that if you still rock the Holy Lance Helm inside the Shar Temple, that Radiant Retort is gonna hurt you. Please don't Smite in there either. Or, do it and tell us in the comments. You get this from the secret meeting chamber in the Mason's Guild (check the wiki for better directions).

Act 2 Cloaks

* [Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws. If you've been using the Mantle, now is the time to free yourself from the shackles of mediocrity. You buy this from Quartermaster Talli in Last Light.

Act 2 Chest Pieces

* [Dwarven Splint Mail](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDwarven_Splintmail)[bg3.wiki] - This Heavy Armour has 19 base AC, gives you +1 to Strength Saving Throws and Ability Checks, gives you +2 Constitution and reduces all Piercing Damage by 1. That Con boost & the Str saves are unbelievably helpful for Myrkul. You get this by passing an Insight Check while talking to Disciple Z'rell and then telling Lann Tarv that you can see his special stash. If you fail this Insight Check, however, do not despair. Your Luminous Armour is still able to mitigate this loss.

Act 2 Gloves

* [Luminous Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FLuminous_Gloves)[bg3.wiki] - +1 to Strength Saving Throws and Radiating Orb when you deal Radiant damage. These finish off the Radiating Orb + Reverb build and it's up to you if you want more stacks of Orb or more stacks of Reverb. You get these from the potters chest in the Ruined Battlefield.
* [Gloves of the Automaton](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_the_Automaton)[bg3.wiki] - Advantage on your Weapon Attacks & Resistance to Lightning for 10 turns per Long Rest. You'll also be considered a Construct but that's kind of irrelevant. While these are nice for almost every other class, if you've kept your Oath of Vengeance, Vow of Enmity does this too when you cast it on yourself so they're kind of redundant; we left them in for all our Oathbreaker homies. Stay rebellious. Buy these from Barcus in Last Light Inn.
* [Flawed Helldusk Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFlawed_Helldusk_Gloves)[bg3.wiki] - 1d4 Fire damage for weapon attacks, and Unarmed Attacks deal 1d4 Necrotic and can Bleed your target. We don't really care about the Unarmed nonsense, we want that sweet, sweet damage rider. You grab these by giving Dammon a piece of Infernal Iron.
* [Gloves of Battlemage's Power](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Battlemage%2527s_Power) [bg3.wiki] - +1 to Strength Saving Throws and Arcane Acuity when you hit something with a cantrip or spell that uses a weapon... like Booming Blade. Honestly, these things are utterly busted. They're somewhat less useful to you as a Paladin as you won't really be slinging spells much and it is always a tossup between spell or smite but nonetheless they're here for you to use and you'll always proc them. Don't double up with the Helm of Arcane Acuity and these though, you don't need to. Pick one if you want to start using CC. You get these from the locked chest in the room with the two locked doors in the Reithwin Tollhouse.

Act 2 Boots

* [Evasive Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FEvasive_Shoes)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 3, IF you keep Last Light alive, otherwise BiS. +1 AC & +1 Acrobatics. You want to stack AC and these help you do that. That's pretty much it. Buy them from Mattis in Last Light.

Act 2 Amulets

* Periapt is still BiS. You can switch it up if you want but we don't recommend it.

Act 2 Rings

* [Ring of Free Action](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Free_Action)[bg3.wiki] - Not mandatory per se but worth picking up, especially when you're trying to take down a certain red-armoured ♥♥♥♥♥♥♥ in Act 3. You ignore Difficult Terrain and are immune to being Paralysed or Restrained. No Paralysis or Hold Person for you anymore! You'll probably want to switch this up with your Ring of Protection, as Bless from The Whispering Promise is more valuable. You buy this from Araj Oblodra in Moonrise Towers.
* [Risky Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRisky_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - Advantage on Attack Rolls but Disadvantage on Saving Throws. You have Aura of Protection which is +4 to Saving Throws and Advantage is mathematically +5. It would be safe to assume Disadvantage is -5, therefore you only have -1 as a Paladin. Up to you but again, Vow of Enmity does this in the fights where you really need it. You also buy this from Araj Oblodra in Moonrise Towers.
* [Killer's Sweetheart](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FKiller%2527s_Sweetheart)[bg3.wiki] - Free Crit after killing something. Useful for proccing your Great Weapon Master bonus attack and for maximising your damage, along with your Luck of the Far Realms. Probably BiS for the Myrkul fight but opposite to Gloom Stalker, drop The Whispering Promise and Keep the Ring of Protection, as you can't heal on Myrkul's platform. You get this by killing your doppelganger in the Shar Temple. For 4-man parties you can have characters engage each others doppelgangers, but only after theirs is dead.

## Paladin: Act 2 Equipment Loadout Cont.

Act 2 Ranged Weapons

* [Darkfire Shortbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDarkfire_Shortbow)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to Fire and Cold damage while allowing you to caste Haste every Long Rest. Kinda nice but may not be worth more than your Bow of Awareness. Up to you. Buy it from Dammon in Last Light.

Act 2 Melee Weapons

* [Halberd of Vigilance](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHalberd_of_Vigilance)[bg3.wiki] - +2 Halberd (sweet) that does 1d4 extra Force damage, gives you +1 to Initiative, Advantage on Perception Checks and when you make an Attack Roll as a Reaction you also have Advantage on that too. It's really up to you whether or not you use this or your Soulbreaker because you'll be using the Doom Hammer on Myrkul anyway but something to consider is that the Drakethroat will make it +3, you can put an Oil of Accuracy or Diluted Sharpness on it *and* cast Magic Weapon on yourself to make it either +5 or +6. You would then completely negate the -5 from All In... up to you! You buy this from Lann Tarv in Moonrise Towers.
* [Drakethroat Glaive](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDrakethroat_Glaive)[bg3.wiki] - This isn't for using, it's for enchanting your weapon with Thunder Damage (the least resisted damage type). It has an ability that casts Magic Weapon and adds 1d4 elemental damage of your choice to a weapon. The buff resets on Long Rest so you have to cast it again each time. Don't forget! Drop your weapon, equip the Drakethroat, use the ability on the weapon, pick up & re-equip your weapon. Job done. The buff resets on Long Rest so you have to cast it again each time. It's also worth noting that this *overrides and is overridden by regular Magic Weapon.* This means if you use the Drakethroat ability then cast Magic Weapon, you will not only now be Concentrating on Magic Weapon but you'll lose your extra 1d4 damage. You buy this from Roah Moonglow in Moonrise Towers.

Act 2 Shields

* Nope, still not using a shield.

## Paladin: End of Act 2 Equipment Loadout

Before we hit Myrkul we were equipped with:  
  
[Grymskull Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGrymskull_Helm)[bg3.wiki]   
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Dwarven Splint Mail](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDwarven_Splintmail)[bg3.wiki]  
[Flawed Helldusk Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FFlawed_Helldusk_Gloves)[bg3.wiki]  
[Evasive Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FEvasive_Shoes)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of the Unworthy](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_the_Unworthy)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Killer's Sweetheart](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FKiller%2527s_Sweetheart)[bg3.wiki]  
[Bow of Awareness](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBow_of_Awareness)[bg3.wiki]  
[Doom Hammer](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDoom_Hammer)[bg3.wiki]  
  
*Technically*, the best weapon you can use instead of Doom Hammer against Myrkul is a Maul +1 but the difference is 5% Hit Chance and +1 Damage, so don't feel the need to go hunting for one; Doom Hammer is enough, especially when you buff it with the Drakethroat and get a Diluted Oil of Sharpness on it then start Smiting, as he barely lasts 4 rounds.   
  
After Myrkul we switched back to our Halberd of Vigilance, Diadem of Arcane Synergy, The Whispering Promise and Periapt of Wound Closure to fight those Gith Monks and enter Rivington. Speaking of...

## Paladin: Act 3 Equipment Loadout

Act 3 Helmets

* [Helm of Balduran](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelm_of_Balduran)[bg3.wiki] - BiS, although you might wanna switch up with the Arcane Acuity sometimes for that sweet CC. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws, Crit Immunity, Stun Immunity and heal 2 points per turn. This is just a dope helmet in general and improves your survivability. The 2HP per turn may not seem like much but read on to find a very, very tidy little synergy with a pair of absolutely slept-on gauntlets. Additionally, the Stun Resistance is handy in the Brain fight for characters that need to get up close.
* [Sarevok's Horned Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSarevok%2527s_Horned_Helmet)[bg3.wiki] - Darkvision, Crit Threshold reduced by 1 and you can't be Frightened or have your emotions altered. Balduran's Helm is better in our opinion but this with the Deadshot means your Crit Thresholed goes down to 18, which with Advantage means you have a roughly 28% chance to land a Crit when you attack once and a roughly 49% chance to Crit when you attack twice. We like those odds, frankly. Get this from killing Sarevok. He should *definitely*  be on the receiving end of a Soulbreaker Smite.

Act 3 Cloaks

* Cloak of Protection is still BiS. You can try other cloaks but the +1AC & +1 to Saving Throws is too good. If you wear the Armour of Persistence, Balduran's Helm, this and your Ring of Protection, you're sitting on a very respectable 23 AC. If you insist:
* [Cloak of Displacement](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Displacement) [bg3.wiki]- Disadvantage on Attack Rolls against you. Basically Blur that refreshes every turn. You can use this instead of the Cloak of Protection when you know you won't be taking guaranteed damage/damage over time, as the effect is removed when you take damage. You can swap between this and the Cloak of Protection as and when you please, but there's not really anything else you'd want for this slot. You can buy it from Danthelon in Rivington.

Act 3 Chest Pieces

You have a choice to make in Act 3 about what direction you want to take your run. Your choice is similar damage to usual but way more defence, or going full send on offence and taking your damage to another level. The two chest pieces that allow you to do this are listed below. Trust us, these two are all you need.  

* [Armour of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FArmour_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki] - BiS for defence, hands down. 20 AC baseline, reduces all incoming damage by 2 and gives you Resistance and Blade Ward, which give you 1d4 to Saving Throws and Resistance to Bludgeoning, Piercing and Slashing damage, respectively. We'll give you that once more again: *resistance to all physical damage types and 1d4 to Saving Throws*. Yes, really. You can become tanky between this, Baldurans Helm, Boots of Persistence and Amulet of Greater Health, while still putting serious damage downrange. It just depends on how much risk you want to take. You buy these from Dammon at the Forge of the Nine.
* [Bhaalist Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBhaalist_Armour)[bg3.wiki] - BiS for offence and it isn't even close. Anything within 2m of you is now vulnerable to Piercing damage. Hey, doesn't Nylruna do Piercing Damage? Yeah... enjoy. You buy this from the Echo of Abazigal in the Murder Tribunal. You have to kill the little flying elephant thing and become an Unholy Assassin of Bhaal before you can talk to Abazigal.

Act 3 Gloves

* [Craterflesh Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCraterflesh_Gloves)[bg3.wiki] - Adds 1d6 Force damage on critical hits, which is actually 2d6 Force damage because... well... you just crit. Nice if you want to keep your Killer's Sweetheart around and/or try to stack some Crit Reduction, since Crits are way more likely when you have Advantage, which you do with Vow of Enmity. You buy these from the Echo of Abazigal in the Murder Tribunal.
* [The Reviving Hands](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Reviving_Hands)[bg3.wiki] - Cast Revivify, +1 to Strength Saving Throws and you cast Blade Ward or Death Ward on healthy or downed opponents, respectively, when you heal them. What you really want these for is to pair them with Balduran's Helm, which will proc Blade Ward for you every turn You buy these from Vicar Humbletoes in the Stormshore Tabernacle. What a name that is, by the way.
* [Legacy of the Masters](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FLegacy_of_the_Masters)[bg3.wiki] - +1 to Strength Saving Throws and +2 to Attack Rolls and Damage Rolls. These are basically two permanent Diluted Oil of Sharpness. Sick. You buy them from Dammon in the Forge of the Nine.

Act 3 Boots

* [Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. Freedom of Movement, Longstrider and +1 to Dex saves. Freedom of Movement is the same thing the Ring of Free Action gives you, so now you don't need to worry about being Held, Restrained or Paralysed ever again. Coupled with Balduran's Helm and you can't be Stunned either. Sweet. You buy these from Dammon in the Forge of the Nine.

Act 3 Amulets

* [Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki] - BiS. Gives you Advantage on Constitution Saving Throws and sets your Constitution score to 23. While the Periapt maxes out your heals and not having that will take some getting used to, being able to dump Constitution AND Strength is very very handy. You get this by robbing it from the House of Hope.

Act 3 Rings

* [Band of the Mystic Scoundrel](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBand_of_the_Mystic_Scoundrel)[bg3.wiki] - BiS when you're using the Helmet of Arcane Acuity. This allows you to cast Illusion Spells as a bonus action after hitting something with a weapon attack. So your combat strat will be opening round dealing damage and weapon attacks, then use your Bonus Action to cast Hold Person/Hold Monster and mop up the next turn. Arcane Acuity means these spells will be anywhere between 80-95% success chance due to your Spell DC being monumentally high. You get this by stealing the Djinni's ring in the Circus, spinning his wheel, winning the jackpot and talking ♥♥♥♥ to him afterwards. He sends you to the Jungle, where this ring can be found in a backpack next to a skeleton.
* [Ring of Regeneration](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Regeneration)[bg3.wiki] - Heals you for 1d4 per turn in combat. This is kind of a "nothing else to use" situation to stack with Balduran's Helm heals if you don't want to use the Risky Ring or Band of the Mystic Scoundrel. You either buy it from Sorcerous Sundries or get it from killing Rolan if you killed Lorroakan before him. We recommend just buying it as soon as you walk in if you want it as it can disappear from the game if Rolan has it.

## Paladin: Act 3 Equipment Loadout Cont.

Act 3 Ranged Weapons

* [Hellrider Longbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHellrider_Longbow)[bg3.wiki] - Bow of Awareness on meth. +3 Initiative and Advantage on Perception checks. Can also possibly inflict Faerie Fire but you won't be shooting this. Buy it from Ferg Drogher in Rivington, near the barn. Don't talk to him with Shadowheart in your party unless she killed the Nightsong or he walks off and takes his toys with him. We know this is a Solo guide but that doesn't mean you can't have companions back at camp, or that you're doing a 4 man party with all 4 builds we've recommended...
* [Gontr Mael](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGontr_Mael)[bg3.wiki] - Possibly cast Guiding Bolt after hitting a target, gives free un-Lethargic-able Haste once per Long Rest and gives Bolt of Celestial Light ability which shoots and arrow that Frightens targets and does an extra 1d4 Radiant Damage for 10 turns afterwards. The ability itself has the Radiant Damage attached to it, which is nice. Oh, and it glows in a 9m radius.   
  You probably won't fire this but Haste IS awesome. Get it from smashing the Steel Watcher Titan in the Foundry.
* [The Dead Shot](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Dead_Shot)[bg3.wiki] - Double your Proficiency Bonus when making an Attack Roll unless you have Disadvantage and reduce the amount you need to Crit by 1. We see this as more of a Crit Reduction item than an actual bow to be fired, really. Buy it from the Stormshore Armoury.

Act 3 Melee Weapons

* [Nylruna](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FNyrulna) [bg3.wiki] - +3 Trident with an extra 1d6 Thunder Damage that gives you Immunity to Fall Damage and +3m to Jump Distance and Movement Speed, glows in a 9m radius and gives access to the Zephyr Flash and Zephyr Break abilities. Zephyr Flash is Charge but looks cooler and possibly causes Bleeding and Zephyr Break is the Gust of Wind spell that deals Thunder Damage. This also does Piercing Damage, so if you want to use the Bhaalist Armour this is what you use it with. Seriously, you thought you did high damage before? Oh, sweet, sweet, naive child... You get this in the Jungle that the Djinn sends you to when you expose him as a cheater. We'd recommend using this as it is very easily accessible and is a fat upgrade to your Halberd of Vigilance damage-wise. Then it's a choice between...
* [Voss's Silver Sword/Silver Sword of the Astral Plane](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FVoss%2527_Silver_Sword)[bg3.wiki] - As we mentioned above, there's no harm in bringing party members along for a solo ride, just don't have them in the party. Lae'zel is worth bringing just to get this. +3 Greatsword that deals 1d6 Psychic and gives Advantage on Intelligence, Wisdom, and 'risma Saving Throws, resistance to Psychic damage and immunity to Charmed... *if* the weilder is a Githyanki. Yes, this old caveat on one of the best weapons in the game. You get Soulbreaker as an ability again though and that doesn't need you to be a Gith, so there's that. You get this from Voss after showing him the Orphic Hammer or by killing him in the sewers.
* [Balduran's Giantslayer](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBalduran%2527s_Giantslayer)[bg3.wiki] - +3 Greatsword that doubles your Strength Modifier (which is +8 using Cloud Giant Elixirs), gives Advantage against Large, Huge or Gargantuan creatures (Netherbrain is classed as Huge, just putting that out there) and you get Topple the Big Folk every Short Rest, which deals additional damage equal to your Proficiency Bonus and Large, Huge or Gargantuan creatures take an additional 2d6 Slashing and have to pass a Strength Saving Throw or be knocked Prone. The ONLY thing wrong with this weapon is you get it for killing Ansur, who is ironically the exact type of target you'd want to use it on. If you don't use Bhaalist Armour, this is the best two-handed melee weapon in the game in our opinion.

Act 3 Shields

* As above, we don't use shields.

## Paladin: End of Act 3 Equipment Loadout

We're here. The Endgame. Since we've given you quite a few options, we'll outline the three main equipment loadouts for full-send on offence, more defence or a nice balance, then show you what we used for the Brain fight.   
  

Offensive Equipment Loadout

[Helmet of Arcane Acuity](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelmet_of_Arcane_Acuity)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Bhaalist Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBhaalist_Armour)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]   
[Craterflesh Gloves](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCraterflesh_Gloves)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]   
[Band of the Mystic Scoundrel](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBand_of_the_Mystic_Scoundrel)[bg3.wiki]  
[Killer's Sweetheart](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FKiller%2527s_Sweetheart)[bg3.wiki]  
[The Dead Shot](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Dead_Shot)[bg3.wiki]  
[Nylruna](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FNyrulna) [bg3.wiki]  
  
  
This build crushes. Run up to something, maul it, ping a Hypnotic Pattern scroll, systematically delete the rest of the enemies while they stand there and eat it. Bosses get hit with the Killer's Sweetheart and usually get 1-turned. Don't really need to say anymore, you've just turned the game into a murder simulator. The only caveat with this is you're looking at 17AC which is *very low* for Act 3, so you probably want to switch between this and the defence build for certain fights, or experiment with whether you really want crit reduction.  
  

Defensive Equipment Loadout

[Helm of Balduran](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelm_of_Balduran)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Armour of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FArmour_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]   
[Legacy of the Masters](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FLegacy_of_the_Masters)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]   
[Ring of Regeneration](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Regeneration)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Gontr Mael](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGontr_Mael)[bg3.wiki]  
[Balduran's Giantslayer](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBalduran%2527s_Giantslayer)[bg3.wiki]  
  
This loadout is for those of us that are confident we can play the game properly, as you will be in combat longer than the above loadout, on average. This build pretty much guarantees you're impossible to kill unless you do something really stupid. Gontr Mael is here so you can Haste yourself to go on the offence but remember, Armour of Persistence basically doubles your health and you get 3-6 HP back per turn, which translates to 6-12 HP. Feel free to keep the Periapt of Wound Closure to guarantee that 6/12 HP. You'll still put out mad damage but you won't be at risk of getting crit, panicking and losing your run.   
  

Balanced Equipment Loadout

[Helm of Balduran](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelm_of_Balduran)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Bhaalist Armour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBhaalist_Armour)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]   
[The Reviving Hands](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Reviving_Hands)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]   
[Ring of Regeneration](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Regeneration)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Gontr Mael](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGontr_Mael)[bg3.wiki]  
[Nylruna](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FNyrulna) [bg3.wiki]  
  
Here you can see we're leveraging defence with the constant regen + Reviving Hands but also retaining the Bhaalist Piercing Vulnerabilty. This is a nice middle-ground as you still get the Piercing Vulnerabilty but you now have 20AC to play with, Blade Ward and the HP regen. 20 AC is still a bit low for Act 3 but it's not scary low.  
  

Brain Fight Equipment Loadout

[Helm of Balduran](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHelm_of_Balduran)[bg3.wiki]  
[Cloak of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCloak_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Armour of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FArmour_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]  
[Amulet of Greater Health](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Greater_Health)[bg3.wiki]   
[Legacy of the Masters](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FLegacy_of_the_Masters)[bg3.wiki]  
[Boots of Persistence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Persistence)[bg3.wiki]   
[Killer's Sweetheart](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FKiller%2527s_Sweetheart)[bg3.wiki]  
[Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki]  
[Gontr Mael](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGontr_Mael)[bg3.wiki]  
[Balduran's Giantslayer](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBalduran%2527s_Giantslayer)[bg3.wiki]  
  
We went into the Brain with this loadout and popped the Gontr Mael Haste once we were inside the final area to utterly maul it. One turn. Seriously. You don't need Bhaalist Armour but it's so much fun.   
  
Play around with your loadouts once you're at this stage in the game as there are plenty of combats you can try builds out with before heading to a boss. You've made it this far, the rest is easy as long as you remain patient and respect your enemies. On to Bladesinger, which is more broken than this, believe it or not...

## Bladesinger: Origin

Here it is. Sword Opera.   
Just as heads-up, *this is hands-down the most broken class in the entire game.* We're genuinely mystified as to why nobody in the community is talking about how utterly broken this class is. You'll see what we mean and we recommend everyone give Bladesinger a try if they're attempting a solo.  
  
Please note some of these entries will be repeated text from up above as we're only human and this amount of writing takes time, so we want to save time where possible. We will also direct you to other sections if the information there will cover something important, as we do have character limits.   
Probably worth mentioning, [here](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pG2koW266to&list=PLoFNCnd-Eom_3_TtO56PmQEXr7Z5velBA&index=21) is our Bladesinger Solo Guide starting at Ethel. Some of the content in the video might be different from what's written with regards to gear and strats; we are two people and we play a little differently but know that whichever route you take will still do the same thing fundamentally, and that thing is "laugh at the game while you trivialise it."  
Anyways, onwards!  
  

The Dark Urge

The Dark Urge gets the Deathstalker Mantle. We don't believe the Mantle is endgame or Best in Slot (BiS), it just makes fights before lvl 5 easier/less risky.  
Medicine Proficiency is whatever, Intimidation can come in handy.   
Slayer Form is ok but in our opinion should be better, considering what you sacrifice to get it. Saving Last Light is almost mandatory as Dammon sells some of the best gear in the game, but there are options in case the Isobel fight doesn't go your way.  
Another reason to pick Durge is to have a 1v1 vs Orin in Act 3, making that fight substantially easier. Top tip, Orin's Slayer Form isn't a Wildshape, so when it gets to 0 health she dies; another reason to save Last Light as, if you have the Slayer Form, she doesn't.   
Finally, and this is the most important reason to pick Durge: walking around kicking squirrels and carving up Bards is just good fun.  
  

Astarion

The main reason you take Astarion is his "Happy" condition after a bite, which gives you +1 to Attack Rolls, Saving Throws and some Ability Checks. As a Bladesinger, you'll be in melee range often so you will be able to consistently get the buffs. As Bite is a Bonus Action, you won't have to worry overmuch about using it in combat or outside of it, but it will stop you from using a Bladesong Climax charge that turn. This is minor at worst though. Eewww nuuu, we have more healing charges next turn... what a shame... anyway...   
It's worth mentioning that Astarion gets a +1d10 (sweet) Necrotic bonus added to his Weapon and Unarmed Attacks if he Ascends, but you will only get to enjoy this in Act 3. He is also an Elf, so he'll get Perception Proficiency, Darkvision & the Charmed Advantage & Magic Sleep Immunity.  
  

Gale

Gale is Human, but you don't play him for that. He gets a free lvl 3 Spellslot in Act 2 after wrecking the Thorm siblings, gets the Ring of Evasion (use your Reaction to pass a Dex Saving Throw if you fail it) and can get Anointed in Splendour for free if he chooses to kiss and make up with Mystra. Not bad, but genuinely not *that* great either. He's a decent choice here as you'll get another lvl 3 Fireball, Haste, Hypnotic Pattern, Lightning Bolt or Counterspell, which is always gonna help, but the tradeoff is you have to actually play as Gale.  
  

Karlach

Skip her for this otherwise you can't get the Infernal Robe.  
  

Lae'zel

Lae'zel is a Githyanki and comes with all of the bonuses they get. She's also starting out as a Fighter. Realistically, she's the same as Shart: unless you want story flavour, just pick a Tav Githyanki.   
  

Shadowheart

Shadowheart is a High Half-Elf (which, fun fact, is why her Firebolt cantrip sucks: it scales off her INT and not her WIS) so you can have some story flavour if you want to pick Half-Elf, but that is genuinely about it.  
  

Wyll

Wyll gives you access to some nice Warlock gear but other than that, also a Human. Snore. You'd probably be better off picking a Tav and grabbing him as a companion (not in your party, just at camp... and not casting buffs on you) to get the gear but realistically you're not gonna use the robe or the Infernal Rapier later on as you'll be using Strength Elixirs therefore there will be better Off-Hand weapons. Still, its stuff to sell.

## Bladesinger: Durge/Tav Race

For all Durge and Tav lovers, we'll list all of the races in the order we feel they suit the playstyle best. For Tav specifically, we recommend choosing Guild Artisan as your background, but it's up to you.  
  

Half-Orc

Half-Orc is a melee race as it comes with the Savage Attacks Passive (not to be confused with Savage Attack*er*) which means you roll an extra Damage Dice on Crits. In English, say a normal roll is 1d6. Crit means 2d6. Half-Orc Crit means 3d6. Very tidy. Note we said "normal" damage dice. In Honour Mode, this *does not include smites or sneak attacks*. A Half-Orc wizard is not only hilariously going against the norm, it's also probably the best version of Bladesinger you can get since you get Booming Blade as one of your starting cantrips, nullifying the need to go High Elf. We did play as High Elf though cos you know, we like moving around a lot.  
It also comes with a sweet ability called Relentless Endurance that improves your survivability drastically. When you die as a Half-Orc, once per Long Rest, you are *insta-rezzed with 1 HP.* Yeah, you read that right. We recommend playing like you don't have a safety net though, as this drastically increases your odds of never having to use it.   
**Note:** This is more for saving you against deaths outside of combat, as in combat it is likely you simply survive one more hit than you normally would and will die anyway, depending on your turn order. It won't help against chasms or falls either. Still, not getting completely bodied and losing your run to a trap is nice.  
  

Elf

Unless you're using a non-melee class or a class that doesn't get Booming Blade at lvl 1, go High Elf. Booming Blade is too good, as you will discover later. Fey Ancestry, Darkvision and Perception Proficiency are way more valuable in a Solo than in a party too, they just don't get Savage Attacks which is really good.  
  

Half-Elf

Half-Elf is the same as Elf except it doesn't get the Perception Proficiency and instead gets Shield Proficiency. Your issue here is the same as full Elf in which the main reason this Race is now Top-Tier is nullified by the fact we're taking Wizard as a class.  
  

Githyanki

Githyanki get Astral Knowledge, Mage Hand and free Enhanced Leap & Misty Step per Long Rest. The Mage Hand in particular can be used as a source of Wet, Haste Spores or just another target on the field that can throw stuff for you, which is pretty valuable when you only have one character. They don't get that extra movement distance or Proficiencies, though.   
Additionally, while there is a lot of equipment that have special effects that only proc when you're a Gith, you *can use the [Mask of the Shapeshifter](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FMask_of_the_Shapeshifter)[bg3.wiki] or a Disguise Spell/Ability to proc them anyway.* Make sure to equip the item after you transform or it won't register your bonus effects properly. The Soulbreaker Greatsword is a nice little weapon to have for a Paladin but genuinely, as MotSS keeps your shapeshifted after you take it off, choosing Half-Orc or Elf is just Gith with extra steps.  
  

Drow

Basically a Wood Elf without the Stealth Proficiency and Movement Speed. They are also objectively cooler. Weapon Proficiencies don't matter as we'll be covering all of that with our class, and a free Faerie Fire (lvl 3) & Darkness (lvl 5) cast can come in handy but it's never going to be game-changing. You also don't have to Shapeshift in Act 1 at all, which is convenient but again, not game-changing.   
  

Halfling

Halflings come with the Lucky & Brave features, which let you re-roll a 1 at will and give you Advantage on Fear checks, respectively. You can also choose between having Advantage on Stealth Checks or Advantage on Saving Throws against Poison and Resistance to Poison Damage. These are fairly handy features, but the drawback is you move 1.5m less than your long-legged counterparts and again, don't get relevant Proficiencies.  
  

Dwarf

Dwarven Resilience, which is Advantage against being Poisoned and Resistance to Poison Damage. Gold Dwarf is a good shout for extra HP, especially in the early acts, and Duergar for the Invis and Advantage against Illusions, Charm and Paralysis is also good. Honestly, you don't need the Invis and the Advantage on Paralysis Saves is probably a waste; if you ever need to use it, you've made a mistake by allowing whatever it is to attempt to Paralyse you in the first place. The Enlarge cast is also good but situational and it only lasts for 10 turns. You also have less Movement Speed than the long-legged races.  
  

Tiefling

Tieflings are cool but all they're really offering consistently is Resistance to Fire. The 3 subraces all have some spells that you *might* use but they either have Charisma or Constitution as their modifiers. Mephistopheles Tieflings would probably be our pick for Mage Hand, closely followed by Asmodeus for the Hellish Rebuke with Zariel coming in last with Thaumaturgy. As for their other spells: Produce Flame sucks, Darkness is fine, Flame Blade & Burning Hands most likely won't be used and Searing Smite is... well... you should know our opinions on this from our info on Karlach. We don't think it's worth it, to be honest.   
  

Gnome

Advantage on Wisdom, Charisma and Intelligence Saving Throws. The Sub-Race bonuses are all meh here for Paladin too, as doubling your History Proficiency Bonus, Speak with Animals or Advantage on Stealth Checks are either pointless or situational. They have nice saving buffs but like Duergar, if you're utilising them it means you've played too risky or made a mistake, and you're also nerfed in movement. There's still better options above, truthfully.  
  

Human

Shield Proficiency and an extra Proficiency, with extra Carry Weight. Meh. Next.   
  

Dragonborn

Oh, this is what's next? Ouch...  
Objectively the coolest-looking race and also objectively the worst to play as; you're nerfing yourself for zero reason as these guys literally have nothing going for them, not even Darkvision. Don't pick Dragonborn unless you plan on impressing your enemies to death with your sick horns and tail. Let us know how it goes in the comments.   
  
Our run was done with a High Elf but as we have a class that offsets the Elven advantage of having Booming Blade from lvl 1, gives you the extra Booming Blade damage at Lvl 5 *and* the extra crit die, we believe Half-Orc to be the optimum and therefore "Best" pick for pretty much any melee-heavy character.

## Bladesinger: Stats

Here are the stat spreads you want for your Bladesinger. We have broken them down with optimum switcheroos in mind, but it is completely up to you how much of our advice you take.   
  

Character Creation Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 16  
Con = 14  
Int = 16  
Wis = 12  
Cha = 8  
  
This is your spread at character creation. You can play with Dex and Con depending on if you want more health or extra AC; we'd rather not get hit than have more health for when we do and stacking AC as a Bladesinger is fairly easy even from the start. You can also drop Wisdom for 'risma if you want the extra Speech boost or even them out for no penalties but remember your Wisdom is what stops Hold Person.  
  
Strength 8 is because we're going to dump Strength, as you will be using Hill Giant Elixirs and, when you unlock them, Cloud Giant Elixirs.   
Dex 16 is because we will be using robes in order to use Bladesong therefore we want as much AC as possible.  
Con 14 gives you respectable health and solid +2 to Con saves.  
Int 16 is because it is our spellcasting stat. Contrary to every other class in the game, you're now really smart. Try not to be a know-it-all though, especially when you're talking to Magic Mirrors...  
Wis 12 is where we choose to put our remaining points and is why we recommend Wood Elf as joint second for your selections, because that Perception Proficiency turns this +1 into a +3.  
Cha 8 is because as we said, we personally value Wisdom more than 'risma due to it being your Hold Person defence. The Silver Necklace can help you with important speech checks but usually your Int can help you out with Wizard-specific dialogue options.  
  

Auntie Ethel Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
Con = 14  
Int = 16/18 (if you choose an ASI)  
Wis = 10  
Cha = 8  
  
You would think we'd go Int since we're using Strength Elixirs for our melee prowess, but we went with more Dex as we want more AC over spellcasting power. We grabbed an ASI at lvl 4 to increase our Int anyway and as a rule, attempting Ethel below Lvl 5 isn't a good idea so we can reliably say that we'll have an ASI at this point if we so choose (more infor in the next section). Wisdom needs to go down to 10 in order to compensate for the extra point wherever you choose though. It is what it is. You *can* swap out some Con if 10 Wisdom makes you uncomfortable and as well it should: you're going to be fairly vulnerable to being Held until you get items to mitigate it and your Zai'thisk checks are going to solely rely on Bliss Spores. It's up to you.   
  

Amulet of Greater Health Stat Spread

Str = 8   
Dex = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
Con = 8  
Int = 18  
Wis = 14 - 10  
Cha = 14 - 10  
  
The Amulet of Greater Health allows you to dump Con & Str, which gives you more room for Wisdom. Bear in mind that you won't see this spread until Act 3 and by then, in our experience, you're not really going to be needing it for many checks and you'll have managed so far without it, so it's up to you. You're basically able to distribute your points between Widsom and 'risma as you see fit here, because you're not gonna be touching your Int or Dex, so this is really whatever you choose.   
  
*These next two stat spreads are pretty extra; you can stop here and skip to the build section if you want. For you fellow Turbo-Nerds, read on.*   
  

Araj + Astarions Strength Potion

Str = 10  
Dex = 14  
Con = 16  
Int = 8   
Wis = 10  
Cha = 17 +1 (Hags Hair)  
  
If you make Astarion bite Araj in Act 2, you will get a potion that permanently increases your Strength by +2. "Aren't we using Strength Elixirs?" we hear you cry. Yes, but there is a reason for gaining these extra 2 points and they are not mandatory.  
  

[Mirror of Loss](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FMirror_of_Loss)[bg3.wiki]

Str = 8  
Dex = 19 +1 (Hags Hair)  
Con = 14  
Int = 18  
Wis = 10  
Cha = 8  
  
We can afford to play with Str now since we are offsetting it with the potion. This will be your endgame stat spread; you won't enjoy these stats until Act 3 but be mindful that you may not get the stat points in Dex (read the wiki page on how the Mirror works.) You can add the Amulet of Greater Health to dump your Con and play with Wisdom and 'risma but you will have some leftover points that you waste. Which is fine. Again, we wanted more AC so went for Dex but feel free to max out your Int instead, although at this point in the game you'll have the Gloves of Battlemage's Power which makes your Int "how many hits until spells are at 95% chance to Hit" rather than "I need this as high as possible to have the best chance of spells landing." More on that in a moment too.   
  
It is worth mentioning that this is min/max'ing and you can function on 18 Dex & Int for the entirety of the run with no issues.

## Bladesinger: Builds and Multiclassing

It's here and it's clear: if you don't like Bladesinger or don't find it to be laughably broken, you're doing it wrong. This is one of those times where we're not gonna budge on our viewpoint and we don't mean it as a bad thing, it's just a fact. Up there in the guide we say something along the lines of "Gloom Stalker isn't an instant win button" but Bladesinger actually is. Well, more accurately, there are three buttons you press to win but we'll tell you what those buttons are. Read on to unlock the full potential of the class and enjoy easy Solo runs.   
  

Bladesinger Build Breakdown

  
We'll be going for Bladesinger 12 as we did a pure Bladesinger run as part of a challenge and in the process discovered it's broken. It has literally everything you need and more. Check the FAQ for more build exploration but it basically boils down to Bladesinger 10 Paladin 2 and you pretty much don't even need Paladin 2.  
  
**Lvl 1: Bladesinger Wizard** Take Wizard as your starting class and grab Booming Blade, Minor Illusion and Toll the Dead.   
As mentioned above, Booming Blade is a melee cantrip that causes opponents to take 1d8 Thunder Damage when they move. Kinda whatever. Just wait though, like Eldritch Blast, it gets better per character level, not class level. Still, you should always use this instead of your regular attack, as it is especially useful against Gobbo Archers and their tendency to Disengage and run away to shoot you as well as any enemy you can manage to Shove away from you after hitting it.  
Minor Illusion is for Grym easy-mode and grouping things up or pulling away stragglers. Toll the Dead is a very good new cantrip in Patch 8 which is going to be your main source of Ranged damage without costing you a spellslot; it is a Wisdom Saving Throw that causes 1d8 Necrotic or 1d12 if the opponent has health missing.   
  
As for spells, you wanna take Mage Armour, Shield, Magic Missile, Longstrider, Enhanced Leap, Feather Fall, Thunder Wave and Chromatic Orb. You wanna have Mage Armour, Shield, Longstrider and either Magic Missile or Thunder Wave for the Nautiloid. Get Mage Armour cast immediately which will put you at a respectable 16 AC at Lvl 1, then get yourself buffed with Longstrider.   
  
You also get an Arcane Recovery Charge which means you get to restore a spell slot outside of combat; this effectively makes your Mage Armour a free cast when you compare other casters as you can use it after that to get your slot back and cast two offensive spells. Nice.  
  
Choose Insight and Investigation as your Proficiencies, or whatever you like.  
  
**Lvl 2: Bladesinger Wizard** is where you get to take two more spells and memorise another one. Grab whatever spells you want and then get Enhanced Leap added to your memorised list. Use this before combat to get around.   
You also get to pick your subclass and we will be going for Bladesinger (shock). Pick your weapon: it doesn't matter as you now have Proficiency with all of them and next level you're gonna be using something way better anyway but you *should* be dual-wielding a combo of shortswords and scimitars at this stage. You also get Performance Proficiency which is cool. Kinda. Whatever. Moving on...  
  
Bladesingers whole thing is activating Bladesong in fights. You start with 2 charges that increase as you level up. It's free to activate in combat but make sure you activate it at the start of combats you want to use it in because Bladesong lasts for 10 turns and while it's active, every time you cast a Spell you get a Bladesong Damage Charge and every time you make a melee attack you get a Bladesong Healing Charge. You can use Bladesong Climax as a Bonus Action which is a Jump (so Enhanced Leap increases the range of it!) which deals 1d6 Force damage to enemies in the radius and heals you (and allies but we're solo so redundant here) for 1d6. Bladesong Climax also cashes in all of your charges and for each charge the amount of Force damage you deal and the amount you are healed goes up by 1d6. You can, therefore, passively accumulate a Bonus Action heal that deals damage to enemies while you wail on them with melee weapons OR sling spells at stuff til you want to drop in with Force Damage AoE then start swingin'.   
While Bladesong is active, you also get (are you ready?) +2 AC, +3m to Movement Speed, Advantage in Acrobatics and +2 to Constitution Saving Throws. It's such a powerful ability and it only gets stronger as you level up. In really spicy fights this early, popping Bladesong and a Haste Potion gives you 20 AC. 20. Utter Madness.   
  
**Lvl 3: Bladesinger Wizard** brings us to the crux of the class and you're like, half online with this build at this point. Pick Magic Weapon and Shadow Blade as your two new Level 2 spells and remove your offensive spell from your bar which should be Magic Missile or Thunder Wave; your bar should now be Mage Armour, Shield, Longstrider, Enhanced Leap, Magic Weapon and Shadow Blade. You may also take Cloud of Daggers instead of anything else if you want to use it in combination with the many Grease scrolls and bottles you'll get in Act 1 as this combo is OP but whatever you choose, Shadowblade is mandatory because...  
  
Shadow Blade is disgusting. You conjure a blade made of shadow (no way) into your hand that deals 2d8 Psychic Damage *and has Advantage against Lightly or Heavily Obscured Targets.* Outside and target is in the shade? Lightly Obscured, Advantage. The entire Grymforge (mostly)? Advantage. Act 2? Advantage. You're also using Strength Elixirs so you're operating at 21 Strength, which means you're already walking around with a good chance of hitting stuff on top of the Advantage.  
Lets put Shadow Blade damage into perspective for you too: right now your most damaging melee options are Two-Handed and we'll use the ones we recommend for Paladin as examples. Sword of Justice/Svartlebees both do 2d6 +1 + Str so they're doing 8-18 Slashing damage. Everburn Blade is doing 2d6 + Str + 1d4 Fire, which is doing 8-17 Slashing + 1d4 Fire, so 8-21 total. Shadow Blade is doing 2d8 + Str so it's doing 7 - 21 Psychic Damage *as a one-handed weapon*. You can have a whole other weapon in your other hand while dealing more/equal damage than the three best melee weapons available to you right now.   
Coupled with Booming Blade and your correct proc'cing of its effect, you're doing 3d8 + 5 to targets, of damage types that are rarely resisted throughout the entire game. 3d8 + 5. At Level 3. Do you understand how much damage that is? Yes you can of course use Booming Blade (and should be) as a Paladin wielding those Two-Handed weapons but remember, Shadow Blade is a One-Handed weapon doing the same damage. Dis-*gust*-ing. And this is level 3. It gets better as you level up! Speaking of levelling up...  
  
**Lvl 4: Bladesinger Wizard** gives us our Feat, another level 2 spellslot and another cantrip. For Feats we took Alert for the bonus Initiative to make sure we we're going first in combat. We took Featherfall but Thunder Wave to get our Booming Blade procs ia a good shout if you have potions. For your cantrip grab Firebolt for the utility and another option of damage type vs Toll the Dead but it's really up to you. One more level to go and you come online properly...  
  
**Lvl 5: Bladesinger Wizard** is where arrogance can creep in, because you get a Level 3 spellslot, another Bladesong charge and +3 to your AC + Con while using it. Your level 3 spellslot means upcasted Shadow Blade which is now doing 3d8 Psychic Damage. Your Booming Blade is now also doing 1d8 Thunder on hit and 2d8 Thunder if the target moves. It's getting silly already, because you're now doing up to 6d8 dmg to stuff when you proc Booming Blade properly, not including whatever you land on your offhand attack. Also worth remembering, this is without equipment. The Underdark should be coming up now too. Advantage anyone?

## Bladesinger: Builds and Multiclassing cont.

**Lvl 6: Bladesinger Wizard** is where you catch up with the melee classes and gain your Extra Attack. On average, with Cull the Weak, stuff with =<40HP is dead in a turn before your Booming Blade movement damage procs. You also get yourself some more spellslots and some more spells. Counterspell is mandatory then you can grab Fireball, Hypnotic Pattern, Haste (if you're confident) Glyph of Warding, Lightning Bolt or Slow.   
A note about Booming Blade here: you can only use it once per Action per round, so you can't use it with your Extra Attack if you used it on the first swing but you can if you opened up with a normal swing. Additionally, if you pop a Haste Potion (or cast it) you can use it, then normal attack, then use it again on your second Action. You can bypass this restriction in the first round of combat due to how combat works; as you make the first Booming Blade strike outside of combat, when combat starts it's a separate "round" so you can hit them with it twice. You should by now know to prioritise threats but definitely do this on scary things. Unfortunately, the Booming Blade condition is overridden and doesn't proc twice but still, you're gaining an extra 1d8 and later on 2d8 per opening round on some targets by remembering this and planning accordingly.  
  
**Lvl 7: Bladesinger Wizard** means more spells and a 4th Level spell slot alongside another Bladesong charge. Don't use this to cast Shadow Blade as it has no effect on your damage. We recommend Wall of Fire or Ice Storm.  
  
**Lvl 8: Bladesinger Wizard** contains your next Feat and more spellslots. For your Feat take Savage Attacker to get those rerolls on your damage; this effectively reduces the range of your damage with Shadowblade on average; while this is gnarly and could be taken earlier, Alert first means you don't have to worry about going first which is way more important. You could also take War Caster or an ASI to up your Int or your Dex, depending on whether you want more AC or more spells memorised. Worth mentioning at this point you'll be in Act 2 which means you'll have access to the Helm of Arcane Acuity and the Gloves of Battlemage's Power which will both give you Arcane Acuity, so your Int being high is now no longer a concern unless you want more memorised spells.  
  
**Lvl 9: Bladesinger Wizard** is yet another jump in power as we get access to a 5th level spellslot which means Shadow Blade is now doing 4d8 *and* our Bladesong gives us +4 AC + Con. You also get to pick a spicy spell; Hold Monster, Cone of Cold and Cloudkill are our recommendations, with Cloudkill being the most efficient use of your spellslot but perhaps not your actions... up to you. You also get another Bladesong Power charge for a total of four casts per Long Rest.  
  
**Lvl 10: Bladesinger Wizard** grants you another lvl 5 spellslot, an additional cantrip and Song of Defence, which allows you to spend spellslots to reduce incoming damage by 5 dmg per spellslot spent. Handy to have but not majorly game-breaking. Pick whatever cantrip you want and whatever spells you want.  
  
**Lvl 11: Bladesinger Wizard** ups your Booming Blade to 2d8 Thunder on hit and 3d8 when the target moves and gives you more spell slots and your first lvl 6 spell.   
You're now landing 9d8 when you properly proc Booming Blade. That's one less damage dice than Chain Lightning, one more and +2 better damage dice than Circle of Death, 3d8 more than Sunbeam and 12 damage more than Otiluke's Freezing Sphere from a one-handed attack. Let that sink in for a moment... 9d8. Absolutely rank levels of damage.   
It's at this point where you can choose to grab Paladin for your final two levels but if you wanna go for your lvl 6 spell slots you absolutely can; it's really a matter of preference.   
  
**Lvl 12: Bladesinger Wizard** if your final level and Feat. We took another ASI to put our Int to 20 to have another spell memorised but you can take whatever you'd like. War Caster, Spell Sniper or Lucky could be good calls but don't take Dual Wielder as you should, once you read our equipment section, already have or be going to get a certain weapon that makes this kind of redundant unless you want to wear armour and really want the +1 AC or don't want to use your Bonus Action for actually fighting with.  
  
That's it! As is the case with all classes, stuff starts to taper off towards the back end of levels for most classes but extra d8's on your stuff is always nice. The equipment section will refine the raw power of this build into a flexible killing machine.   
  
On to equipment!

## Bladesinger: Act 1 Equipment Loadout

Gimme the Good Stuff

We'll go slot by slot, act by act. We'll list the order of items that you want to show your progression and tell you why it's good. If you just want to know what we wore at the end of each milestone/what we consider to be BiS, skip ahead to the "End of Act X" sections.  

Act 1 Helmets

* [Haste Helm](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHaste_Helm)[bg3.wiki] - Momentum for 3 turns at the start of combat. Bladesong activation and this give you +6m of Movement. With Enhanced Leap, you shouldn't have any issues traversing the battlefield. You find this in the chest next to the fish wagon in the Blighted Village.

Act 1 Cloaks

* [The Deathstalker Mantle](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Deathstalker_Mantle)[bg3.wiki] - We don't need to repeat ourselves, if you wanna use this you do you but it's a crutch and very hard one to wean off of, in our opinion.

Act 1 Chest Pieces

* [Infernal Robe](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FInfernal_Robe)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Creche. +1 AC and lets you cast Fire Shield as a Bonus Action once per Long Rest. Fire Shield is whatever, it's the AC we want. You get these for Killing Karlach with Wyll in your party then Long Resting. For those of you that are reluctant to murder her, let us tempt you further: killing her and giving her head to the "Paladins of Tyr" will make the scary one give you his sword and, as long as you don't stop him from reaching you, he'll fight you Unarmed. She's also mid as a character, there we said it. Now there's *three* reasons.

Act 1 Gloves

* [Gloves of Power](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Power)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Sleight of Hand. They're better than nothing, but the Absolute's Bane doesn't proc unless you have the Absolute's Brand and by the time you get that you'll have better gloves. Loot them from Zak'rug after the fight in front of the Grove.
* [Bracers of Defence](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBracers_of_Defence)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 2, possibly Act 3 depending on your choices. +2AC. With our stat set up and your Infernal Robe, Mage Armour and Bladesong you're sitting on 21 AC, which is wild. You get these from a chest inside the Cellar in the Blighted Village, where the skeleton coffins are. You don't have to trigger the skeletons to get these.

Act 1 Boots

* [Swiresy Shoes](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSwiresy_Shoes)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Acrobatics and increased Jump distance of 1.5m. Buy them from Grat the Trader.
* [Boots of Aid and Comfort](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Aid_and_Comfort)[bg3.wiki] - Gives 3 Temporary HP to things you heal, including you. Coupled with The Whispering Promise, you'll be getting this HP a lot. Wearing these means that each potion is guaranteed to be at least 4HP minimum, and a Rest counts as healing so you'll start your next morning after a Long Rest with the Temp HP that you can top up when you're proccing your Bless. You also buy these from Grat the Trader.
* [Boots of Speed](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Speed)[bg3.wiki] - Probably BiS until Act 2. Gives you a bootleg version of Cunning Action: Dash. On top of all your other Movement abilities this might seem overkill but genuinely, these help so much when you need to guarantee you're out of range. Get these from Thulla in the Myconid Colony. Be nice to her and give her an antidote, she'll give you these boots.
* [Boots of Stormy Clamour](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBoots_of_Stormy_Clamour)[bg3.wiki] - Nice to have as Booming Blade is a condition and when you inflict a condition on a target it causes Reverberation. Kind of works against you though since Reverb has a chance to knock the target Prone, which is the opposite of what you want when they have Booming Blade on them but for AoE spells they can be real nice as the finishing touch of your CC plans. Buy them from Omeluum after completing his quest.
* [Disintegrating Night Walkers](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDisintegrating_Night_Walkers)[bg3.wiki] - Free Misty Step, you can't slip on Ice or Grease and cannot be Ensnared, Enwebbed or Entangled. They're ok. You get these from killing Nere.

Act 1 Amulets

* [Silver Pendant](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSilver_Pendant)[bg3.wiki] - Guidance for free. We argue this is BiS until you get to the creche, but be aware it is a Concentration spell which can override your Hunters' Mark. Find this on a plateau outside the Grove, should be on your left as you head west towards the Blighted Village. Check the wiki link for more detailed directions.
* [Amulet of Misty Step](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_Misty_Step)[bg3.wiki] - Free Misty Step cast per Short Rest. It's Misty Step, what's not to like? You get this from a chest in Gut's Private Quarters, just don't let Paloma catch you. Or, you know, kill her first. That works too.
* [The Ever-Seeing Eye](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Ever-Seeing_Eye) [bg3.wiki]- Free Protection from Good and Evil per Short Rest. It also stays on when you remove the amulet, so that's nice. Grab this from Ethel's back room. Honestly, this is more prep for Act 2 than something you wanna use now.
* [Amulet of the Unworthy](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAmulet_of_the_Unworthy)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to Slashing damage and Vulnerability to Bludgeoning damage. This is slept on as it *sounds* trash but when you can guarantee something isn't going to do Bludgeoning damage (inside Myrkuls arena, for example) it's really nice to have. We grab this for Myrkul later on. You grab it from... I can't believe my eyes... A Minotaur... In the Underdark near the Selunite Outpost.

Act 1 Rings

* [The Whispering Promise](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Whispering_Promise)[bg3.wiki] - Bless for 2 turns after you heal. This is BiS until Act 3, hands-down. Bless is a flat 10% to your Hit Chance and 1d4 to your saving throws. We won't bang on about how good this is more than we already have. Buy this from Volo in the Grove after you pass the three Druids forming the roadblock.
* [Crusher's Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FCrusher%2527s_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - A handy little ring that increases you movement speed by 3m. Until you get the Ring of Protection, this and TWP are your go-tos. You get this after killing Crusher in the Gobbo Camp Exterior.
* [Ring of Protection](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Protection)[bg3.wiki] - Arguably BiS along with The Whispering Promise until the back end of Act 2/Act 3. +1 AC & +1 to Saving Throws. You get this from stealing the Idol of Silvanus for Mol in the Grove. ***You must make sure to get the quest from Mol BEFORE you rescue Halsin.***
* [Ring of Poison Resistance](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Poison_Resistance)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to being Poisoned and Poison Damage (these aren't the same thing). Situational but it comes in handy in those situations. Auntie Ethel and the Phase Spider Matriarch are both good places to use this. You get it from a Sarcophagus outside of the Worg Pens in the Shattered Sanctum.
* [The Sparkswall](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Sparkswall)[bg3.wiki] - Resistance to Lightning damage and you can't be Electrocuted. A staple of Lightning Charge builds because of those stupid boots that electrify water, we want this for fighting Bernard and Ansur later on. You get this from a chest in the Basement of the Arcane Tower in the Underdark. Check our tips section to find out how to get here without using Guiding Light.

## Bladesinger: Act 1 Equipment Loadout Cont.

Act 1 Ranged Weapons

* [Hunting Shortbow](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHunting_Shortbow)[bg3.wiki] - Advantage on Monstrosities. This is a stat stick and so you can shoot levers and stuff from a distance. Or, you know, you can shoot enemies with it too: you do you. Buy this from Dammon in the Grove.
* [Bow of Awareness](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FBow_of_Awareness)[bg3.wiki] - +1 Initiative. As above, stat stick and utility tool. Buy it from Roah Moonglow in the Shattered Sanctum.

Act 1 Melee Weapons

* You can use whatever you want to use until you unlock Shadow Blade at lvl 3; we highly recommend using Shadow Blade. Any weapon listed here will mostly be a stat stick or something you can use for an extra bit of damage. Remember, using anything you aren't Proficient in means you won't be able to use Bladesinging. We won't include Rapiers or Longswords as they will need to be in your Main Hand (which is where Shadow Blade lives) and we don't want to take Dual-Wielder as a Feat. Having said that, here's our picks for off-hand weapons:
* [Steelforged Sword](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSteelforged_Sword)[bg3.wiki] - +1 shortsword. Better than a normal shortsword. Find this in the forge where you make the Sussur weapon: it's up a ladder in a chest.
* [Speedy Reply](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSpeedy_Reply) [bg3.wiki]- Scimitar that gives you Momentum when you hit something with it. This is nicer than you think as it's basically bootleg Click Heels with extra steps. You get this from a dead caravan agent's body near the gnoll cave where the Flind is.
* [Worgfang](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FWorgfang) [bg3.wiki]- Goblins have Disadvantage on Attack Rolls against the wielder. Really slept on little dagger this, it's worth rushing it when you get to the Shattered Sanctum before you fight the place or do the Grove fight as the Disadvantage is really tidy. Once you're heading into the Underdark or the Mountain Pass it's completely useless though. You get this from a skeleton inside the cage with the 2 Worgs in the room where you rescue Halsin.
* [Hunter's Dagger](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FHunter%2527s_Dagger) [bg3.wiki]- Rupture a target for 3 turns on hit. Rupture causes the target to take 1d4 Piercing for every 1m they move, reducing the condition duration by 1 each time. Works nicely with Booming Blade damage. You buy this from Roah Moonglow in the Shattered Sanctum.
* [Ritual Dagger](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRitual_Dagger)[bg3.wiki] - we mention this one in our Gloomstalker section and it was pointed out to us in the comments; the same stuff stands here. For those that don't know, this dagger gives you Bless every time you damage something with it or use the Bonus Action ability it gives you. Since we highly recommend The Whispering Promise and stocking up on Light Healing Potions, it's worth noting the Bless from this and TWP are the same and don't stack. Unless you *really* want to wear another ring, you kind of don't need this dagger. You get it from Abdirak's table in the Shattered Sanctum.
* [Shortsword of First Blood](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FShortsword_of_First_Blood)[bg3.wiki] - Deal an additional 1d8 dmg to targets that have all their HP. Use this to open with against full HP enemies then follow up with your Booming Blade otherwise use something else. You get this from a Deep Gnome corpse at the entrance to the Decrepit Village (where the Duergar you need to kill for Spaw live).
* [Sussur Dagger](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FSussur_Dagger)[bg3.wiki] - You can also use the Sickle but we're not doubling up on entries, plus we like the option of being able to throw the dagger. +1 Dagger and any target struck must succeed a DC 12 Constitution Saving Throw or become Silenced. Really nice against certain enemies but not game-breaking. You forge this with the Sussur Bark and a Dagger (check the wiki for more details).
* [Adamantine Scimitar](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FAdamantine_Scimitar) [bg3.wiki]- +1 Scimitar that ignores Resistance to Slashing Damage and auto-crits objects. It's alright. You use the Adamantine Forge to get this and since you can't use Shields or Armour, you might as well give this a go.

Act 1 Shields

* You won't be using a shield because you can't use Armour and Sword Opera.

## Bladesinger: Act 1.5 Equipment Loadout

Continuing our loadout, here are the items you will find in the Mountain Pass/Githyanki Creche, followed by what we were wearing as we headed into Act 2.  

Act 1.5 Helmets

* [Diadem of Arcane Synergy](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FDiadem_of_Arcane_Synergy)[bg3.wiki] - This adds your Spellcasting Modifier to your next attack when you inflict a condition. Your INT is at least 16, which means you're doing at least +3 Psychic Damage on the next hit after you cast Booming Blade, which does add up. That's potentially 3-9 damage per turn depending on if you're using Reverb gear or have Illithid Ability Drain unlocked. You get this by killing Ardent Jhe'rezath in the W'wargaz fight.

Act 1.5 Cloaks

* Unless you have the Deathstalker Mantle, there aren't any cloaks here.

Act 1.5 Chest Pieces

* [The Graceful Cloth](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FThe_Graceful_Cloth)[bg3.wiki] - +2 Dex, +1 to Dex Saving Throws, Advantage on Dex Saving Throws, take half Falling damage and Jump 1.5m further. +2 Dex means we don't lose any AC from switching out the Infernal Robe so really down to Jump Distance and Saving Throws vs a cast of Fire Shield. You buy this from Lady Esther on the Rosymorn Trail.

Act 1.5 Gloves

* [Gloves of Belligerent Skies](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FGloves_of_Belligerent_Skies)[bg3.wiki] - Inflicts Reverberation against a target when you deal Thunder, Lightning or Radiant damage. This can proc with your Booming Blade. We talked about Reverb conflicting with Booming Blade and we kept the Bracers of Defence but these can give you some nice CC. You find these in a chest in Inquisitor W'wargaz' room.

Act 1.5 Boots

* Amazingly, nothing here better than the Boots of Speed, or whatever your chosen footwear may be.

Act 1.5 Amulets

* [Periapt of Wound Closure](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FPeriapt_of_Wound_Closure)[bg3.wiki] - BiS until Act 3, and even then it's a toss-up. This amulet stabilises you at the start of your next turn, which is useless as the game ends before that can happen, but it also *gives you the maximum amount of HP possible from any source of healing.* Greater Healing Potions are now a flat 20HP. Superiors are 40HP. Your cheeky Healing Potions to proc Bless before every fight are now a flat 10HP, which will keep you topped up easily. You NEED this guarantee in a fight to know where you stand, where you can take risks and where you need to bail. We cannot stress enough how important removing the randomness from healing potions is. You buy this from Lady Esther.

Act 1.5 Rings

* [Strange Conduit Ring](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FStrange_Conduit_Ring)[bg3.wiki] - +1d4 Psychic Damage while you're Concentrating on a spell. You're gonna be Concentrating on Magic Weapon pretty often and Protection from Evil and Good into the next Act, so this is just good sense. The only downside is losing +1 to AC and Saving Throws or Bless on heal, which are both way, way more useful. You get this from an Elegant Chest in W'wargaz's room.
* [Ring of Elemental Infusion](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FRing_of_Elemental_Infusion) [bg3.wiki]- Deal an additional 1d4 elemental damage when you deal damage of that element using a spell or cantrip. Booming Blade does Thunder Damage now, so this means 1d4 Thunder on your next attack. With the Diadem of Arcane Synergy, you're looking at anywhere between 4-13 extra damage per turn. That's Sharpshooter/GWM and then some, for free. It *does* add up. Again, is this worth losing TWP and RoP? We didn't think so. You get this by killing Gish Umr'a'ac in the Infirmary.

Act 1.5 Ranged Weapons

* Other than your stat sticks, there's nothing else here.

Act 1.5 Melee Weapons

* [Knife of the Undermountain King](https://steamcommunity.com/linkfilter/?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbg3.wiki%2Fwiki%2FKnife_of_the_Undermountain_King)[bg3.wiki] - -1 to Crit Threshold, Advantage on Obscured Targets and when you roll <2 for damage you reroll the dice and take the highest result. The Advantage is whatever as we get that from Shadow Blade (it's literally the same buff) but the other two things are decent. In your off-hand you'll hit 1's and 2's more often than you realise and crit reduction is always good unless you're going Assassin, which we definitely aren't. You can buy this from the Githyanki merchant in the Creche.

Act 1.5 Shields

* No Shields. On to Act 2!
